**Decision:** A new `wireSelectorOrdering(cfg gardener.Config, metricsDir string) (gardener.Config, gardener.EvolveV2Config)` helper (`cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`) points `cfg.SelectorStatsPath` at `<metricsDir>/selector-stats.json` and returns an `EvolveV2Config` with `SelectorOrdering: true`. `main()` calls it immediately after building `cfg`, and `GardenerRunCycleTool` gains a `v2Cfg` field set once at construction (via the new `newGardenerRunCycleTool(g, v2Cfg)`) instead of calling `gardener.DefaultEvolveV2Config()` fresh inside `Call`, so both the timer-driven daemon cycle and every MCP-triggered `gardener_run_cycle` call now share the same wired config. Alongside this, a pre-existing `Registry.loadAll` bug (`internal/gardener/gardener.go`) that would otherwise have silently defeated the new production test coverage is fixed: a persisted tree file `tree-<name>.json` not already shadowing a builtin was registering under the literal name `"tree-<name>"` instead of the bare `"<name>"` that `SaveTree`/`addBuiltin` otherwise use, so a tree evolved and saved under a short name would, after the *next* process restart, only be resolvable under the doubly-prefixed name — orphaning it from any caller, including the gardener's own re-evolution passes, still looking it up by its original short name. `treeName` is now derived via `strings.TrimSuffix(strings.TrimPrefix(name, "tree-"), ".json")`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes the gardener half of ADR-029's milestone 4–5 production-wiring caveat.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ (amends ADR-029) The learned-ordering pass now runs unattended: every scheduled `bt-gardener` daemon cycle and every MCP-triggered `gardener_run_cycle` call reorders each tree's Selector children from accumulated telemetry before persisting, closing the "automatic loop still operator-triggered" caveat for the gardener call sites specifically. `domains.SelectorStatsPath`/`SelectorStatsPathFn` (the separate build-time tree-resolver seam noted in the same caveat) remains unset in production — a tree resolved fresh at request time is still not reordered; only the gardener's own persisted copies are.
- ✅ A persisted tree saved under a bare name (e.g. `custom_tree`, written as `tree-custom_tree.json`) now reloads under that same bare name after a restart instead of `tree-custom_tree`, so lookups, re-evolution, and the newly wired selector-ordering pass all resolve it consistently across restarts. Pinned by the updated `TestRegistry_PersistedTreeLoading` (`internal/gardener/coverage_test.go`).
- ✅ Pinned end to end by `TestWireSelectorOrdering_EnablesLearnedOrderingPass` and `TestGardenerRunCycleTool_CallAppliesLearnedSelectorOrdering` (`cmd/bt-gardener/main_test.go`), the latter seeding real telemetry, running a full `gardener_run_cycle` tool call, and asserting the persisted tree's Selector children come back in the learned order.
- ⚠️ `selector-stats.json` under `metricsDir` is a new durable file with no explicit cap or rotation, alongside the existing gardener metrics/reference files in the same directory (§7.2.2).

---

## ADR-080: `handleAnalyze` Adopts the `Workflow` Task-Derivation Engine and Stops Swallowing ThinkTank Orchestrator Errors (Q1 Correctness Program, Milestones 1–3/4)

**Context (2026-07-13):** Program "Q1 Correctness — Adopt the dashboard's ThinkTank→Workflow task-derivation engine in production and stop swallowing analysis failures." Two gaps coexisted after ADR-072: (1) `internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go`'s `Workflow`/`WorkflowTask`/`RecommendationsToTasks`/`Prioritize` remained callerless in production — ADR-072 deliberately retrofitted the `Approval`/`WorkflowPriority` *shape* onto the simpler `dashboard.Task`/`TaskStore` instead of migrating `handleAnalyze` onto `Workflow`, flagging that migration as an open consequence. (2) `handleAnalyze` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) called only `orch.RunResearchRound()`, discarding its error (`_ = orch.RunResearchRound()`), and derived tasks straight from `tt.ResearchFindings` — it never ran `RunDebate`/`RunSynthesis`, so `tt.Synthesis` was always nil and `RecommendationsToTasks` (which no-ops on a nil `Synthesis`) could never have produced a task even if called. The MCP counterpart, `bt_thinktank_analyze` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`), had the same discard on `orch.RunFullAnalysis(params.Topic)`, so a failed LLM call returned a 200-shaped response with zero findings indistinguishable from a topic that genuinely produced none. Separately, `Workflow.RunFullPipeline` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go:356`) unconditionally read `w.Tasks[0].Title` after `RecommendationsToTasks`, which panics whenever that call no-ops to zero tasks (nil synthesis or an otherwise-empty recommendation set).

**Decision:** Three milestones. (1) `RunFullPipeline` now guards the `w.Tasks[0]` read behind `len(w.Tasks) > 0`, leaving `Company.SprintGoal` at its prior value when there is nothing to summarize; `workflow_engine_test.go` adds nil-synthesis and zero-recommendation cases. (2) `handleAnalyze` now runs the full three-phase pipeline it needs to populate `Synthesis` — `RunResearchRound` → `RunDebate` → `RunSynthesis`, checking each error and returning early with an `{"error": ...}` JSON body on the first failure — then builds a `dashboard.NewWorkflow(tt.Name, tt, companyState)`, calls `RecommendationsToTasks()` + `Prioritize()`, and maps each resulting `WorkflowTask` onto a `dashboard.Task` (`Priority`/`Assignee`/`Sprint`/`StoryPoints`/`Approval` taken from `WorkflowTask.Priority.String()`/`AssigneeRole`/`SprintTarget`/`EstimatedEffort`/`Approval`) before `taskStore.Create` and the existing `PickTreeForTask` routing (ADR-073) — this is `Workflow`'s first production caller. (3) `bt_thinktank_analyze` now checks `orch.RunFullAnalysis`'s error and returns an explicit `{"error": ...}` tool result instead of proceeding with a half-populated `ThinkTank`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestones 1–3/4. Milestone 4/4 of the stated program is not part of this change. **Milestone 4/4 closed (2026-07-13, ADR-081).**

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A nil-`Synthesis` or zero-recommendation `RunFullPipeline` call no longer panics on an out-of-bounds `w.Tasks[0]` read. Pinned by `TestRunFullPipeline_NilSynthesis_NoPanic` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`).
- ✅ (amends ADR-072's ⚠️ consequence) `workflow_engine.go`'s `RecommendationsToTasks`/`Prioritize` are no longer callerless in production — `handleAnalyze` is a real caller, and because it now also runs `RunDebate`/`RunSynthesis` first, `RecommendationsToTasks` actually has a populated `Synthesis` to convert instead of no-oping. Dashboard tasks derived from thinktank output now carry real `AssigneeRole`/`SprintTarget`/`Approval` state pulled from `WorkflowTask` rather than being minted directly from raw `ResearchFindings` insight text. `ApproveTask`, `ExecuteSprint`, and `RunFullPipeline` itself remain test-only (`RunFullPipeline` gains a production caller in ADR-116). Pinned by `TestHandleAnalyze_UsesWorkflowForTaskDerivation` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`).
- ✅ A failed research round, debate, or synthesis call now surfaces as an explicit `{"error": ...}` response from `handleAnalyze` instead of silently falling through to an empty success body. Pinned by `TestHandleAnalyze_SurfacesOrchestratorError` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`).
- ✅ `bt_thinktank_analyze` now returns an explicit `error` field when `RunFullAnalysis` fails, instead of a 200-shaped tool result reporting zero fellows/findings/scenarios as if the topic genuinely produced none. Pinned by `TestBTThinkTankAnalyzeSurfacesOrchestratorError` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).
- ⚠️ `dashboard.Task.ID` is now built as `wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID`, and `NewWorkflow`'s `wf.ID` is `fmt.Sprintf("wf-%d", time.Now().Unix())` — second, not nanosecond, granularity — while `WorkflowTask.ID` values are fixed per-category strings (`rec-001`, `agree-001`, …). Two `handleAnalyze` calls landing in the same wall-clock second reuse the same task IDs, reintroducing the collision class ADR-072 fixed on the old `tt-<nanos>-<idx>` scheme, this time one level up in the ID construction `handleAnalyze` itself now owns. **Resolved by ADR-082 (2026-07-13):** `WorkflowTask.ID` now carries a random per-call nonce, so `wt.ID` — and therefore the composed `dashboard.Task.ID` — is unique even when `wf.ID` collides at second granularity.
- ⚠️ `RunFullPipeline` still discards its own `ttOrch` phase errors internally (`_ = ttOrch.RunResearchRound()` etc.) — this program's error-surfacing fix applies to `handleAnalyze`/`bt_thinktank_analyze`'s direct orchestrator calls, not to `RunFullPipeline`'s, which remains uncalled in production regardless. **Resolved by ADR-116 (2026-07-15):** `RunFullPipeline` now checks all five phase errors and halts on the first failure, and gained its first production caller.

---

## ADR-081: Workflow-Level Approval Endpoints Wired Over HTTP and `RunFullPipeline`'s Auto-Approval Removed (Q1 Correctness Program, Milestone 4/4 — Closes the Program)

**Context (2026-07-13):** ADR-080 closed milestones 1–3 of "Q1 Correctness — Adopt the dashboard's ThinkTank→Workflow task-derivation engine in production and stop swallowing analysis failures," explicitly leaving milestone 4/4 open: `internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go`'s `Workflow.PendingApprovals`/`ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` were fully unit-tested but had zero callers outside `workflow_engine_test.go` — `handleAnalyze` built a `*dashboard.Workflow` purely as a local variable to derive `WorkflowTask`s, copied them into `dashboard.Task` values, and let the `Workflow` itself fall out of scope, so the approval gate its own methods enforced could never be exercised outside a test. Separately, `Workflow.RunFullPipeline` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go:346-351`) auto-approved every task with `Priority <= PriorityHigh` as `"system"` immediately after deriving it, before `ExecuteSprint` ran — a bypass of the same approval gate the program was trying to make reachable, silently self-approving the majority of realistic workloads (critical/high priority tasks are the common case) the moment `RunFullPipeline` ran.

**Decision:** Two changes. (1) `handleAnalyze` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) now assigns the `*dashboard.Workflow` it builds to a new package-level `currentWorkflow` var (mirroring the existing `taskStore`/`companyState` package vars) instead of letting it fall out of scope. Three new handlers, registered alongside the existing `/api/tasks/approve`/`/api/tasks/reject` routes — `handleWorkflowPending` (`GET /api/workflow/pending`), `handleWorkflowApprove` (`POST /api/workflow/approve?id=`), `handleWorkflowReject` (`POST /api/workflow/reject?id=&reason=`) — call `currentWorkflow.PendingApprovals()`/`ApproveTask(id, "dashboard")`/`RejectTask(id, "dashboard", reason)` directly, returning 404 with an `{"error": ...}` body when there is no active workflow or the task ID does not resolve. (2) `RunFullPipeline`'s auto-approval loop is deleted outright; the method now goes straight from `Prioritize()` into `ExecuteSprint`, so every `WorkflowTask` — regardless of priority — stays `StatusPending` until an explicit `ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` call, and `ExecuteSprint` (which only ever advances `StatusApproved` tasks) advances nothing on its own.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes milestone 4/4; the "Q1 Correctness — Adopt the dashboard's ThinkTank→Workflow task-derivation engine" program is now fully landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `Workflow.PendingApprovals`/`ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` are no longer test-only — the three new HTTP handlers are real production callers, pinned by `TestHandleWorkflowApprovalEndpoints` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`).
- ✅ `RunFullPipeline` can no longer self-approve a task: `TestRunFullPipeline` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`) now asserts every task's `Approval.IsApproved` is `false` and its `Status` stays `StatusPending` after a full pipeline run with no explicit decision, and that `PendingApprovals()` still lists all of them.
- ⚠️ The new `/api/workflow/*` endpoints operate on `currentWorkflow.Tasks`, a separate in-memory copy of the tasks `handleAnalyze` also writes into `taskStore` as `dashboard.Task` values (ADR-080) under a different ID scheme (`wt.ID` vs. `wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID`). Approving or rejecting through `/api/workflow/approve`/`/api/workflow/reject` does not change the corresponding `taskStore` entry, and `handleSprintExecute`'s dispatch loop reads only `TaskStore.Approved()` (ADR-072) — so the workflow-level gate this milestone set out to make reachable is now reachable over HTTP, but not yet wired to the approval path that actually gates sprint dispatch. Closing that requires either dropping one of the two parallel `Task`/`WorkflowTask` representations or reconciling decisions made through either endpoint pair.
- ⚠️ `currentWorkflow` is a single unscoped package var overwritten by every `handleAnalyze` call. A second analysis run silently discards any pending approvals left over from a prior `Workflow`, and concurrent dashboard sessions share one workflow rather than one each. **Partially addressed by ADR-082 (2026-07-13):** the pointer read/write and every `Workflow` method's access to `Tasks` are now mutex-guarded, so concurrent HTTP handlers no longer data-race on this var or the slice it points at — but the single-workflow, last-write-wins overwrite semantics described above are unchanged; a second `handleAnalyze` call still discards a prior workflow's pending approvals, just safely rather than racily.
- ⚠️ `RunFullPipeline` itself remains uncalled in production (per ADR-080's residual consequence); this milestone's auto-approval removal is verified by its unit tests and takes effect the moment a production caller is wired, but has no live behavioral effect today. **Resolved by ADR-116 (2026-07-15):** now that `RunFullPipeline` has a production caller, this auto-approval removal takes live effect — every task it derives stays `StatusPending` until an explicit approval.

---

## ADR-082: `Workflow`-Derived Task IDs Made Collision-Resistant and `currentWorkflow`/`Workflow.Tasks` Made Concurrency-Safe

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal. ADR-080/081's residual caveats named two related gaps left by wiring `handleAnalyze` onto the `Workflow` task-derivation engine and exposing it over HTTP. First, `RecommendationsToTasks` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go`) minted `WorkflowTask.ID` as a fixed per-category string (`rec-001`, `agree-001`, …), and `NewWorkflow`'s `wf.ID` only carries second, not nanosecond, granularity (`fmt.Sprintf("wf-%d", time.Now().Unix())`); two `handleAnalyze` calls landing in the same wall-clock second produced identical composed `dashboard.Task.ID` (`wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID`) values, reintroducing the collision class ADR-072 had already fixed once on the dashboard's older ID scheme. Second, the package-level `currentWorkflow *dashboard.Workflow` var ADR-081 introduced was read and reassigned by `handleAnalyze`/`handleWorkflowPending`/`handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` from separate HTTP goroutines with no synchronization at all, and every `Workflow` method (`RecommendationsToTasks`, `ApproveTask`, `RejectTask`, `Prioritize`, `GetApprovedTasks`, `GetTasksByRole`, `GetTasksBySprint`, `PendingApprovals`, `ExecuteSprint`) read or mutated the shared `Tasks` slice with no lock — a genuine Go data race under concurrent requests, not just the already-documented last-write-wins overwrite semantics.

**Decision:** Two independent fixes in the same two files. (1) `RecommendationsToTasks` now mints one `newTaskNonce()` — 4 random bytes from `crypto/rand`, hex-encoded, falling back to `time.Now().UnixNano()` only if `rand.Read` errors — per call, and appends it to every `WorkflowTask.ID` it constructs (`rec-001-<nonce>`, `agree-%03d-<nonce>`, `disagree-%03d-<nonce>`, `dissent-%03d-<nonce>`), so IDs from separate `RecommendationsToTasks` calls can never collide regardless of `wf.ID`'s second-granularity timestamp. (2) `Workflow` gains an internal `mu sync.Mutex`; every exported method now takes `w.mu.Lock()`/`defer w.mu.Unlock()` before touching `Tasks`, and `GetTasksBySprint`'s body is extracted into an unexported `getTasksBySprintLocked` so `ExecuteSprint` — which already holds `w.mu` for its own duration — can reuse it without deadlocking on the non-reentrant mutex. In `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`, a new package-level `currentWorkflowMu sync.RWMutex` guards every read and write of the `currentWorkflow` pointer itself: `handleAnalyze` takes the write lock to reassign it, and `handleWorkflowPending`/`handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` take the read lock to snapshot the pointer into a local `wf` before calling any of its (separately, internally locked) methods.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ (amends ADR-080) Two `handleAnalyze` calls in the same wall-clock second no longer produce colliding `dashboard.Task.ID`/`WorkflowTask.ID` values. Pinned by `TestHandleAnalyze_TaskIDsUniqueAcrossAnalyses` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), which forces a second `Workflow`'s `wf.ID` to collide with the first and asserts the composed task IDs still don't, and the updated `TestRecommendationsToTasks_IDs` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`), which now checks per-category ID prefixes rather than exact IDs.
- ✅ (amends ADR-081) `currentWorkflow` and `Workflow.Tasks` are now guarded by `currentWorkflowMu`/`Workflow.mu` respectively. Pinned by `TestCurrentWorkflow_GuardedByMutex` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`) and `TestWorkflow_ApproveTask_SynchronizesOnMutex` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`), both of which hold the relevant mutex from an external goroutine and assert the guarded call blocks until it is released.
- ⚠️ Neither fix touches the last-write-wins semantics ADR-081 already flagged: a second `handleAnalyze` call still replaces `currentWorkflow` outright, discarding a prior workflow's pending approvals — now race-free, not multi-tenant.
- ⚠️ The nonce is per-`RecommendationsToTasks`-call, not per-`WorkflowTask` — every task minted by one call shares the same nonce, so uniqueness holds across calls but the four fixed category prefixes (`rec-001`, `agree-%03d`, …) remain the within-call disambiguator exactly as before.

## ADR-083: `persistEvolvedWinner`'s Knowledge-Graph Bookkeeping and Tree-Store Write Made Atomic

**Context (2026-07-13):** NotebookLM research goal. ADR-078 gated `persistEvolvedWinner`'s overwrite of a stored `<base>-evolved` winner on a real fitness comparison, but its own consequences flagged that the fix inverted rather than closed the underlying KG/file divergence risk ADR-042 first noted: `RegisterEvolved`'s `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` bookkeeping bump ran *before* `persistGeneratedTree` was even attempted, so a subsequent `engine.ValidateTreeFull` failure or a `nil` `deps.treeStore` could still leave the knowledge graph claiming a fitness/node-count for `evolvedID` with no tree-store file backing it until the next successful run overwrote it.

**Decision:** Split the "does this improve" check from the "commit the bookkeeping" write, and order the write to only follow a confirmed successful persist. A new non-mutating `KnowledgeGraph.EvolvedFitnessImproves(evolvedID, fitness)` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) takes only `kg.mu.RLock()` and reports whether `fitness` would beat `evolvedID`'s currently-stored `StructuralFitness` (an unregistered `evolvedID` always "improves," matching `RegisterEvolved`'s own first-registration behavior). `persistEvolvedWinner` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) now calls this peek first and returns early (`persisted: false`, `skip_reason: "fitness does not improve on stored evolved winner"`) without ever attempting `persistGeneratedTree` when it is false — identical externally-observable early-exit behavior to before. When the peek passes, `persistGeneratedTree` runs as before; only if `result["persisted"]` comes back `true` does `persistEvolvedWinner` then call `RegisterEvolved` to actually commit the `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` bump and connect the `evolved_from` edge. `RegisterEvolved`'s own signature and internal fitness re-check are unchanged, so a concurrent writer that improved on the same `evolvedID` between the peek and the commit still can't be clobbered by a stale bookkeeping write.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — closes ADR-078's residual consequence.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ (amends ADR-078/ADR-042) A validation failure or `nil` `deps.treeStore` can no longer leave the knowledge graph's `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` bumped to a winner that has no backing tree-store file — the bookkeeping commit and the disk write now either both happen or neither does. Pinned by `TestPersistEvolvedWinner_AtomicWithFailedDiskWrite` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), which persists a strong winner, then a second winner with a higher fitness but an invalid node type, and asserts the KG entry still reflects the first winner's fitness/node-count/`EvolvedCount` rather than the failed second one's.
- ✅ The `evolved_from` edge's "connects regardless of fitness outcome" semantics (ADR-078) are preserved unchanged — only the `NodeCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`StructuralFitness` bump moved, not the edge connection, since `RegisterEvolved` itself is unchanged and still connects the edge on every call it makes.
- ⚠️ The two-step peek-then-commit is not a single atomic transaction across `deps.kg` and `deps.treeStore` — a fitness-improving winner from a concurrent call could in principle land its `RegisterEvolved` commit between this call's peek and its own `persistGeneratedTree` write, in which case this call's later `RegisterEvolved` still re-checks fitness internally and would correctly no-op rather than regress the winner, but the tree-store file itself would still be overwritten by this call's (non-improving, relative to the concurrent winner) write. No mutex spans both stores; this matches every other `persistGeneratedTree` caller's existing concurrency assumptions rather than introducing a new gap.

---

## ADR-084: `MarkSuccessful` and `EscalateToDeepSeek` Make the `OutcomeSelector`'s Self-Correction and Escalation Paths Real (Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability Program, Milestones 1–2/4)

**Context (2026-07-13):** The universal `PreGate→StrategyRouter→OutcomeSelector` pattern (§8.1) routes every task's outcome through a three-child `Selector`: `MarkSuccessful` → `SelfCorrect` (retried up to 3x) → `EscalateToDeepSeek`. `MarkSuccessful` (`internal/engine/registry.go`) unconditionally returned `1`, so the Selector's first child never failed — `SelfCorrect` and `EscalateToDeepSeek` were unreachable from any tree built on this pattern regardless of output quality. Compounding this, `EscalateToDeepSeek` was itself a no-op stub (`func(_ *btcore.BTContext[Blackboard]) int { return 1 }`) that made no LLM call and wrote no result, so even a tree that reached it would falsely report success.

**Decision:** `MarkSuccessful` now gates its `return 1` behind `validateOutputQuality(ctx.Blackboard)` — the same quality check already registered as the `ValidateOutput` condition (`internal/engine/conditions_core.go`) and used elsewhere for post-hoc scoring (§8.7) — returning `-1` when the blackboard's result is empty, too short, or otherwise fails the check, so the parent `Selector` actually falls through. `EscalateToDeepSeek` is rewritten to mirror the adjacent `SelfCorrect` action: it invokes `bb.LLM.Generate` with an escalation-framed prompt (task, plan-so-far, and previous output), writes the result to `bb.Result` and sets `bb.Outcome` to success only when the call succeeds, and returns `-1` on a `nil` LLM or a `Generate` error instead of always claiming success.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-13) — milestones 1–2 of a 4-milestone program; milestones 3–4 (broader escalation-path coverage/telemetry) are not part of this change.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Garbage or truncated output (e.g., a `max_tokens`-starved chain) at the end of the primary strategy path no longer gets marked successful outright — it now retries via `SelfCorrect` and, if that also fails, actually escalates to an external LLM instead of silently succeeding. Pinned by new `internal/engine/registry_test.go` cases: `MarkSuccessful` returns `-1` for short/garbage blackboard output and `1` for valid output; `EscalateToDeepSeek` covers both the successful-escalation path (asserting `bb.Result` is written and `bb.Outcome` is success) and the no-LLM-available path (asserting `-1`).
- ✅ No tree topology changes — every tree already built with the `outcome()`/`NewDefaultOutcomeSelector` helper (`internal/domains/trees.go`, `internal/evolution/tree_builders.go`) gets the corrected routing automatically, since both actions are registered once in `registry.go`'s global `init()`.
- ⚠️ `EscalateToDeepSeek`'s name is now slightly misleading: like `SelfCorrect`, it calls whatever `bb.LLM` is configured (fallback chain included), not specifically a DeepSeek endpoint — this matches `SelfCorrect`'s existing behavior and the pre-existing naming convention, not a new gap introduced here.

---

## ADR-085: `RunTask`'s Terminal Backstop Flips `bb.Outcome` on Failed Quality Validation, Closing the OutcomeSelector Program (Milestone 4/4)

**Context (2026-07-14):** ADR-084 closed milestones 1–2 of "Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability — Make the OutcomeSelector's self-correction and escalation paths real" by gating `MarkSuccessful` on `validateOutputQuality` and giving `EscalateToDeepSeek` a real LLM call. But that gate — and the `OutcomeSelector` itself — only fires for trees built through the `outcome()`/`NewDefaultOutcomeSelector` helper. `RunTask` (`internal/engine/tree.go`) already called `validateOutputQuality(bb)` unconditionally at the end of every run as a stated defense-in-depth check, but discarded its boolean result — so a tree that terminates without ever routing through `outcome()` (e.g. a compiled GOAP fusion tree, whose terminal leaf just returns a bare success/failure code) could report `bb.Outcome = "success"` with garbage or truncated output and nothing downstream would ever catch it.

**Decision:** `RunTask` now acts on `validateOutputQuality`'s result instead of discarding it. After computing `qualityOK := validateOutputQuality(bb)`, if the run is not `bb.Sandbox`, `qualityOK` is false, `bb.Outcome` is currently `Success`, the resolved result (`resolvedResult`, a helper factored out of `validateOutputQuality` so both sites judge the same string) is non-empty, and the result is not one of `isStructuredOutput`'s recognized zero-LLM/structured markers (the same `"## "`/`route:`/`status:`/`delivered` heuristics `validateOutputQuality` already used, now extracted into a shared `isStructuredOutput` helper) — `bb.Outcome` flips to `Failure`. The `bb.Sandbox` exemption preserves gardener structural-evaluation runs, whose action stubs intentionally write short `"[sandbox] <name>"` placeholders with nothing genuine to judge; the non-empty-result exemption preserves a deliberate no-op success marker (`AlwaysSucceed`, used by guard/preflight composition) that produces no output to judge either.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14) — closes milestone 4/4 of the Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability OutcomeSelector program (ADR-084 closed milestones 1–2).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Trees that terminate without ever reaching `outcome()`'s `MarkSuccessful`→`SelfCorrect`→`EscalateToDeepSeek` chain — compiled GOAP fusion trees being the primary example — now get the same garbage/truncated-output defense the `OutcomeSelector` enforces elsewhere, closing the gap ADR-084 left open. Pinned by three new `internal/engine/coverage_test.go` cases: `TestRunTask_TerminalBackstopFlipsOutcome` (a bare Action writing `"bad"` flips to Failure), `TestRunTask_TerminalBackstopSkipsStructuredOutput` (a `"## X"` result stays Success), and `TestRunTask_TerminalBackstopSkipsSandboxMode` (a `Sandbox`-mode run stays Success regardless of output).
- ⚠️ Because the backstop is unconditional across every `RunTask` call (not scoped to GOAP fusion trees specifically), several pre-existing engine test fixtures whose canned mock/action output was shorter than `validateOutputQuality`'s 30-character minimum (e.g. `"action executed"`, `"code path selected"`, `"Hello! How can I help?"`) had never had their `bb.Outcome` checked before and needed lengthening to stay above threshold — `internal/engine/chains_test.go`, `decision_tree_test.go`, `edge_case_coverage_test.go`, and `internal/blackboard/engine_integration_test.go` all updated their fixture strings accordingly, with no behavioral change to the code under test.
- ⚠️ `resolvedResult`/`isStructuredOutput` are now shared between `validateOutputQuality` and the terminal backstop; a future change to either heuristic changes both call sites identically, which is the intended behavior but is worth remembering if one is ever meant to diverge from the other.

---

## ADR-086: `handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` Mirror Their Decision into `taskStore`, Closing the Approval-Surface Split ADR-081 Left Open

**Context (2026-07-14):** ADR-081 gave `Workflow.PendingApprovals`/`ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` their first production callers — `GET /api/workflow/pending`, `POST /api/workflow/approve`, `POST /api/workflow/reject` — but flagged in its own consequences that these endpoints mutate only the in-memory `currentWorkflow.Tasks` copy, keyed on the bare `WorkflowTask.ID` (e.g. `rec-001`). `handleAnalyze` separately persists each derived `WorkflowTask` into `taskStore` as a `dashboard.Task`, keyed on the composed ID `wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID` (ADR-080), and `handleSprintExecute`'s dispatch loop reads exclusively from `TaskStore.Approved()` (ADR-072). The two records never stayed in sync: approving a task through `/api/workflow/approve` left its `taskStore` counterpart stuck at `Status: "pending"` forever, so the approval was reachable over HTTP but had no effect on what a sprint actually dispatched.

**Decision:** `handleWorkflowApprove` and `handleWorkflowReject` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) now also call `taskStore.Approve(wf.ID+"-"+taskID, "dashboard")` / `taskStore.Reject(wf.ID+"-"+taskID, "dashboard", reason)` immediately after the corresponding `Workflow.ApproveTask`/`RejectTask` call succeeds, composing the same `wf.ID + "-" + wt.ID` ID `handleAnalyze` used when it created the record. Both calls' errors are discarded (`_ = ...`), matching the existing handlers' error-handling style for this best-effort mirror — the primary HTTP response still reflects the `Workflow`-side outcome.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14) — closes the residual gap ADR-081 flagged.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Approving or rejecting a task through `/api/workflow/approve`/`/api/workflow/reject` now updates the same `taskStore` record `handleSprintExecute` dispatches from — the workflow-level approval gate is not just reachable over HTTP (ADR-081) but actually governs sprint dispatch. Pinned by `TestHandleWorkflowApproveReject_UpdatesTaskStore` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), which seeds a `taskStore` record under the composed ID, approves/rejects through the workflow endpoints, and asserts both the `taskStore` record's `Status`/`Approval.IsApproved` and its presence in `taskStore.Approved()`.
- ⚠️ If `taskStore` has no record under the composed ID — e.g. a task approved through `/api/workflow/approve` that was never routed through `handleAnalyze`'s `taskStore.Create` call — `taskStore.Approve` returns an error that is silently discarded; the `Workflow`-side decision still succeeds and is reflected in `currentWorkflow.Tasks`, but nothing lands in `taskStore`. This matches `handleAnalyze`'s own always-paired construction (every `WorkflowTask` it derives gets both a `currentWorkflow.Tasks` entry and a `taskStore.Create` call), so the gap is theoretical for the one production code path that builds both records, not a live risk.
- ⚠️ The two records remain independently owned and separately mutable (`Workflow.Tasks[i]` directly, `dashboard.Task` via `TaskStore`'s own methods) — this fix makes the one round-trip `handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` need reliable, not a general reconciliation between the two representations. A task approved directly through `/api/tasks/approve` (ADR-072) still does not update `currentWorkflow.Tasks`, though that direction was never the one gating sprint dispatch.

---

## ADR-087: `handleAgentExecute` Carries `RunResult.Quality` into `AgentResult.QualityScore` for Remote-Routed Runs

**Context (2026-07-14):** `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`'s in-process `newLocalAgentExecutor` (the `LocalExecutor` adapter ADR-011 introduced for the horizontal-scaling substrate) has always set `QualityScore: res.Quality` straight from `agent.RunOnce`'s result. The dashboard's HTTP counterpart, `handleAgentExecute` (`POST /api/agents/execute` — the endpoint `reliability.RemoteExecutor.Execute` calls into, per ADR-011/ADR-075, whenever an `AgentRouter` routes a task to a remote dashboard node), built its `reliability.AgentResult` from `AgentExecutor.RunTask`'s `(output, outcome, err)` triple, which drops the `agent.RunResult.Quality` value the richer `RunTaskResult` method (which `RunTask` itself wraps) had already computed. Every remote-routed successful run therefore reported `QualityScore: 0.0` regardless of its actual quality — a silent blind spot for any fitness/quality tracking downstream of a routed, as opposed to purely local, execution.

**Decision:** `handleAgentExecute` now calls `executor.RunTaskResult(req.Agent, req.Task, treeID)` (already implemented in `internal/dashboard/executor.go`) instead of `RunTask`, and a new `agentExecuteResult` helper builds the `reliability.AgentResult` from the returned `*agent.RunResult` — copying `Output`, `Quality` into `QualityScore`, and deriving `Success` from `Outcome` exactly as the old inline code did — collapsing the two duplicated inline blocks (worker-pool path and synchronous-fallback path) into one shared function in the process.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Remote-routed agent runs dispatched through `/api/agents/execute` now report a real `QualityScore`, matching the in-process `LocalExecutor` path's behavior — closing a gap that would otherwise widen as more nodes join the `AgentRouter` registry (ADR-011/ADR-075). Pinned by `TestHandleAgentExecute_SetsQualityScoreFromRunResult` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), which forces the synchronous fallback path with a bare `AlwaysSucceed` tree and asserts `QualityScore > 0`.
- ✅ The worker-pool and synchronous-fallback branches of `handleAgentExecute` no longer duplicate the `AgentResult`-construction logic — both now call the same `agentExecuteResult` helper.
- ⚠️ `TraceID`/`SpanID` still do not round-trip through `reliability.AgentResult` (ADR-075's pre-existing caveat) — this fix only addresses `QualityScore`, not the tracing-correlation gap.

## ADR-088: `evolution.MetaValidator` Wired into the Gardener's Live Mutation-Acceptance Loop

**Context (2026-07-14):** `evolution.MetaValidator` (`internal/evolution/meta_validator.go`) combines structural validation (root type, node names, size/depth limits, selector shape, retry bounds), `ExpertKnowledge` anti-pattern checks, archetype fit, and fitness-floor/regression gates into one explainable `MetaAccept`/`MetaWarn`/`MetaReject` decision — a P0 safety layer distinct from and stricter than composite-score-only acceptance. It had existed fully implemented and unit-tested since its own introduction but had zero production callers: `Gardener.evolveTreeV2` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`) accepted a candidate mutation once `QuickValidate`'s benchmark score, the pre-score regression check, and `QualityGate.ValidateFor` all passed, so a candidate that scored well on those composite metrics alone — e.g. one that emptied out a Selector's children, or introduced an unbounded retry loop, or matched a known expert anti-pattern — could still be committed to the live tree.

**Decision:** `gardener.Config` gains an optional `MetaValidator *evolution.MetaValidator` field. `evolveTreeV2`'s per-candidate acceptance loop now calls `MetaValidator.ValidateMutation(tree, candidateTree, currentFitness.Composite, candidateFitness.Composite)` — `tree` as the pre-mutation baseline, the mutated clone as candidate — immediately after `QualityGate.ValidateFor` accepts a candidate and before `ApplyMutations` commits it to the live tree; a `MetaReject` decision rejects the candidate (counted the same as any other rejection) regardless of its fitness score. A `nil` `MetaValidator` (the default for any caller that doesn't set it) preserves the historical fitness-only acceptance behavior exactly. `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`'s `buildGardenerConfig` sets `MetaValidator: evolution.NewMetaValidator(evolution.MetaValidatorConfig{})` — the validator's production-safe zero-value defaults (`MinScore` 0.70, `WarnScore` 0.85, `MinComposite` 0.30, `MaxRegression` 0.20, `MaxNodes` 120, `MaxDepth` 8) — for the daemon's real evolution cycles.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ The gardener's 24/7 evolution cycle now has a second, structurally-focused acceptance gate independent of composite fitness — a mutation cannot buy its way past a broken-selector or unbounded-retry rejection just by scoring well on fitness/quality/SLO metrics. Pinned by `TestEvolveTreeV2_MetaValidatorRejectsStructurallyBrokenMutation` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`), whose two subtests show the same fixture candidate accepted with `MetaValidator: nil` and rejected once a strict (`MinScore: 1.0`) validator is wired in.
- ✅ Callers outside `cmd/bt-gardener` (tests, any future binary constructing `gardener.Config` directly) are unaffected unless they opt in by setting the field — no behavior change for existing callers that leave it nil.
- ⚠️ `MetaValidator` is consulted only inside `evolveTreeV2`'s gardener cycle; the daemon/MCP-tool `bt_evolve_*` genetic-algorithm paths (`Population.Evolve`, `EvolveWithExperience`, etc., §8.5) have no equivalent structural gate — a follow-up would need its own wiring into `newProductionPopulation` or the tools that construct populations directly.

## ADR-089: `handleSprintExecute` Reconciles `currentWorkflow`'s Task State with `taskStore` as Tasks Actually Execute

**Context (2026-07-14):** The dashboard carries two independent representations of the same sprint task: `dashboard.Task` records in the package-level `taskStore`, and `dashboard.WorkflowTask` records inside the package-level `currentWorkflow.Tasks`, keyed by a composed `wf.ID+"-"+wt.ID` / bare `wt.ID` respectively (§6.3). ADR-086 reconciled one direction — an `/api/workflow/approve`/`/api/workflow/reject` decision now also updates the matching `taskStore` record. The opposite direction stayed open: `handleSprintExecute`'s per-task dispatch loop (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) called `taskStore.UpdateStatus`/`taskStore.SetOutput` as each dispatched task started, timed out, failed, or completed, but never touched `currentWorkflow` at all. Any dashboard surface reading `currentWorkflow` directly — `GET /api/workflow/pending`, the sprint-goal UI, `Workflow.Company.CurrentSprint` — stayed frozen showing a task as merely `"approved"` even after a sprint had actually run and finished it.

**Decision:** `internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go` gains `Workflow.SetTaskStatus(taskID, status)`, mirroring `ExecuteSprint`'s own convention: it updates the matching `WorkflowTask.Status` under the existing `w.mu` lock and, when the new status is `StatusCompleted`, stamps `CompletedAt` and advances `Company.CurrentSprint` to the task's `SprintTarget` if higher. `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go` gains a `syncWorkflowTaskStatus(taskID, status)` helper that reads `currentWorkflow` under `currentWorkflowMu`, checks the dispatched `taskID` against the `wf.ID+"-"` prefix (the same composed-ID convention `handleAnalyze`/`handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` already use), and — only on a matching prefix — calls `SetTaskStatus` with the ID's `wt.ID` suffix. `handleSprintExecute`'s dispatch loop now calls `syncWorkflowTaskStatus` at the same three points it already calls `taskStore.UpdateStatus`: `StatusInProgress` when a task starts, `StatusBlocked` on timeout or failure, `StatusCompleted` on success.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `currentWorkflow` now advances alongside `taskStore` as a sprint actually executes, not just as tasks are approved/rejected — closing the reverse direction ADR-086 left open. Pinned by `TestHandleSprintExecute_UpdatesCurrentWorkflow` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), which seeds a `currentWorkflow` task and its composed-ID `taskStore` counterpart, runs a real sprint dispatch against a stubbed always-succeeding tree, and asserts both `currentWorkflow.Tasks[0].Status == StatusCompleted` and `Company.CurrentSprint` advanced to the task's `SprintTarget`.
- ✅ A task dispatched by `handleSprintExecute` whose composed ID does not carry the active `currentWorkflow.ID+"-"` prefix (e.g. a manually-created `dashboard.Task` with no `Workflow` origin) is a no-op for `syncWorkflowTaskStatus` — sprint dispatch for non-workflow-derived tasks is unaffected.
- ⚠️ This closes the execution-outcome direction only; the two records remain structurally independent (separate structs, separate stores) rather than unified into one source of truth — a future change to either's shape still risks the sync drifting again.

## ADR-090: A2A Agent-Card Signing Wired into the Card-Serving and Card-Consuming Paths

**Context (2026-07-14):** `SignAgentCard`/`VerifyAgentCard` (`internal/a2a/signing.go`) — a SHA-256 content hash over the marshaled card, not a keyed or asymmetric signature — had existed since the ADR-008 auction work with tests but no production caller on either side: nothing signed a card when it was built or served, and nothing checked a signature before trusting a card as an auction candidate.

**Decision:** Sign on both card-producing paths: `ConvertToAgentCard` (`internal/a2a/card.go`), the single origin point every registered-agent card passes through (`BuildCardRegistry` and the server's per-bid card build), appends a `SignAgentCard` result to `card.Signatures` before returning. `handleGlobalAgentCard` (`internal/a2a/server.go`), which assembles the `/.well-known/agent-card.json` response ad hoc from `CardCache` rather than via `ConvertToAgentCard`, signs it separately just before writing the JSON response (best-effort: a signing error leaves the card unsigned rather than failing the request). Check on the one card-consuming trust boundary: `auctionCandidates` (`internal/a2a/auction.go`) — which turns the live card registry into `AuctionDelegate`'s dispatchable candidate set — now filters the registry through a new `cardSignatureValid(card)` (`internal/a2a/signing.go`) before calling `EligibleBidders`. `cardSignatureValid` trusts a card with no `Signatures` at all (signing is opt-in, so unsigned is the expected state for cards predating this feature or built outside `ConvertToAgentCard`) but rejects a card whose trailing signature no longer verifies against the rest of its content — re-verified by re-hashing the card with that trailing signature stripped off, since the hash covers the whole card including prior signatures.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A card that is tampered or forged after being signed is no longer indistinguishable from a legitimate one at the one point production actually trusts card content to pick auction candidates. Pinned by `TestConvertToAgentCard_AttachesSignature`, `TestHandleGlobalAgentCard_ResponseIsSigned`, and `TestAuctionCandidates_RejectsTamperedCardSignature` (`internal/a2a/signing_test.go`), the last of which asserts a tampered card is excluded, a validly-signed card is included, and an unsigned card is still trusted.
- ⚠️ The signature is an unkeyed SHA-256 hash: it detects accidental corruption or staleness (a cached card edited after signing) but does not authenticate origin — an adversary who can forge a card can also compute a valid hash over their own forged content. This closes an integrity-of-cache gap, not an authentication gap; `EligibleBidders`/`ScoreAnnouncement` still have no notion of *who* signed a card, only *whether* it was altered since signing.
- ⚠️ Only `auctionCandidates` checks signatures before use; other card readers in `internal/a2a` (e.g. direct `CardCache` lookups outside the auction path) do not yet consult `cardSignatureValid`.

---

## ADR-091: Durable Cross-Run Archives for the Pareto Front and NSGA-II's Final Front (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/5)

**Context (2026-07-14):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Give Pareto-front and NSGA-II evolution the same durable cross-run archive the other four algorithms already have." ADR-033/ADR-043 gave `IslandModel` and `MAPElitesGrid` a durable, merge-safe, cap-bounded archive, and ADR-041 did the same for `QTable` — but `ParetoFront` and `NSGAIIPopulation` were the two remaining evolution algorithms with no analogous state: ADR-057 wired `bt_evolve_pareto` into production, but its `ParetoFront` was rebuilt from scratch and discarded with every response, and `NSGAIIPopulation` (driven by `bt_evolve_multiobjective`) had never had a persistence primitive at all.

**Decision:** Give `ParetoFront` the same persistence idiom as `MAPElitesGrid` and `IslandModel`, adapted for a front with no niche key. `ParetoFront.Save`/`Load` (`internal/evolution/pareto.go`) persist `Individuals` as JSON — atomic tmp+rename under the ADR-024 `acquireExperienceLock` flock — bounded by a new optional `Cap` field (0 = unbounded): `cappedIndividuals` evicts the lowest composite-`Fitness` individuals first, tie-broken by `Genome` for determinism, applied on both `Save` and post-merge `Load`. Because a Pareto front has no niche key to merge by, `Load` instead merges disk individuals into memory through the front's own dominance-based `Add`: a disk individual is dropped if anything already in memory dominates it, and it evicts whatever it dominates in turn — so a fitter in-memory copy always survives a dominated disk one. A missing archive is a silent cold start; a corrupt one is an error that leaves memory untouched. `bt_evolve_pareto` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) wires this end to end via a new `paretoFrontArchivePath(treeID)` (mirroring `mapElitesArchivePath`, sanitized per ADR-034): a `ParetoFront` kept separate from `pp.Front` (which `EvolvePareto`'s internal `Evaluate` rebuilds every generation) is capped at `population*5`, loaded before evolution, merged against the evolved population via `AddFromPopulation` after, and saved back — reporting `warm_started` (via `os.Stat` before the load attempt) plus non-fatal `archive_load_error`/`archive_save_error`, exactly as `bt_evolve_island`/`bt_evolve_qd` already do. The tool's `front_size`/`diversity_score`/`best_per_dim` now come from `archive.Stats()` — the durable, merged front — rather than the single call's `pp.Front.Stats()`. `NSGAIIPopulation` gains the equivalent `Cap`/`Archive` fields and `Save`/`Load` methods (`internal/evolution/multi_objective.go`): `frontZeroIndividuals` wraps `Fronts[0]` — the final non-dominated front, mutually non-dominated by construction like `ParetoFront.Individuals` — as `MultiIndividual`s, and both methods build a `ParetoFront` around them and delegate to its `Save`/`Load` rather than reimplementing the merge/cap logic. `bt_evolve_multiobjective` does not call `NSGAIIPopulation.Save`/`Load` yet, so this half of the program ships as a tested-but-unwired primitive — the same state `EvolvePareto` itself was in before ADR-057.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-14) — milestones 1–3 of 5.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `bt_evolve_pareto`'s Pareto-optimal individuals now accumulate across runs instead of resetting every call, closing the Pareto-front analogue of the gap ADR-033 left open and ADR-043 later closed for MAP-Elites.
- ✅ Reuses the ADR-024 flock and the ADR-034 sanitization helper rather than inventing new persistence or bounding semantics; `NSGAIIPopulation.Save`/`Load` reuse `ParetoFront.Save`/`Load` directly rather than duplicating the merge/cap logic a third time.
- ✅ Bounded from the start: like ADR-043's MAP-Elites wiring, `bt_evolve_pareto`'s archive ships capped (`population*5`) in its first landing rather than shipping unbounded and closing the gap later (contrast the QTable, ADR-041).
- ⚠️ `NSGAIIPopulation.Save`/`Load` have no `bt_evolve_multiobjective` caller — milestones 4–5 of this program (wiring the tool and reconciling `bt_evolve_pareto`/`bt_evolve_multiobjective`'s already-divergent response shapes, per ADR-057) remain open.
- ⚠️ `bt_evolve_pareto`'s reported `front_size`/`diversity_score`/`best_per_dim` changed meaning with this ADR: they now describe the durable archive across all runs on this base tree, not just the individuals this call's `EvolvePareto` produced — a caller diffing consecutive responses to gauge one run's contribution needs to account for the merge.
- Pinned by `TestParetoFront_Load_MissingFileColdStart`, `TestParetoFront_Save_EvictsLowestFitnessFirst`, and `TestParetoFront_Load_MergeKeepsFitterCopy` (`internal/evolution/pareto_test.go`); `TestBTEvolveParetoAccumulatesDurableArchive` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`); `TestNSGAIIPopulation_Load_MissingFileColdStart`, `TestNSGAIIPopulation_Save_EvictsLowestFitnessFirst`, and `TestNSGAIIPopulation_Load_MergeKeepsFitterCopy` (`internal/evolution/multi_objective_test.go`).

## ADR-092: Characterization Tests for the Goal and Blackboard MCP Tools Surface Two Real Bugs

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Deterministic coverage backlog: characterization tests for `cmd/benchcmp/main.go` and 2 more (Q1 Correctness)," milestones 2–3 of 3. `cmd/bt-agent/blackboard_tools.go` and `cmd/bt-agent/goal_tools.go` had no direct test files of their own; the milestone's mandate was to pin currently-observed behavior with new `blackboard_tools_test.go`/`goal_tools_test.go` files, table-driven where natural, and to touch production code only if a test exposed a real bug. Writing the tables exposed two.

**Decision:** Fix both minimally, in place, with no other behavior change.
- **`goalTreeSlug`** (`cmd/bt-agent/goal_tools.go`) compresses a goal name into the tree-ID fragment used by `"goal:" + goalTreeSlug(goal.Name)` (autopilot tree IDs), MCP-tool task IDs, and feedback task IDs. It built the underscore-joined slug by writing a separator whenever a field's *index* was `>0`, not whenever the previous field actually contributed characters — so a field that filtered down to nothing (pure punctuation, e.g. `"watch & alert!"`'s `"&"` field) still triggered a separator, producing a stray/doubled underscore (`"watch__alert"` instead of `"watch_alert"`). The fix accumulates each field into a scratch `strings.Builder` first and only appends it — with a preceding separator if the output so far is non-empty — when that field actually produced at least one character, so an empty field contributes nothing at all instead of an orphan separator.
- **`scopedStore.get`** (`internal/blackboard/memory_store.go`) — see §8.4.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 2 and 3 of 3; milestone 1 (`cmd/benchcmp/main.go`) covered separately.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Goal names with trailing punctuation or symbol-only words no longer mint tree/task IDs with stray double underscores, which previously risked two distinct goal names slugging to visually-confusable-but-distinct IDs and made generated IDs harder to read.
- ✅ `bt_bb_read` now agrees with `bt_bb_write`/`bt_bb_delete`/`bt_bb_list` on whitespace/slash-padded keys, closing the one asymmetric path in the `scopedStore` key contract.
- Pinned by `TestGoalTreeSlug` (`cmd/bt-agent/goal_tools_test.go`, table-driven, including a `"strips punctuation"` case asserting `"watch & alert!"` → `"watch_alert"`) and `TestBTBBWriteThenReadNormalizesKeyIdentically` (`cmd/bt-agent/blackboard_tools_test.go`); both new test files otherwise characterize the pre-existing behavior of their respective tool files with no further production changes.

## ADR-093: Durable Pre-Mutation Snapshots and Operator-Triggered Tree Rollback for the Gardener

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Wire durable pre-mutation snapshots, tree rollback, and benchmark-gated winner acceptance into the gardener and evolve tools," milestones 1–3 of 3 (benchmark-gated acceptance itself — `QuickValidate`/`QualityGate.ValidateFor` — already existed and is unchanged by this ADR). `gardener.Config.SnapshotDir` (`internal/gardener/gardener.go:425`) was already populated in production (`cmd/bt-gardener/config.go:79`, `snapDir := filepath.Join(metricsDir, "snapshots")`) and `evolution.SnapshotTree`/`RestoreTree` (`internal/evolution/quality_gate.go:143`/`:167`) already existed, but nothing called either: the pre-mutation tree state lived only in `evolveTreeV2`'s in-memory `originalTree := cloneTreeForGardener(tree)` clone, used solely for that same cycle's own ACCEPT/ROLLBACK comparison (§6.2/§8.5) — a process crash mid-cycle lost the pre-mutation state entirely, and there was no way to recover a bad mutation already committed by a *previous* cycle without hand-editing the tree file.

**Decision:** Wire the existing snapshot/restore primitives into the live gardener path. **Milestone 1:** `evolveTreeV2` calls `evolution.SnapshotTree(tree, entry.Name, g.cfg.SnapshotDir)` immediately before the mutation loop begins (right before `originalTree` is cloned), when `SnapshotDir` is configured; a snapshot failure logs a warning and does not abort the cycle. **Milestone 2:** a new `Registry.RollbackTree(name, snapshotDir string) error` (`internal/gardener/gardener.go`) calls `evolution.RestoreTree(name, snapshotDir)`, swaps the matching registry entry's in-memory `Tree` under `r.mu`, and durably persists it via the existing `SaveTree` — returning an error rather than silently no-op'ing when `name` has no registry entry. **Milestone 3:** a new `GardenerRollbackTool` (`cmd/bt-gardener/main.go`), mirroring the existing `GardenerStatusTool`/`GardenerRunCycleTool` langchain-tool pattern, parses a tree name from its input and calls `Registry.RollbackTree`, registered as `gardener_rollback` alongside the three pre-existing tools (status/run_cycle/recommend) and described in the agent's tool prompt — so recovery is operator- or LLM-triggerable instead of requiring code changes.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1–3 of 3 landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A process crash mid-cycle no longer loses the pre-mutation tree state: it now survives on disk at `~/.go-bt-gardener/snapshots/snapshot_<name>.json`, independent of the in-memory `originalTree` clone the same cycle's own ACCEPT/ROLLBACK logic uses
- ✅ A bad mutation already committed and saved by a prior cycle can be recovered via `gardener_rollback` without rerunning a full evolution cycle
- ⚠️ The snapshot is a single slot per tree, overwritten (tmp+rename) at the start of every cycle — it always holds only the *most recent* cycle's pre-mutation state, not a history; rolling back after N accepted cycles only undoes the most recent one, not all N
- ⚠️ `RollbackTree` restores unconditionally — it does not re-run `QualityGate.ValidateFor`/`MetaValidator.ValidateMutation` against the restored tree, nor does it reset a tree's `QualityGate` fail-streak (`ResetFailCount`/`IsDisabledFor`) if the mutation that made rollback necessary had already disabled the gate for that tree; this caveat carries forward unresolved into the automatic rollback path ADR-115 (2026-07-15) adds
- Pinned by `TestEvolveTreeV2_SnapshotsTreeBeforeMutation`, `TestRegistry_RollbackTree`, `TestRegistry_RollbackTree_UnknownTree` (`internal/gardener`), and `TestGardenerRollbackTool_CallRestoresSnapshot` (`cmd/bt-gardener`)

## ADR-094: Gardener-Embedded Transposition Table and Deterministic Eviction (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Wire the Stockfish transposition-table search into the gardener's live evolution loop." `evaluator.TranspositionTable` (`internal/evaluator/stockfish.go`) already cached `(tree, task)` fitness evaluations, but its only production caller was the standalone `bt-evaluator` MCP binary — the gardener's own `RunCycleV2` never constructed, stored into, or persisted one, so cached evaluations were lost on every gardener restart and re-computed from scratch even when `bt-evaluator` had already scored the same position. Eviction was also non-deterministic: `Store`'s over-capacity path and `load`'s startup trim both deleted whatever entry Go's randomized map iteration visited first.

**Decision:** Thread a `*evaluator.TranspositionTable` through the gardener's own cycle instead of leaving it exclusive to `bt-evaluator`. A new `Config.TranspositionTablePath` backs a lazily-constructed, `Gardener`-cached table (`transpositionTable()`, `internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`); `evolveTreeV2` stores this cycle's evaluation into it right after computing `baseFitness` — ahead of the evidence gate, so every processed tree contributes regardless of downstream short-circuits — and `RunCycleV2` saves the table after every tree, alongside the existing `MetricsTracker.Save()` call. `CycleMetrics` gains `DeepSearchUsed`/`DeepSearchDepth`/`TTHitRate`, populated from `evaluator.IterativeDeepening`'s `DeepeningResult` when a table is configured (metrics-only — the deep-search result does not yet bias mutation selection). Eviction in `TranspositionTable.Store`/`load` is now deterministic: `TranspositionEntry` gains an `InsertedAt` marker stamped from a monotonic per-table sequence counter on every `Store`, and a new `evictLowestValueEntry` helper always removes the least-recently-written entry instead of a random one.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1–3 of 3 landed. ~~`TranspositionTablePath` is not yet set by `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`, so the daemon's production cycles do not populate or persist a table yet — this is analogous to `Config.SelectorStatsPath` before ADR-079 wired it into `buildGardenerConfig`.~~ — resolved by ADR-114 milestone 2 (2026-07-15): `buildGardenerConfig` now sets `TranspositionTablePath`.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Once `TranspositionTablePath` is wired into a production entry point, cached `(tree, task)` evaluations survive gardener restarts instead of resetting with every process cycle, closing the gap between the gardener's own evolution loop and the persistence `bt-evaluator` already had.
- ✅ Cache eviction is now reproducible: given the same sequence of `Store` calls, the same entry is always evicted, which makes eviction behavior testable (`TestTranspositionTable_Store_EvictsLowestValueDeterministically`) where the prior random-map-order deletion was not.
- ⚠️ Eviction picks the least-recently-*written* entry, not the lowest-fitness one — a high-value entry written once early and never overwritten is evicted before a low-value entry rewritten more recently; `Probe` (a read) does not refresh `InsertedAt`, so this is closer to FIFO-by-write than true LRU.
- ⚠️ ~~`IterativeDeepening`'s result is recorded in `CycleMetrics` but not yet consulted by the mutation-acceptance path — deep search runs and reports, but doesn't (yet) change which mutation the cycle applies.~~ — resolved by ADR-107 (2026-07-15): a `BestMutation` that beats the cycle's current fitness is now applied to the live tree.
- ⚠️ ~~No production binary sets `Config.TranspositionTablePath` yet, so milestones 1–2 are reachable only from tests until a follow-up wires it into `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`.~~ — resolved by ADR-114 milestone 2 (2026-07-15).
- Pinned by `TestRunCycleV2_TranspositionTablePersistsAcrossGardenerInstances` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`); `TestEvolveTreeV2_DeepSearchMetrics_PopulatedWhenTTConfigured`, `TestEvolveTreeV2_DeepSearchMetrics_ZeroWithoutTranspositionTable` (`internal/gardener/gardener_test.go`); and `TestTranspositionTable_StoreProbeRoundTrip`, `TestTranspositionTable_StoreProbeMiss`, `TestTranspositionTable_SaveLoadRoundTrip`, `TestTranspositionTable_Store_EvictsLowestValueDeterministically`, `TestTranspositionTable_Store_EvictsLowestValueRepeated`, `TestTranspositionTable_Store_EvictionPreservesLowestAmongSurvivors` (`internal/evaluator/stockfish_test.go`)

## ADR-095: A Durable, Learning Archive for Expert Knowledge (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–2/2)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Give Expert Knowledge a durable, learning archive instead of a static hardcoded rule set." `ExpertKnowledge` (`internal/evolution/expert.go`) had shipped as a fixed, code-defined catalog — 6 design `Patterns`, 5 `AntiPatterns`, `Heuristics`, `TreeArchetypes` — consulted by `MetaValidator` (ADR-088) and surfaced advisory-only through `bt_evolve_expert`'s `RecommendMutations`/`DetectAntiPatterns`. Unlike every other evolution algorithm in the package (`QTable`/ADR-041, `IslandModel`/ADR-033, `MAPElitesGrid`/ADR-033, `ParetoFront`/ADR-091), it had no durable state and could not grow from what real evolution runs actually observed: a mutation `bt_evolve_qlearning` found to genuinely improve fitness left no trace `ExpertKnowledge` could later draw on.

**Decision:** Give `ExpertKnowledge` the same durable-archive idiom the other algorithms use, then thread it through the one production path that already computes a fitness delta per mutation. **Milestone 1:** a new `ExpertKnowledge.LearnedPatterns []LearnedPattern` field (`{Action, Category string; Gain float64}`) accumulates via a new `Observe(action, category string, gain float64)` method — nil-safe (a `nil *ExpertKnowledge` is a no-op) and gain-gated (`gain <= 0` is discarded, so only genuine improvements are retained). `Population.EvolveQLearning` (`internal/evolution/learning.go:846`) and its per-mutation helper `qLearnMutate` (`learning.go:887-924`) both gain an additional `ek *ExpertKnowledge` parameter; `qLearnMutate` calls `ek.Observe(action, category, after-before)` right after computing the same `after-before` delta it already feeds into `qt.Update`, so `ExpertKnowledge` and the `QTable` learn from the identical signal. `ExpertKnowledge.Save`/`Load` (mirroring `QTable.Save`/`Load`) persist `LearnedPatterns` alone (the hardcoded catalog fields are rebuilt fresh by `NewExpertKnowledge` every call and are deliberately not persisted) as JSON via atomic tmp+rename under the ADR-024 `acquireExperienceLock` flock; `Load` appends rather than clobbers, and a missing archive is a silent cold start. **Milestone 2:** a new `expertArchivePath(treeID string) string` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) mirrors `qtableArchivePath`'s per-base-tree scoping and sanitization (ADR-034). `bt_evolve_qlearning` constructs an `ExpertKnowledge`, loads it from this path before calling `EvolveQLearning`, and saves it back after — both non-fatal on error (`expert_archive_load_error`/`expert_archive_save_error`), matching the QTable archive's degrade-to-cold-start behavior in the same handler. `bt_evolve_expert` loads the same archive before calling `RecommendMutations`/`DetectAntiPatterns` and returns the merged `learned_patterns` in its JSON result (plus a non-fatal `expert_archive_load_error`), so an advisory call surfaces accumulated cross-run learned patterns alongside the static catalog rather than the catalog alone.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1–2 of 2 landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `ExpertKnowledge` is no longer purely static: every `bt_evolve_qlearning` call that finds a genuinely fitness-improving mutation grows the same durable archive `bt_evolve_expert` reads, closing the one evolution algorithm in the package that had no cross-run memory at all.
- ✅ Reuses the ADR-024 flock, the ADR-034 sanitization helper, and the QTable's `Save`/`Load` cold-start/corrupt-archive contract rather than inventing new persistence semantics.
- ✅ Nil-safe `Observe` means every other existing `EvolveQLearning`/`qLearnMutate` caller (tests, and any future caller that doesn't care about `ExpertKnowledge`) is unaffected by the new parameter beyond having to pass `nil`.
- ⚠️ ~~Unlike `QTable`/`IslandModel`/`MAPElitesGrid`/`ParetoFront`, `ExpertKnowledge`'s archive has no `Cap` — `LearnedPatterns` grows unbounded across repeated `bt_evolve_qlearning` calls against one base tree, the same unbounded state the QTable itself shipped with before ADR-041 milestone 4 closed the gap.~~ — resolved by ADR-103 milestone 1/3 (2026-07-15)
- ⚠️ `LearnedPatterns` is observed only from `EvolveQLearning`'s mutation loop — the other five evolution algorithms (`Evolve`, `EvolveWithExperience`, `EvolvePareto`, `EvolveAll`, `EvolveMAPElites`, `NSGAIIPopulation.Evolve`) do not call `Observe`, so the learned archive reflects only what `bt_evolve_qlearning` itself has explored, not the fleet's full mutation history.
- ⚠️ `RecommendMutations`/`DetectAntiPatterns` still score and rank using only the hardcoded `Patterns`/`AntiPatterns` catalog — `LearnedPatterns` is surfaced in `bt_evolve_expert`'s JSON response for a caller to read, but does not yet feed back into which recommendations are generated or how they're ranked.
- Pinned by `TestExpertKnowledge_ObservesLearnedPatternFromQLearning` and `TestPopulation_EvolveQLearning_NilExpertKnowledgeNoOp` (`internal/evolution/learning_test.go`) and `TestBTEvolveExpertSurfacesLearnedPatternFromQLearning` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

## ADR-096: Benchmark-Suite Gate on the Standalone Evolution Tools' Durable-Archive Winners (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–2/4)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Gate the standalone evolution-algorithm tools' durable-archive winners through the benchmark suite, not structural fitness alone." Every `bt_evolve_*` tool that persists a winner to a durable cross-run archive (`bt_evolve_multiobjective`/ADR-091, `bt_evolve_island`/ADR-033, and — pending milestones 3–4 — `bt_evolve_pareto`, `bt_evolve_qlearning`) picks that winner using only `evolution.StructuralMultiFitness`/its siblings — success_rate/node_efficiency/stability computed from tree shape via structural heuristics, never an actual execution. A mutation can score as structurally elite while performing worse than the untouched base tree against the tree's own `internal/benchmark` suite; the "Mutation quality gates" Known Technical Debt row (§11) already flagged this exact gap for the daemon's `bt_evolve_*` tools after ADR-088 closed the analogous gap for the gardener's `evolveTreeV2`.

**Decision (milestone 1 of 4):** Add `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner(treeID string, base, winner *evolution.SerializableNode) (rejected bool, baseRate, winnerRate float64)` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`). It runs both the base tree and the winner through `benchmark.SuiteForTree(treeID)` with a `Sandbox`-mode mock LLM via `benchmark.RunSuite` — deterministic, no real LLM calls, `-short`-safe, preserving the ADR-009 determinism guarantee for these tools — called through a new package-level `benchmarkRunSuiteFn` seam (mirroring the existing `DelegateToA2AFn`/`AuctionDelegateFn` test-override pattern), and reports `rejected=true` when the winner's `SuccessRate` is lower than the base tree's. `bt_evolve_multiobjective` calls it on the NSGA-II front's lead individual immediately before `nsga.Save(archivePath)`: on rejection the save is skipped entirely (the durable NSGA-II archive, ADR-091, is left untouched), and the JSON result always carries `benchmark_gate_rejected`/`benchmark_base_success_rate`/`benchmark_winner_success_rate` so a caller can see the gate's verdict either way.

**Decision (milestone 2 of 4):** Reuse the same `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`/`benchmarkRunSuiteFn` seam for `bt_evolve_island`. `IslandModel.EvolveAll`'s return value — a `map[string]*evolution.SerializableNode` of each domain's best tree for that generation — was previously discarded by the handler's generation loop (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`); the loop now captures the final generation's map, and the handler picks a "cross-island best individual" as the tree belonging to whichever *seeded* domain has the highest `perIslandBest` structural fitness this run (a run with no seeded domain scoring at all skips the gate rather than gating a nil tree). That winner is gated against `baseTree` immediately before `im.Save(archivePath)`, with the identical unconditional-result/skip-save-on-reject contract as milestone 1: `benchmark_gate_rejected`/`benchmark_base_success_rate`/`benchmark_winner_success_rate` are always set, and `im.Save` runs only when the gate does not reject.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1 and 2 of 4 landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `bt_evolve_multiobjective` and `bt_evolve_island` can no longer persist a structurally-elite-but-behaviorally-regressed winner into their durable archives — the first two of the four `bt_evolve_*` tools with a durable archive to be checked against real execution behavior rather than a structural proxy alone.
- ✅ `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`/`benchmarkRunSuiteFn` were written in milestone 1 to be reused verbatim, and milestone 2 confirms that: `bt_evolve_island`'s wiring adds no new gating logic, only the cross-island-winner selection that feeds it.
- ⚠️ Both NSGA-II's and `IslandModel`'s mutation use unseeded `math/rand`, so an ordinary (non-adversarial) run can also regress and trip the gate — by design, but it means `benchmark_gate_rejected: true` can surface on an ordinary call, not only a contrived worst case, and any test asserting unconditional archive persistence must pin `benchmarkRunSuiteFn` to a non-regressing stub to stay deterministic (done for `TestBTEvolveMultiObjectiveAccumulatesDurableArchive` and, since milestone 2, every pre-existing `bt_evolve_island` archive-accumulation test — `TestBTEvolveIslandAccumulatesDurableArchive`, `TestBTEvolveIslandArchiveIsScopedPerBaseTree`, `TestBTEvolveIslandCapsBoundDurableArchiveAcrossCalls`, `TestBTEvolveIslandSurfacesEvictionCounters`, `TestBTEvolveIslandAdoptsLegacyGlobalArchiveOnce`).
- ⚠️ ~~Milestones 3–4 (`bt_evolve_pareto`, `bt_evolve_qlearning`) remain open — those two tools still persist their durable archives on structural fitness alone.~~ — `bt_evolve_qlearning` gated by ADR-111 (2026-07-15), which also gates `bt_evolve_qd` (outside this ADR's original four-tool count — `bt_evolve_qd` has its own durable MAP-Elites archive, ADR-043). `bt_evolve_pareto` gated by ADR-113 (2026-07-15) — every durable-archive `bt_evolve_*` tool now has this gate.
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveMultiObjectiveBenchmarkGateRejectsRegressedWinner` and, since milestone 2, `TestBTEvolveIslandBenchmarkGateRejectsRegressedWinner` (both `cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

## ADR-097: Domain-Aware Fitness Override for Genuine Knowledge-Graph Runs (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-15):** NotebookLM research goal. `domains.NotebookLMFitness` (`internal/domains/notebooklm_fitness.go`) computes a 0.0–1.0 score from a run's outcome, an anti-fabrication penalty, and output quality — but nothing in the repo ever called it. Every tree's `Fitness`, including `"notebooklm"`/`"notebooklm_consumer"`, was driven solely by `RecordRun`'s generic 0.9/0.1 runtime-success EMA (§8.4, ADR-028), which cannot distinguish an honestly-reported research run from a fabricated one.

**Decision:** Give the knowledge graph a per-tree fitness override hook rather than hardcoding NotebookLM's scoring into `RecordRun` itself. `KnowledgeGraph.RegisterDomainFitness(treeID, fn)` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) registers `fn func(runs []RunSummary) float64` against a tree ID in an unexported, in-process `domainFitness` map. `RecordRun`'s genuine-execution branch (`feedback.go`) now always appends the run's `{Outcome, Quality}` to a new bounded `TreeMeta.RecentRuns` window (`maxRunHistory` = 20, oldest evicted first), and — when a function is registered for that tree — sets `Fitness = fn(tree.RecentRuns) * 100` in place of the EMA; unregistered trees keep the EMA unchanged. `domains.RegisterNotebookLMFitness(kg)` wires `NotebookLMFitness` (adapting `RunSummary` to its own `NotebookLMRunSummary`) for both `"notebooklm"` and `"notebooklm_consumer"`, and `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` calls it once at startup, right after `kg.ExpectedDomains` is seeded.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `NotebookLMFitness` is reachable in production for the first time: `"notebooklm"`/`"notebooklm_consumer"` `Fitness` now reflects anti-fabrication-aware scoring instead of a bare success/failure EMA.
- ✅ The hook is domain-agnostic — a future tree category can register its own fitness function without adding another `RecordRun` branch.
- ✅ Every other tree is unaffected: with no registration, behavior is bit-for-bit the pre-existing EMA.
- ⚠️ `RecentRuns` is not part of the `feedback_persist.go` snapshot (only `Fitness`/`RunCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`LastOutcome`/`LastDuration` are, ADR-028) — a daemon restart preserves the last computed `Fitness` but resets the run-history window the domain function scores from, so it only ever sees genuine runs recorded since the most recent restart.
- ⚠️ `domainFitness` registrations are equally unpersisted — every process start must re-register via `RegisterNotebookLMFitness`, the same re-registration contract other in-memory seams (e.g. `RegisterEvolved`'s callers) already rely on.
- Pinned by `TestRecordRun_DomainFitness_OverridesGenericEMA`, `TestRecordRun_TracksBoundedRecentRunsWindow`, `TestRecordRun_NoDomainFitness_KeepsGenericEMA` (`internal/knowledge/graph_test.go`) and `TestRegisterNotebookLMFitness_WiresIntoKnowledgeGraph`/`TestRegisterNotebookLMFitness_DoesNotAffectOtherTrees` (`internal/domains/notebooklm_fitness_wiring_test.go`).

## ADR-098: An Operator-Triggered Evolution Kill Switch for the Gardener

**Context (2026-07-15):** NotebookLM research goal. `gardener.Registry.DeactivateAll()` (`internal/gardener/gardener.go`) — deactivating every registered tree and returning the prior active count — shipped fully tested (mirroring the ADR-093 `RollbackTree` pattern) but with no caller outside its own test, so an operator needing to halt all evolution cycles immediately (e.g. a runaway or misbehaving mutation pass) had no lever short of killing the `bt-gardener` process.

**Decision:** Expose it the same way `gardener_rollback` exposes `RollbackTree` (ADR-093). A new `GardenerDeactivateAllTool` (`cmd/bt-gardener/main.go`), registered alongside `gardener_status`/`gardener_run_cycle`/`gardener_recommend`/`gardener_rollback` and described in the agent's tool prompt, calls `Registry.DeactivateAll()` and returns `{"deactivated": <count>}` — the number of trees that were active immediately before the call.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Operators and the langchain gardener agent gain a kill switch reachable without a process restart, and the response confirms how many trees the call actually affected.
- ✅ Follows the `GardenerRollbackTool` precedent (ADR-093) exactly: a thin langchain-tool wrapper over an already-tested `Registry` method.
- ⚠️ `DeactivateAll` is one-directional — there is no paired reactivate-all tool, so recovery from an accidental call means re-enabling trees individually or restarting the daemon, since `Active` is never persisted and `loadAll`/`addBuiltin` always set it `true` on a fresh process.
- Pinned by `TestGardenerDeactivateAllTool_CallDeactivatesAllTrees` (`cmd/bt-gardener/main_test.go`).

---

## ADR-099: `DashboardRoutes()` Gains 15 Missing `/api/*` Route Definitions, Closing the Response-Validator's Route-Coverage Blind Spot (Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability Program, Milestone 1/4)

**Context (2026-07-15):** `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go` wires `api.ResponseValidator(api.DashboardRoutes(), ...)` as the last middleware in the dashboard's stack, comparing every `/api/*` response against the documented schema for that method+path. `ResponseValidator`'s route lookup (`internal/api/response_validator.go`) is an exact method+path match against a `RouteIndex` built from `DashboardRoutes()`; on a miss it silently passes the response through unvalidated — a documented `// TODO: support path parameter matching` (`response_validator.go:358`), not a bug, but one with no test pinning which live endpoints it left uncovered. `DashboardRoutes()` (`internal/api/openapi.go`) had drifted out of lockstep with the mux registrations added by many prior ADRs: agents lifecycle, workflow approval, HITL, and pipelines/blackboard endpoints were all live on `main.go`'s mux with zero matching `Route`, so their responses bypassed schema-drift detection entirely and — because a miss is silent — with no signal that they were unchecked.

**Decision:** A new `TestDashboardAPIRoutesHaveOpenAPICoverage` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`) regex-parses `main.go`'s `mux.HandleFunc("/api/...")` registrations and asserts every distinct path has a matching `Route` in `DashboardRoutes()`, naming any gap by path instead of leaving it to silent pass-through. Closing the gap it found, `DashboardRoutes()` gained 15 `Route` definitions across six groups: Agents lifecycle (`/api/agents`, `/api/agents/run`, `/api/agents/create`, `/api/agents/delete`), Tasks (`/api/tasks/create`), Workflow approval (`/api/workflow/pending`, `/api/workflow/approve`, `/api/workflow/reject` — the thinktank-recommendation review queue, distinct from the task pipeline's existing `/api/tasks/approve`/`/api/tasks/reject`), HITL (`/api/hitl/pending`, `/api/hitl/`), Pipelines (`/api/pipelines`, `/api/pipelines/run`, `/api/pipelines/status`), and Blackboard (`/api/blackboard`, `/api/blackboard/scopes`).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestone 1/4 of program "Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability — close the dashboard API response-validator's route-coverage blind spot."

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Every `/api/*` path currently registered on the dashboard mux has a matching `Route`, so `ResponseValidator`'s schema-drift check (and its `Enforce`-mode 500 rejection) now actually runs against all of them instead of silently passing ~15 endpoints through unchecked. Pinned by `TestDashboardAPIRoutesHaveOpenAPICoverage`.
- ⚠️ `RouteIndex.Lookup` matches the exact request path, so the new `/api/hitl/` `Route` only validates a literal `GET /api/hitl/` — it does not cover the `/api/hitl/{id}`, `/api/hitl/{id}/approve|reject|escalate` sub-paths `dashboard.HandleHITL` actually serves behind that same Go `ServeMux` subtree registration. Those sub-paths remain unvalidated until the validator itself gains path-parameter matching; milestones 2-4/4 of this program remain open.
- ⚠️ The coverage test derives its "registered" list from a regex over `main.go`'s `mux.HandleFunc("/api/...")` call sites — a registration added through any other pattern (a wrapping helper, a subrouter) would not be seen by the test and could silently reopen a gap.
- Pinned by `TestDashboardAPIRoutesHaveOpenAPICoverage` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`).

---

## ADR-100: `dashboard.PickTreeForTask` Consults `knowledge.KnowledgeGraph.Discover` Instead of Relying Solely on Its Static Keyword Switch
