**Context (2026-07-15):** NotebookLM research noted that `PickTreeForTask` (`internal/dashboard/executor.go`) — the tree-selection seam every dashboard-minted task without an explicit `TreeID` runs through (`handleAnalyze`, manual creation, the sprint loop, §6.3) — picked a tree via a static, hand-maintained 7-branch keyword `switch` (bug/build/security/research/test/refactor/default), entirely independent of `knowledge.KnowledgeGraph.Discover` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`), which already combines embedding similarity and ranked keyword/capability matching over the full 41+-tree knowledge graph and is the discovery mechanism every other tree-selection path in the platform uses. A dashboard task whose text didn't happen to match one of the seven hardcoded branches fell through to a generic default regardless of how confidently the knowledge graph itself could have matched it.

**Decision:** `internal/dashboard/executor.go` gains a package-level seam, `var DiscoverTreeFn func(task string) (treeID string, confidence float64)`, matching `Discover`'s signature so `internal/dashboard` can consult it without importing `internal/knowledge` (which would risk an import cycle back through the packages `internal/knowledge` itself depends on). `PickTreeForTask` now checks `isAuctionShapedTask` first exactly as ADR-073 left it, then — if `DiscoverTreeFn` is set and reports `confidence > 0` with a non-empty `treeID` — returns that tree ID, and only falls through to the historical static switch when `DiscoverTreeFn` is `nil` or reports no confident match (mirroring `Discover`'s own `("", 0)` "unconfident" contract). `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go` wires the seam immediately after constructing the graph: `kg = knowledge.BuildKnowledgeGraph(); dashboard.DiscoverTreeFn = kg.Discover`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15). Landed by hand from run 20260715T062853 alongside ADR-101 (the run's autonomous landing failed the gate on an unrelated errcheck finding).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Dashboard tasks now route through the same discovery mechanism (embedding similarity + ranked keyword/capability matching across all registered trees) every other tree-selection path already uses, instead of a separately maintained 7-branch keyword list that could only ever match what its author anticipated.
- ✅ The auction pre-check (ADR-073) still runs first and is unaffected — auction/delegation-shaped task text continues to route to `auction_demo` even when the knowledge graph would confidently suggest a different tree, since Contract Net auction routing is a distinct selection concern from generic tree discovery.
- ✅ A task the knowledge graph has no confident opinion on (a cold-started or lightly-registered tree category) still falls back to the static switch rather than routing to an empty tree ID — the switch is now a fallback, not the primary mechanism.
- ⚠️ `DiscoverTreeFn` is package-level, mutable, unguarded process-global state (mirroring several other injection seams in this codebase, e.g. `engine.AuctionDelegateFn`) — a test that sets it without restoring the original via `t.Cleanup` can leak into a sibling test in the same package.
- Pinned by `TestMainWiresKnowledgeGraphDiscoverIntoDashboard` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`) and `TestPickTreeForTask_ConsultsKnowledgeGraphOverStaticSwitch`/`TestPickTreeForTask_FallsBackToStaticSwitchWhenGraphUnconfident`/`TestPickTreeForTask_AuctionRoutingBeatsKnowledgeGraph` (`internal/dashboard/executor_test.go`).

## ADR-101: `dashboard.TaskStore` Load/Save Made Atomic and Fail-Loud on Corruption

**Context (2026-07-15):** NotebookLM research noted that `dashboard.TaskStore` (`internal/dashboard/tasks.go`) — the persisted record `handleSprintExecute`'s dispatch loop and the approval endpoints (ADR-072/080/081/086) all read and write — did not follow the atomic-write, fail-loud idiom §8.4/ADR-003 documents for every other store in this section (the DLQ's `save()`/`load()`, ADR-036; `QTable.Save`/`Load`, ADR-041; `IslandModel.Save`/`Load`, ADR-033). `Load` discarded `json.Unmarshal`'s error via a bare `_ = json.Unmarshal(data, s)`, so a corrupted `tasks.json` was indistinguishable from a freshly created empty store — the dashboard would silently start with zero tasks and no signal that anything had been lost. `saveLocked` wrote via a direct `os.WriteFile(s.path, data, 0644)`, truncating the live file in place rather than writing to a sibling temp file and renaming it into place, so a save that failed partway (disk full, process killed mid-write) could leave `tasks.json` truncated or corrupt instead of leaving the prior valid content untouched.

**Decision:** `Load` now returns an `error`: a missing file remains the historical no-op "fresh store, no tasks yet" case, but a read failure or a `json.Unmarshal` failure returns a wrapped `fmt.Errorf`. `NewTaskStore` — the constructor `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go` actually calls at startup, which has no error return in its signature — panics with that error rather than silently proceeding with an empty `Tasks` slice, since a panic-on-corrupt-startup is preferable to a dashboard that looks healthy while having quietly discarded every approved task. `saveLocked` now marshals to `s.path + ".tmp"`, then `os.Rename`s it into place, matching the tmp+rename idiom every other store in §8.4 already follows; a failure at either the write or the rename step returns an error without having touched the original file.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15). Landed by hand from run 20260715T062853 after its autonomous landing failed the gate on an errcheck finding in the regression test (unchecked `defer os.Chmod`); the production diff is the run's own, the test's deferred chmod now checks its error.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A corrupted `tasks.json` now fails loudly at startup (`NewTaskStore` panics) instead of silently presenting as an empty task list — an operator finds out immediately rather than discovering days later that every pending approval vanished. Pinned by `TestNewTaskStore_PanicsOnCorruptFile` and `TestTaskStore_LoadReturnsErrorOnCorruptJSON` (`internal/dashboard/tasks_test.go`).
- ✅ A save that cannot complete (e.g. a read-only directory blocking the sibling temp-file create) now leaves the existing `tasks.json` byte-for-byte untouched instead of risking a truncated write. Pinned by `TestTaskStore_SaveAtomicWriteLeavesOriginalIntactOnFailure`, which chmods the store's directory read-only mid-test to force exactly that failure mode and asserts the file content is unchanged.
- ✅ `dashboard.TaskStore` now matches the atomic-write, fail-loud-on-corruption pattern every other persisted store in §8.4 already follows, closing the one store in this section that had drifted from it.
- ⚠️ A caller that still wants the historical "log and continue with whatever's in memory" behavior on a corrupt file has no way to opt out — `NewTaskStore` always panics. This matches the deliberate severity choice already made for `agent.FileJobStore`/`goap.GoalStore` elsewhere in the codebase, not a new departure.

---

## ADR-102: `Gardener.evolveTreeV2`'s Crisis Mutation Budget Now Scales from the Detector's Calibrated `EmergencyRate` Instead of a Hardcoded Doubling

**Context (2026-07-15):** NotebookLM research noted that `evolveTreeV2`'s own per-cycle "Stage 3.5" crisis check (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`, distinct from the GA-level `Population.Crisis`/ADR-031 mechanism §8.5 documents separately) already called `CrisisDetector.Detect`/`Intervene` on diversity collapse or stagnation, but discarded the returned `InterveneAction.EmergencyRate` — the detector's own calibrated μ_emergency value — entirely, always setting `maxMutations = g.cfg.MaxMutations * 2` regardless of what the detector actually reported. A `CrisisDetector` calibrated to a higher or lower emergency rate than the 0.50 default had zero effect on the boosted budget: the hardcoded `× 2` and the detector's `EmergencyRate` field had silently diverged into two independent numbers, one of which was pure dead weight on every gardener cycle.

**Decision:** `maxMutations` is now derived from the actual `EmergencyRate` the intervention returns: `rate` is first clamped to `[0, 0.99]` (guarding a misconfigured `EmergencyRate` of 1.0 or above from dividing by zero or inverting the budget), then `maxMutations = int(math.Ceil(float64(g.cfg.MaxMutations) / (1 - rate)))`, with the existing `< 1 → 1` floor unchanged. At the detector's unchanged 0.50 default this reduces to exactly the historical `× 2` (`ceil(N / 0.5) == N * 2`), so today's production behavior is bit-for-bit unchanged; at any other calibration it diverges (e.g. `MaxMutations=2`, `EmergencyRate=0.75` now budgets `ceil(2/0.25)=8` mutations for that cycle, not `2*2=4`).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15). Landed by hand from run 20260715T062853 alongside ADR-101 (the run's autonomous landing failed the gate on an unrelated errcheck finding).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A `CrisisDetector` calibrated away from the 0.50 default now actually changes the gardener cycle's crisis mutation budget — the detector's `EmergencyRate` field is a live input to `evolveTreeV2` rather than a value it computes and the caller ignores.
- ✅ The default (unconfigured) behavior is provably unchanged — the new formula reduces to the old `× 2` exactly at `EmergencyRate = 0.50` — so this is a pure widening of the mechanism's range, not a behavior change for any cycle running with today's production config.
- ⚠️ `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go` still constructs the detector via the bare `evolution.NewCrisisDetector()` and never sets `EmergencyRate` away from its 0.50 default, so this fix is correctly wired but currently dormant in production — no gardener cycle today runs with a calibrated rate different from the historical doubling. A follow-up would need to actually calibrate `EmergencyRate` (e.g. from observed stagnation severity) for the widened range to matter.
- Pinned by `TestEvolveTreeV2_CrisisIntervention_UsesCalibratedEmergencyRate` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`), which sets `EmergencyRate = 0.75` on a fresh detector and asserts the resulting `CycleMetrics.MutationBudget` is `8`, not the hardcoded-doubling `4`.

---

## ADR-103: `ExpertKnowledge.LearnedPatterns` Capped at 500, Evicting the Lowest-Gain Entry (Q2 Evolvability, Milestone 1/3)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Feed Expert Knowledge's learned-pattern archive back into mutation recommendations and every production evolution algorithm." ADR-095 gave `ExpertKnowledge.LearnedPatterns` durable, cross-run growth via `Observe` but explicitly left it uncapped, the one durable archive in the evolution package without a bound — `QTable` (ADR-041 milestone 4), `IslandModel`/`MAPElitesGrid` (ADR-033/ADR-040), and `ParetoFront` (ADR-091) all cap; `ExperienceBank` set the original precedent (ADR-018, cap 500). Every `bt_evolve_qlearning` call against a base tree that finds a fitness-improving mutation appends to the same archive `bt_evolve_expert` loads and re-persists, so the slice — and the JSON file `ExpertKnowledge.Save` rewrites in full on every call — grows without bound across a tree's lifetime.

**Decision:** A new unexported `learnedPatternCap = 500` constant (`internal/evolution/expert.go`) mirrors `ExperienceBank`'s `experienceBankCap` exactly. `Observe` now checks `len(ek.LearnedPatterns) > learnedPatternCap` after every append and, when over cap, evicts the single lowest-`Gain` entry via a linear scan — a simpler eviction than `ExperienceBank.enforceCapLocked`'s reuse-count-protected variant, since `LearnedPatterns` (unlike `ExperienceEntry`) has no reuse-tracking field to protect on.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestone 1 of 3. Milestones 2–3 (folding `LearnedPatterns` into `RecommendMutations`/`DetectAntiPatterns`' ranking, and calling `Observe` from the five evolution algorithms besides `EvolveQLearning` — `Evolve`, `EvolveWithExperience`, `EvolvePareto`, `EvolveAll`, `EvolveMAPElites`, `NSGAIIPopulation.Evolve` — that don't yet report observed gains) are not yet implemented.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `ExpertKnowledge.LearnedPatterns` can no longer grow unbounded — a base tree evolved indefinitely via `bt_evolve_qlearning` now holds a fixed-size, fixed-cost archive instead of an ever-growing JSON file re-marshaled on every `Save`, closing the gap ADR-095 flagged and the last uncapped durable archive in the evolution package.
- ✅ Eviction favors the highest-gain patterns surviving under pressure, so the capped archive still represents the most valuable observed mutations rather than an arbitrary or oldest-first subset.
- ⚠️ Eviction is an unweighted linear scan over the full slice on every over-cap `Observe` call (O(n) per call, n ≤ 501) — cheap at this cap but, unlike `ExperienceBank`'s reuse-count protection, gives no special treatment to a lower-gain entry that has proven repeatedly useful, so a single high-gain outlier observed once can outlast a modest-gain pattern matched far more often.
- ⚠️ Milestones 2–3 of this program remain open: `LearnedPatterns` is still surfaced only as advisory JSON in `bt_evolve_expert`'s response (ADR-095) and still observed only from `EvolveQLearning`'s mutation loop — capping its growth does not yet change what it's used for or how it's populated.
- Pinned by `TestExpertKnowledge_Observe_CapsLearnedPatterns` (`internal/evolution/expert_test.go`), which observes 600 strictly-increasing-gain entries and asserts the surviving 500 all have gain > 100 (i.e. the lowest 100 were evicted, not an arbitrary subset).

## ADR-104: `ExpertKnowledge` Observation Across MAP-Elites/Pareto/NSGA-II/Island and Island Real Breeding (reconstructed)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15, reconstructed 2026-07-16)

**Provenance:** the original ADR-104 entry was never written to this log — the log jumped from ADR-103 to ADR-106, whose context paragraph carries only a one-line summary. This entry was reconstructed from §5.3's inline per-file annotations during the 2026-07-16 docs consolidation.

**Decision:** `NSGAIIPopulation`, `ParetoPopulation`, `IslandModel`, and `MAPElitesPopulation` each gain an optional `ExpertKnowledge` field that their mutation-application step `Observe`s into on every genuinely fitness-improving mutation — the same ek wiring `EvolveQLearning` already had (ADR-095).

**Details:**
- **pareto.go:** `ParetoPopulation`'s wiring lands via a new package-level `materializeMutationOp` helper that fills in a concrete `Node` payload for the `add_before`/`add_after`/`add_fallback` ops that `randomMutation`'s fallback vocabulary otherwise leaves incomplete (`ApplyMutations` silently no-ops those incomplete ops).
- **island.go:** `EvolveAll`'s per-island `selfHealGeneration` reproduce closure — previously a bare `pop.Evaluate(fitnessFn)` call that re-scored the same individuals every generation with no offspring produced at all (see ADR-038 milestone 5) — now actually breeds: elites survive untouched, the rest draw parents via `Select`/`Crossover` and apply a mutation observed into the new optional `ExpertKnowledge` field, mirroring `EvolvePareto`/NSGA-II `Evolve`'s mutation step.
- **multi_objective.go / map_elites.go:** `NSGAIIPopulation` and `MAPElitesPopulation` observe via their `Evolve`/`EvolveMAPElites` (already-existing crossover/) mutation steps on every genuinely-improving mutation.

**Consequences:** `Observe` coverage reached five of the six production algorithm families — `Population.Evolve`/`EvolveWithExperience` (the plain and experience-bank-warm-started GA) remain the one family that does not call it. Within-island evolutionary progress across generations, previously driven only by `Migrate` copying elites in from other islands, is now real.

## ADR-106: `ExpertKnowledge` Warm-Start/Save Wired into `bt_evolve_island`, `bt_evolve_multiobjective`, and `bt_evolve_pareto` (Q2 Evolvability, NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-15):** ADR-104 gave `NSGAIIPopulation`, `ParetoPopulation`, `IslandModel`, and `MAPElitesPopulation` each an optional `ExpertKnowledge` field that their mutation-application step `Observe`s into on every genuinely fitness-improving mutation — the same signal `EvolveQLearning`'s `qLearnMutate` already reports (ADR-095). But that wiring was algorithm-level only: none of the four production MCP tools that construct these types (`bt_evolve_multiobjective`, `bt_evolve_pareto`, `bt_evolve_island`, `bt_evolve_qd`) ever set the field, so the durable per-tree archive `bt_evolve_expert` reads kept growing from exactly one caller — `bt_evolve_qlearning`'s own tool-level `ek := evolution.NewExpertKnowledge(); ek.Load(expertArchivePath(...)); ...; ek.Save(...)` sequence (ADR-095 milestone 2) — no matter how many other algorithms could now observe into it.

**Decision:** Give `bt_evolve_multiobjective`, `bt_evolve_pareto`, and `bt_evolve_island` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) the identical warm-start/save sequence `bt_evolve_qlearning` already runs: construct `ek := evolution.NewExpertKnowledge()`, `ek.Load(expertArchivePath(params.Tree))` before evolving (a load error is surfaced non-fatally as `expert_archive_load_error`), assign it to the population's field (`nsga.ExpertKnowledge` / `pp.ExpertKnowledge` / `im.ExpertKnowledge`) before calling `Evolve` / `EvolvePareto` / `EvolveAll`, and `ek.Save(expertPath)` afterward (`expert_archive_save_error` on failure). For `bt_evolve_multiobjective` and `bt_evolve_island`, the save sits inside the same `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`-gated block that already guards `nsga.Save`/`im.Save` (ADR-096), so the learned-pattern archive only grows alongside a benchmark-accepted winner. `bt_evolve_pareto` has no such gate (ADR-096 milestones 3–4 remain open for it), so its `ek.Save` runs unconditionally alongside the pre-existing unconditional `archive.Save`. `bt_evolve_qd` is untouched by this change.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Three of the four newly `ExpertKnowledge`-capable production tools now actually populate the durable archive `bt_evolve_expert` reads — `bt_evolve_qlearning` is no longer the only caller growing it.
- ✅ Reuses `expertArchivePath` (ADR-034 per-base-tree scoping) and the load/save-error-as-JSON-field contract `bt_evolve_qlearning`'s own wiring established (ADR-095); no new persistence idiom is introduced.
- ⚠️ ~~`bt_evolve_qd` remains unwired — `MAPElitesPopulation.ExpertKnowledge` (ADR-104) still has no production caller that sets it.~~ — resolved by ADR-110 (2026-07-15)
- ⚠️ Milestone 3 of the ADR-103 program (folding `LearnedPatterns` into `RecommendMutations`/`DetectAntiPatterns`' ranking) is untouched by this change — the archive now grows faster but is still consulted only advisory-JSON-side by `bt_evolve_expert`.
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveIslandFeedsExpertKnowledgeArchive`, `TestBTEvolveMultiObjectiveFeedsExpertKnowledgeArchive`, and `TestBTEvolveParetoFeedsExpertKnowledgeArchive` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), each asserting `bt_evolve_expert` surfaces a non-empty `learned_patterns` after the sibling tool runs.

## ADR-107: The Gardener's Deep-Search `BestMutation` Now Feeds Back into the Live Tree (Q2 Evolvability, NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-15):** ADR-094 milestone 2 wired `evaluator.IterativeDeepening` into `evolveTreeV2` once the greedy per-candidate mutation loop finished, populating `CycleMetrics.DeepSearchUsed`/`DeepSearchDepth`/`TTHitRate` — but the returned `DeepeningResult.BestMutation`/`BestFitness` were computed and then discarded every cycle. A deep search that found a mutation genuinely better than anything the greedy loop had applied left the tree unchanged.

**Decision:** After the deep-search call, if `deep.BestMutation != nil && deep.BestFitness != nil` and `deep.BestFitness.Composite` beats the cycle's current `newFitness.Composite` by more than the existing `0.0001` epsilon, `evolveTreeV2` applies `deep.BestMutation.Op` directly to the live `tree` via `evolution.ApplyMutations`. On a non-zero apply count it increments `applied`, records the mutation into the experience bank via `bank.AddFromMutation` (mirroring how the greedy loop's own accepted mutations are recorded), replaces `newFitness` with `*deep.BestFitness`, recomputes `nodesAfter`, re-derives `improved`, and calls `g.cfg.Registry.SaveTree` again to persist the change. This is the only path left that can still improve a tree once the greedy loop's mutation budget is exhausted or found nothing.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A deep search that finds a genuinely better mutation than the greedy loop no longer goes to waste — it is applied, recorded into the experience bank, and persisted like any other accepted mutation.
- ✅ Reuses the same `evolution.ApplyMutations`/`bank.AddFromMutation`/`Registry.SaveTree` calls the greedy loop already uses, rather than introducing a parallel commit path.
- ⚠️ ~~The deep-search-applied mutation runs *after* the cycle's `ValidationGate`/`MetaValidator` checks (both of which only ever see the greedy loop's candidates) and after the crisis-intervention stagnation reset — it is gated only by the fitness-delta epsilon, not by the structural-safety (`MetaValidator`) or quality-threshold (`ValidationGate`) checks the rest of the cycle enforces.~~ — the `ValidationGate` half resolved by ADR-112 (2026-07-15): `evolveTreeV2` now re-runs `ValidationGate` against the deep-search-mutated tree before its second `Registry.SaveTree` call and reverts on rejection. ~~The `MetaValidator` half remains open — the deep-search-applied mutation still bypasses the structural-safety check the greedy loop's candidates go through.~~ — resolved by ADR-114 milestone 1 (2026-07-15): the deep-search mutation is now cloned and re-validated through `MetaValidator.ValidateMutation` before it is applied to the live tree.
- ⚠️ ~~`cmd/bt-gardener/config.go` still does not set `Config.TranspositionTablePath` (ADR-094), so this feedback path — like deep search itself — is reachable only from tests until a follow-up wires a table into the production daemon.~~ — resolved by ADR-114 milestone 2 (2026-07-15).
- Pinned by `TestEvolveTreeV2_DeepSearchResultAppliedWhenGreedyLoopFindsNothing` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`).

## ADR-108: The A2A Auction Responder Reuses `RespondToAnnouncement` and a Cached, Already-Signed Card (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-15):** `RespondToAnnouncement` (`internal/a2a/server.go`, introduced with ADR-008) already implemented the candidate-side parse → score → marshal sequence and was covered by its own tests, but `BTAgentExecutor.Execute`'s auction-bid branch never called it — it duplicated the same parse/score/marshal steps inline instead. That branch also called `ConvertToAgentCard(inst.Definition, "")` fresh on every inbound announcement, re-deriving (and re-`SignAgentCard`-ing, ADR-090) a card from the tree definition rather than reusing the already-signed card the server had built once at startup and was already advertising via `Server.CardCache`.

**Decision:** `BTAgentExecutor` gains a `CardCache map[string]*a2a.AgentCard` field, populated by `NewServer` from the same `cards` map assigned to `Server.CardCache` (`BuildCardRegistry`'s output — one map, two references, not two independent copies). `Execute`'s auction-bid branch now looks up `e.CardCache[agentName]` first, falling back to a fresh `ConvertToAgentCard` conversion only when no cached card exists for that agent, and calls `RespondToAnnouncement(agentName, card, taskText)` instead of re-implementing its logic — collapsing three duplicated call sites (`ConvertToAgentCard`, `ScoreAnnouncement`, `json.Marshal`) into the one function the auctioneer's own candidates already run through.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ One implementation of parse → score → marshal instead of two — a future change to bid scoring or encoding can no longer drift between `RespondToAnnouncement` and `Execute`'s inline copy.
- ✅ The card scored against an announcement is now the same card the A2A server actually advertises and signs, rather than a freshly re-derived (and separately re-signed) one computed per request — removing both the redundant signing work and the possibility that the two cards for one agent silently diverge.
- ⚠️ ~~`CardCache` is set once at `NewServer` construction and never reassigned in production; if the underlying card registry needed to change at runtime, the map itself would need in-place mutation (which both `Server.CardCache` and `BTAgentExecutor.CardCache` would observe, since they alias the same map) rather than a wholesale replacement of one side only.~~ — resolved by ADR-117 (2026-07-15): `Server.RefreshCards()` reassigns both `s.CardCache` and `s.Executor.CardCache` from a fresh `BuildCardRegistry` snapshot, called from `bt_agent_create` and autopilot's `activateAutomation` after each mutates the registry.
- Pinned by `TestExecute_AnnouncementScoresAgainstCardCacheNotFreshConversion` (`internal/a2a/server_test.go`), which gives the cached card different tag coverage than the tree-derived one would have and asserts `Execute` scores against the cached card.

## ADR-109: `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' Genetic Path Conditions Its Warm-Start on the Failing Task, Not Just Tree Type (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Condition bottleneck mutation search on the failing task's semantics, not just tree-type." `Population.EvolveWithExperience`'s warm-start (ADR-017) only ever consulted `bank.RetrieveByTreeType(treeType, ...)` — every bottleneck of the same tree type got the identical warm-start hints regardless of *which* failure motivated re-evolving it, even though `ComputeAnalytics` already captures a structured `LastFailureTask` per bottleneck and `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` already surfaces it in the report via `addFailureContext` (§8.5). That structured signal was reported but never consulted for retrieval.

**Decision:** **Milestone 1:** `Population.EvolveWithExperience` (`internal/evolution/learning.go`) is refactored into a thin wrapper around a new `Population.EvolveWithExperienceContext(generations, fitnessFn, bank, query string) *SerializableNode`: a non-empty `query` warm-starts from `bank.Retrieve(query, experienceHintTopK)` instead of `bank.RetrieveByTreeType(treeType, experienceHintTopK)`; an empty query keeps the exact `RetrieveByTreeType` path, so `EvolveWithExperience` itself (`return p.EvolveWithExperienceContext(generations, fitnessFn, bank, "")`) is behavior-preserving for its existing callers. **Milestone 2:** `ExperienceBank.AddFromMutation` (`internal/evolution/experience_bank.go`) gains an optional trailing `failureContext ...string` parameter; when a non-empty value is supplied, it is appended to the recorded entry's `Context` field as `"; failing_task=<text>"`, giving a later `Retrieve` query built from that same failing task's text real content in the entry's `Context` to match against (`Retrieve`'s search text already concatenates `TreeType`/`MutationOp`/`TargetNode`/`Context`/`Strategy`/`Summary`). **Milestone 3:** `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' genetic-fallback branch (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) builds `query` from `b.LastFailureTask` and calls `pop.EvolveWithExperienceContext(params.Generations, structuralFitnessFn, deps.expBank, b.LastFailureTask)` instead of the plain `EvolveWithExperience` that `bt_evolve_genetic` and `bt_evolve_selection_pressure` still call.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — all 3 milestones landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A bottleneck's warm-start hints are no longer scoped solely to its own tree type: `ExperienceBank.Retrieve`'s Jaccard-similarity ranking can surface a relevant prior mutation recorded under a completely different `TreeType`, as long as the query text scores well against it.
- ✅ `EvolveWithExperience`'s existing callers (`bt_evolve_genetic`, `bt_evolve_selection_pressure`, and the genetic-fallback branch's own prior behavior) are unaffected — the empty-query path is byte-for-byte the old `RetrieveByTreeType` call.
- ⚠️ ~~Milestone 2's write path has no production caller yet: `Population.mutateAndRecord` (the recorder `EvolveWithExperienceContext` itself drives) and both `AddFromMutation` call sites in `internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go` still record with no `failureContext` argument, so entries written by the live GA/gardener loops carry no `failing_task=` text in `Context`. Until a follow-up threads it through, milestone 3's query-conditioned retrieval re-ranks existing entries by their pre-existing `TreeType`/`MutationOp`/`TargetNode`/`Strategy`/`Summary` tokens plus `QualityScore` — the same signal `RetrieveByTreeType` already scores on — rather than by genuine failing-task semantic overlap.~~ — resolved 2026-07-16 (ADR-125): both write-side call sites now supply real failing-task context, and the gardener's `biasCandidatesWithExperience` retrieval is itself conditioned on the same signal.
- Pinned by `TestEvolveWithExperienceContext_QueryRetrievesAcrossTreeTypes`/`TestEvolveWithExperienceContext_EmptyQueryFallsBackToTreeType` (`internal/evolution/learning_test.go`, milestone 1), `TestAddFromMutation_RecordsFailureContext`/`TestRetrieve_MatchesOnFailureContext` (`internal/evolution/experience_bank_test.go`, milestone 2), and `TestBTEvolveBottlenecksGeneticPathQueriesExperienceByFailureTask` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`, milestone 3), which seeds an off-tree-type entry that `RetrieveByTreeType` could never find and asserts it is retrieved and marked reused once the handler threads `LastFailureTask` through.

---

## ADR-110: `bt_evolve_qd` Adopts `MAPElitesPopulation.EvolveMAPElites` Directly and Wires `ExpertKnowledge`, Closing the Q2 Evolvability Learned-Pattern Program (Milestones 1–2/2)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Wire `bt_evolve_qd`'s MAP-Elites illumination into the shared `ExpertKnowledge` learned-pattern archive." ADR-106 wired `ExpertKnowledge` warm-start/save into `bt_evolve_multiobjective`, `bt_evolve_pareto`, and `bt_evolve_island`, but left `bt_evolve_qd` as "the sole holdout": its handler evolved a bare `*evolution.Population` via plain `Population.Evolve` and only constructed a `MAPElitesGrid` afterward via `InsertFromPopulation`, so it never called `MAPElitesPopulation.EvolveMAPElites` — the one method whose mutation-application step actually `Observe`s into `ExpertKnowledge` (ADR-104, `map_elites.go:347`) — at all. `EvolveMAPElites` itself had shipped fully tested, including ADR-031 milestone 5's crisis-aware illumination, but with zero production callers (ADR-031's own Consequences: "`EvolveMAPElites` runs only on the LLM-supervised path"). ADR-009's Context, written when `bt_evolve_qd` was first exposed, gave the reason: `EvolveMAPElites`'s full driver "invoke[s] the LLM supervisor — non-deterministic and unusable under `-short`."

**Decision:** **Milestone 1:** `MCTSMutator.Mutate` (`internal/evolution/mcts_mutate.go:158`) now returns `(*SerializableNode, string)` instead of just `*SerializableNode` — the string is the winning mutation's `MutationOp.Operation` name, already tracked internally on `MCTSNode.MutationOp` but previously discarded at the `Mutate` return boundary. The zero-iteration fallback path threads it through a new `firstOpName(ops []MutationOp) string` helper (first op's `Operation`, or `""` for an empty slice); the full MCTS-search path returns `bestNode.MutationOp` directly; `validateOrFallback` gains a matching `opName` parameter and returns `""` whenever validation rejects the mutation, since no mutation actually took effect in that case. `Population.Evolve`'s sole production call site (`internal/evolution/learning.go:212`) discards the new value (`mutated, _ := mctsMutator.Mutate(...)`) — `Evolve` has no `ExpertKnowledge` field to feed it into. **Milestone 2:** `bt_evolve_qd`'s handler (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) replaces `pop.Evolve(params.Generations, structuralFitnessFn)` with constructing `mp := &evolution.MAPElitesPopulation{Population: pop, Grid: grid, Domain: params.Domain}` and calling `mp.EvolveMAPElites(params.Generations, structuralFitnessFn)` directly — the same production-adoption step ADR-057/ADR-106 gave the other four GA-family algorithms, closing out the last one. This was safe to do because `LLMSupervisor` (`internal/evolution/llm_supervisor.go`) is, despite its name, a deterministic and local heuristic ("mirrors the LLM policy contract from the research paper so production callers can consume stable JSON guidance without requiring a network LLM") — every other production `Evolve`/`EvolvePareto`/`EvolveAll`/NSGA-II `Evolve` variant already calls `supervisor.Guide` every generation with no `-short` cost, so ADR-009's stated rationale for routing `bt_evolve_qd` around `EvolveMAPElites` no longer holds (§9 ADR-009, corrected). `bt_evolve_qd` also gains the `ek := evolution.NewExpertKnowledge(); ek.Load(expertArchivePath(params.Tree)); mp.ExpertKnowledge = ek; ...; ek.Save(expertPath)` sequence, mirroring `bt_evolve_pareto`'s unconditional (non-benchmark-gated) load/save — `bt_evolve_qd` has no benchmark gate either — and surfacing `expert_archive_load_error`/`expert_archive_save_error` non-fatally exactly like the three ADR-106 tools. `grid.InsertFromPopulation` now reads `mp.Population` (the individuals `EvolveMAPElites` actually bred) instead of the pre-change `pop`, and the JSON result's `generations` field reads `mp.Generation`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — both milestones landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `EvolveMAPElites` is now reachable in production — the last of the five GA-family algorithms (`Evolve`, `EvolveWithExperience`, `EvolvePareto`, `EvolveAll`, `NSGAIIPopulation.Evolve`, `EvolveMAPElites`) to gain a `bt_evolve_*` caller — so `bt_evolve_qd`'s illumination is genuinely MAP-Elites-guided breeding (niche-diverse elite-parent selection, phase-aware mutation-rate guidance, and the ADR-031 milestone 5 crisis-intervention/specialist-resurrection loop) rather than a bare GA with the grid built only after the fact.
- ✅ Closes the last gap ADR-106 flagged: `MAPElitesPopulation.ExpertKnowledge` now has a production caller, so the durable `LearnedPatterns` archive `bt_evolve_expert` reads accumulates from all five `Observe`-capable algorithms' production tools instead of four.
- ✅ Corrects ADR-009's stated rationale for the original bare-`Evolve` choice: `LLMSupervisor` was never a network call; it is deterministic and `-short`-safe, matching every other production `Evolve` variant already calling it every generation.
- ⚠️ Milestone 1's `MutationOp` return value has no production consumer: `Population.Evolve` discards it via `_`, and `EvolveMAPElites`'s own mutation step tracks the op name it needs (`ops[0].Operation`) through a separate, already-existing code path rather than through `Mutate`'s new return — the new value is exercised only by the milestone's own unit test.
- ⚠️ `bt_evolve_qd`'s output for a given `population`/`generations` is no longer directly comparable to a pre-ADR-110 run — it now runs the fuller `EvolveMAPElites` loop (crossover, phase-aware mutation rate, crisis detection) rather than the bare GA — though neither path was reproducible run-to-run regardless, since both use unseeded `math/rand`.
- ⚠️ ~~`bt_evolve_qd` has no benchmark gate either.~~ — resolved by ADR-111 (2026-07-15): `grid.Save`/`ek.Save` are now gated by `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner` the same way `bt_evolve_multiobjective`/`bt_evolve_island` already were (ADR-096).
- Pinned by `TestMCTSMutator_Mutate_ReturnsWinningMutationOp` (`internal/evolution/mcts_mutate_test.go`, milestone 1) and `TestBTEvolveQdFeedsExpertKnowledgeArchive` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`, milestone 2), which asserts `bt_evolve_expert` surfaces a non-empty `learned_patterns` after `bt_evolve_qd` runs — mirroring the sibling assertions ADR-106 added for the other three tools.

## ADR-111: Benchmark-Suite Gate Extended to `bt_evolve_qd` and `bt_evolve_qlearning` (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 2–3/4)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Finish gating every evolution algorithm's durable-archive winner through the real benchmark suite." ADR-096 gave `bt_evolve_multiobjective` and `bt_evolve_island` a `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner` check before their durable-archive `Save`, but explicitly left the other durable-archive tools ungated: `bt_evolve_qlearning` (ADR-096's own milestone 4) unconditionally called `qt.Save`/`ek.Save` on `EvolveQLearning`'s returned `best`, and `bt_evolve_qd` — outside ADR-096's original four-tool count, since its own durable MAP-Elites archive (ADR-043) predates that program — unconditionally called `grid.Save`/`ek.Save` on `grid.BestIndividual().Tree` with a comment noting explicitly it "has no benchmark gate" (ADR-110). Both winners are still picked by `evolution.StructuralMultiFitness`/its siblings alone, so both tools could persist a structurally-elite-but-behaviorally-regressed tree into their durable archives exactly like the pre-ADR-096 `bt_evolve_multiobjective`/`bt_evolve_island`.

**Decision:** Reuse `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`/`benchmarkRunSuiteFn` (ADR-096) verbatim for both tools, with the identical unconditional-result/skip-save-on-reject contract. In `bt_evolve_qd` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`, ~932–1015), after `grid.InsertFromPopulation`, `grid.BestIndividual().Tree` is gated against `baseTree`; on rejection both `grid.Save(archivePath)` and the ADR-106 `ek.Save(expertPath)` are skipped. In `bt_evolve_qlearning` (~1318–1441), `EvolveQLearning`'s returned `best` is gated against `baseTree` before `qt.Save(archivePath)`/`ek.Save(expertPath)`, replacing the two prior unconditional saves. Both handlers set `benchmark_gate_rejected`/`benchmark_base_success_rate`/`benchmark_winner_success_rate` on every call, gate result or not.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 2 and 3 of 4 landed (milestone 1 = ADR-096's `bt_evolve_multiobjective`/`bt_evolve_island` gating; milestone 4 = ADR-112, a related but mechanically distinct gate in the gardener).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `bt_evolve_qd` and `bt_evolve_qlearning` can no longer persist a structurally-elite-but-behaviorally-regressed winner into their durable archives. ~~`bt_evolve_pareto` is now the only `bt_evolve_*` tool with a durable archive and no benchmark gate.~~ — resolved by ADR-113 (2026-07-15): `bt_evolve_pareto` now gates the same way, closing this out for every durable-archive `bt_evolve_*` tool.
- ✅ No new gating logic: both call sites reuse `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`/`benchmarkRunSuiteFn` exactly as ADR-096 wrote them, only supplying a different winner tree per algorithm.
- ✅ `bt_evolve_qd`'s gate also covers the ADR-106 `ek.Save` — like the `bt_evolve_multiobjective`/`bt_evolve_island` gating, the ExpertKnowledge archive now only grows alongside a benchmark-accepted winner, not unconditionally as ADR-110 left it.
- ⚠️ Both `EvolveMAPElites`'s and `EvolveQLearning`'s mutation use unseeded `math/rand`, so — mirroring the ADR-096 caveat for NSGA-II/`IslandModel` — an ordinary run can also regress and trip the gate; every pre-existing archive-accumulation/ExpertKnowledge test for these two tools now pins `benchmarkRunSuiteFn` to a non-regressing stub to stay deterministic (`TestBTEvolveQDAccumulatesDurableArchive`, `TestBTEvolveQLearningAccumulatesDurableArchive`, `TestBTEvolveExpertSurfacesLearnedPatternFromQLearning`, `TestBTEvolveQdFeedsExpertKnowledgeArchive`).
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveQDBenchmarkGateRejectsRegressedWinner` and `TestBTEvolveQLearningBenchmarkGateRejectsRegressedWinner` (both `cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), each forcing a regression via `benchmarkRunSuiteFn` and asserting `benchmark_gate_rejected: true` plus no new archive file on disk.

## ADR-112: Deep-Search-Applied Gardener Mutations Are Re-Validated Before Persisting (Q2 Evolvability, Milestone 4/4)

**Context (2026-07-15):** ADR-107 made `evolveTreeV2`'s deep-search `BestMutation` apply directly to the live tree and persist via a second `Registry.SaveTree` call — but, as ADR-107's own Consequences flagged, that apply runs *after* the cycle's `ValidationGate`/`MetaValidator` checks (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`, ~line 307), both of which only ever see the greedy per-candidate loop's mutations. The deep-search path was gated only by the fitness-delta epsilon (`deep.BestFitness.Composite > newFitness.Composite+0.0001`), not by the same quality-threshold check every other accepted mutation in the cycle goes through — a deep-search mutation that improved structural fitness while failing the tree's own SLO-evidence bar could still be committed and persisted.

**Decision:** Mirror the greedy loop's own gate (`ValidationGate(entry.Name, entry.Name, g.cfg.ValidationGate)`, called immediately after the greedy loop's `SaveTree` decision) for the deep-search path too. Before applying `deep.BestMutation`, `evolveTreeV2` now snapshots the tree, fitness, and `applied` count (`preDeepSearchTree := cloneTreeForGardener(tree)`, `preDeepSearchFitness`, `preDeepSearchApplied`). After the mutation is applied and `newFitness`/`nodesAfter`/`improved` are recomputed, it re-runs `ValidationGate` against the mutated tree; on rejection it restores all three snapshotted values (`*tree = *preDeepSearchTree`, `newFitness = preDeepSearchFitness`, `applied = preDeepSearchApplied`) and skips the second `Registry.SaveTree` call entirely; on acceptance, `SaveTree` runs exactly as ADR-107 left it.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestone 4 of the "Finish gating every evolution algorithm's durable-archive winner through the real benchmark suite" program (ADR-111). Mechanically distinct from that program's other three milestones: this gate is `ValidationGate`'s SLO-evidence check (`internal/gardener/validation_gate.go`), not `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`'s `internal/benchmark`-suite comparison — the gardener has no equivalent per-mutation benchmark-suite gate — but it closes the same class of gap (a winner persisted before real-execution evidence backs it) for the one production mutation-acceptance path ADR-107 had left ungated.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A deep-search mutation that improves structural fitness but fails `ValidationGate` (e.g. no SLO evidence for the tree, failing closed — `validation_gate.go:55–66`) can no longer be committed to the live tree or persisted via `Registry.SaveTree`; the cycle reverts to its pre-deep-search state, matching the greedy loop's own rejection behavior exactly.
- ✅ Reuses the existing `ValidationGate`/`cloneTreeForGardener` primitives — no new gating mechanism, only a second call site.
- ⚠️ ~~`MetaValidator` — the structural-safety check the greedy loop's candidates also pass through — still does not see the deep-search mutation; ADR-107's second flagged gap (structural-safety, as opposed to quality-threshold, coverage) remains open.~~ — resolved by ADR-114 milestone 1 (2026-07-15).
- ⚠️ ~~`cmd/bt-gardener/config.go` still does not set `Config.TranspositionTablePath` (ADR-094), so deep search — and therefore this gate — remains reachable only from tests until a follow-up wires a table into the production daemon.~~ — resolved by ADR-114 milestone 2 (2026-07-15).
- Pinned by `TestEvolveTreeV2_DeepSearchMutationRejectedByValidationGateNotPersisted` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`), which configures `ValidationGate` with no SLO evidence for the test tree (fail-closed) and asserts the mutation count, fitness, in-memory tree, and on-disk tree file are all reverted to their pre-deep-search state.

## ADR-113: Benchmark-Suite Gate Extended to `bt_evolve_pareto`, Closing Out Q2 Evolvability's Benchmark-Gate Coverage

**Context (2026-07-15):** ADR-096 gated `bt_evolve_multiobjective`/`bt_evolve_island`'s durable-archive saves through `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`, and ADR-111 extended the same gate to `bt_evolve_qd`/`bt_evolve_qlearning` — but both ADRs' own Consequences explicitly named `bt_evolve_pareto` as the one remaining durable-archive `bt_evolve_*` tool with no benchmark gate: its handler (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`, ~1240) called `archive.Save(archivePath)`/`ek.Save(expertPath)` unconditionally on the front-elitism winner `EvolvePareto` returned, picked using only `evolution.StructuralMultiFitness` — the same structural-heuristic-only risk ADR-096 first flagged for NSGA-II/Island.

**Decision:** Reuse `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`/`benchmarkRunSuiteFn` (ADR-096) verbatim, with the identical unconditional-result/skip-save-on-reject contract every other gated tool already uses: `pp.EvolvePareto`'s returned `best` is gated against `baseTree` immediately before the archive/ExpertKnowledge saves; `benchmark_gate_rejected`/`benchmark_base_success_rate`/`benchmark_winner_success_rate` are always set on the JSON result, and both `archive.Save` (the ADR-091 durable Pareto front) and `ek.Save` (the ADR-106 ExpertKnowledge archive) are skipped together on rejection — mirroring `bt_evolve_qd`'s gate, which likewise covers both its grid and ExpertKnowledge saves in one block (ADR-111).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15). Closes the gap ADR-096 and ADR-111 both flagged as their sole remaining open item — every `bt_evolve_*` tool with a durable cross-run archive (`bt_evolve_multiobjective`, `bt_evolve_island`, `bt_evolve_qd`, `bt_evolve_qlearning`, `bt_evolve_pareto`) now gates its winner through the real benchmark suite before persisting. ⚠️ This change was reported as "milestone 1 of 4" of a program titled "Finish gating every evolution algorithm's durable-archive winner through the real benchmark suite" — but that exact title's 4 milestones were already spent by ADR-096 (milestone 1), ADR-111 (milestones 2–3), and ADR-112 (milestone 4). This ADR is a follow-on fix for the one item ADR-096/ADR-111's own Consequences left open, not a new numbered milestone of that already-closed program.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ No `bt_evolve_*` tool with a durable cross-run archive can still persist a structurally-elite-but-behaviorally-regressed winner — the gap `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner` (ADR-096) was built to close now covers all five.
- ✅ No new gating logic: reuses `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner`/`benchmarkRunSuiteFn` exactly as ADR-096 wrote them, only supplying `bt_evolve_pareto`'s own winner tree.
- ⚠️ `EvolvePareto`'s mutation uses unseeded `math/rand`, so — mirroring the ADR-096/ADR-111 caveat for the other four tools — an ordinary run can also regress and trip the gate; the pre-existing `TestBTEvolveParetoAccumulatesDurableArchive` and `TestBTEvolveParetoFeedsExpertKnowledgeArchive` now pin `benchmarkRunSuiteFn` to a non-regressing stub to stay deterministic.
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveParetoBenchmarkGateRejectsRegressedWinner` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), which forces a regression via `benchmarkRunSuiteFn` and asserts `benchmark_gate_rejected: true` plus no new Pareto front archive file on disk.

## ADR-114: The Gardener's Deep-Search Apply Path Is Gated Through `MetaValidator` and Wired Live in Production (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Harden and activate the gardener's Stockfish-style deep-search apply path in production." Two gaps ADR-107 and ADR-112 had each already flagged in their own Consequences remained open: (1) the `deep.BestMutation` branch in `evolveTreeV2` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`, ~line 356–386) — since ADR-112, re-validated against `ValidationGate` before its second `Registry.SaveTree` — still bypassed `MetaValidator`, the structural-safety check the greedy per-candidate loop above it (line 274–280) already consults via `g.cfg.MetaValidator.ValidateMutation`; a deep-search mutation that improved fitness while being structurally broken (empty selector, unbounded retry, expert antipattern) could still be committed. (2) `buildGardenerConfig` (`cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`) never set `Config.TranspositionTablePath` (ADR-094), so `Gardener.transpositionTable()` always returned `nil` and `evaluator.IterativeDeepening` — and therefore both the deep-search apply path (ADR-107) and this very `MetaValidator` gate — never ran outside tests. Separately, `gardener-metrics.json`'s aggregate `metricsDocument` (ADR-032) had no rollup of deep-search activity; seeing it required replaying the full per-cycle `History` array for `DeepSearchUsed`/`TTHitRate`.

**Decision (milestone 1 of 3):** Mirror the greedy loop's own `MetaValidator` gate for the deep-search path. Before applying `deep.BestMutation.Op` to the live tree, `evolveTreeV2` builds a cloned `candidateTree`, applies the mutation to the clone, and — when `g.cfg.MetaValidator` is set — calls `ValidateMutation(preDeepSearchTree, candidateTree, preDeepSearchFitness.Composite, deep.BestFitness.Composite)`; a `MetaReject` decision sets a `metaRejected` flag and the subsequent `evolution.ApplyMutations(tree, ...)` call (and everything downstream of it — `applied++`, the experience-bank record, the second `Registry.SaveTree`) is skipped, leaving `tree`/`newFitness`/`applied` at their pre-deep-search values exactly as an `ValidationGate` rejection already does (ADR-112).

**Decision (milestone 2 of 3):** `buildGardenerConfig` now sets `Config.TranspositionTablePath` to `filepath.Join(metricsDir, "transposition")`, alongside the existing `MetaValidator` construction. This is the same class of fix ADR-079 made for `Config.SelectorStatsPath`: the mechanism was already implemented and tested (ADR-094 milestones 1–3), only unreachable from the daemon's real evolution cycles for want of a configured path. With a path set, `transpositionTable()` lazily constructs and caches a real `*evaluator.TranspositionTable`, so deep search (ADR-107) and the milestone-1 `MetaValidator` gate above both become live for every scheduled `bt-gardener` cycle and every MCP-triggered `gardener_run_cycle` call, not only in tests.

**Decision (milestone 3 of 3):** `metricsDocument` (`internal/gardener/gardener.go`) gains `TotalDeepSearchCycles int` and `AvgTTHitRate float64`. `MetricsTracker.Save()` computes them in the same per-cycle loop that already aggregates `TotalCrisisInterventions`/`TotalImprovements`: `TotalDeepSearchCycles` counts cycles with `CycleMetrics.DeepSearchUsed`, and `AvgTTHitRate` is the mean `TTHitRate` over exactly those cycles (rounded to 4 decimal places), left at its zero value when no cycle used deep search.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — all 3 milestones landed together.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A deep-search mutation that improves structural fitness but fails `MetaValidator` (empty selector, unbounded retry, expert antipattern, oversized/over-deep candidate, >2.5x-baseline node growth) can no longer be committed to the live tree — closing the second of the two gaps ADR-107's Consequences named, the first (`ValidationGate`) having already been closed by ADR-112. The deep-search apply path is now gated by the same two checks — quality-threshold and structural-safety — as every other accepted mutation in the cycle.
- ✅ Deep search itself, and everything gated on a configured transposition table (the ADR-107 apply path, the milestone-1 `MetaValidator` gate above), is reachable in production for the first time — closing the "reachable only from tests" caveat ADR-094, ADR-107, and ADR-112 each carried forward unresolved.
- ✅ `gardener-metrics.json` now self-reports deep-search coverage (`total_deep_search_cycles`, `avg_tt_hit_rate`) the same way it already self-reports crisis interventions (ADR-032) — a consumer can read aggregate deep-search activity without replaying `history`. No dashboard panel reads these two fields yet; they are surfaced in the document, not yet wired to a consumer, matching how `TotalCrisisInterventions` itself shipped before ADR-032's dashboard-side milestones landed.
- ⚠️ The transposition table now persists to `<metricsDir>/transposition` in production, adding one more file the gardener's metrics directory must remain writable for; a save failure there is a non-fatal warning (ADR-094 milestone 1), not a cycle abort.
- Pinned by `TestEvolveTreeV2_DeepSearchMutationRejectedByMetaValidatorNotPersisted` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`, milestone 1 — a strict `MetaValidator{MinScore: 1.0}` with `ValidationGate` disabled, isolating `MetaValidator` as the sole rejection source), `TestBuildGardenerConfig_TranspositionTableWired` (`cmd/bt-gardener/config_test.go`, milestone 2), and `TestMetricsTracker_SaveAggregatesDeepSearchCoverage` (`internal/gardener/gardener_test.go`, milestone 3).

## ADR-115: Multi-Revision Snapshot History and Automatic Fail-Closed Rollback for the Gardener (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Make gardener mutation rollback automatic, multi-revision, and observable instead of a dead-code-guarded manual snapshot." ADR-093 wired `evolution.SnapshotTree`/`RestoreTree` and `Registry.RollbackTree` into the gardener, but left two gaps: `SnapshotTree` (`internal/evolution/quality_gate.go`) overwrote a single `snapshot_<treeName>.json` on every cycle, so a regression discovered several cycles after it was introduced had no earlier state to roll back to — only the immediately-preceding (and possibly already-regressed) one; and `evolveTreeV2`'s (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go:229-235`) fail-closed branch, reached once `QualityGate.IsDisabledFor` trips after `ConsecutiveFails` regressions, only skipped that cycle's mutation candidates — `RollbackTree` existed but was reachable solely through an operator- or LLM-triggered `gardener_rollback` call, so a tree stayed frozen in its regressed state until someone noticed and intervened, or the process restarted.

**Decision (milestone 1 of 3):** Replace the single overwritten snapshot file with an accumulating, indexed history. `SnapshotTree` now writes each call's tree to a sequentially-numbered `snapshot_<treeName>_<seq>.json` and appends the new sequence number to a small `snapshot_<treeName>.index.json` index file (both via the existing atomic tmp+rename idiom), never overwriting an earlier revision. A new `ListRevisions(treeName, snapshotDir) ([]int, error)` reads that index, oldest-first; a new `RestoreTreeRevision(treeName, snapshotDir, revision int)` loads one specific revision; and `RestoreTree` becomes a thin wrapper that resolves to the newest entry in `ListRevisions`, so existing callers keep their "restore the latest snapshot" behavior unchanged.

**Decision (milestone 2 of 3):** Wire automatic rollback into the fail-closed path. The `g.cfg.Gate.IsDisabledFor(entry.Name)` check in `evolveTreeV2` moves to immediately before the pre-mutation `SnapshotTree` call (snapshotting the current regressed state first would make it the new "most recent" revision and defeat the rollback). When the gate is disabled, `evolveTreeV2` now calls `g.cfg.Registry.RollbackTree(entry.Name, g.cfg.SnapshotDir)` — restoring and durably re-persisting the tree's last-known-good revision — and returns a `CycleMetrics` with `Rollbacks: 1` on success (0 on a failed or unconfigured rollback) instead of merely logging a warning and skipping the candidate loop.

**Decision (milestone 3 of 3):** Surface rollback activity end to end. `metricsDocument` (`internal/gardener/gardener.go`) gains `TotalRollbacks int`, summed by `MetricsTracker.Save()` from each cycle's `CycleMetrics.Rollbacks` in the same loop that already aggregates `TotalCrisisInterventions`/`TotalImprovements`. `dashboard.GardenerMetrics` (`internal/dashboard/metrics.go`) gains a matching `Rollbacks` field, and `loadGardenerMetrics()` reads it from `total_rollbacks`, so a recorded rollback becomes visible on the dashboard's gardener panel the same way crisis interventions already are.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — all 3 milestones landed together.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A regression discovered several cycles after it was introduced can be rolled back past just the immediately-preceding cycle — `ListRevisions`/`RestoreTreeRevision` reach any prior snapshotted state, not only the latest.
- ✅ A tree whose quality gate trips is actively repaired the same cycle, not merely paused: `RollbackTree` now runs automatically instead of requiring an operator- or LLM-triggered `gardener_rollback` call.
- ✅ Rollback activity is now observable in `gardener-metrics.json` (`total_rollbacks`) and the dashboard's `GardenerMetrics.Rollbacks`, closing the "silently dropped" gap `CycleMetrics.Rollbacks` had carried since its own introduction.
- ⚠️ The ADR-093 caveat still applies to the automatic path too: rollback restores unconditionally, without re-running `QualityGate.ValidateFor`/`MetaValidator.ValidateMutation` against the restored tree and without resetting the fail-streak (`ResetFailCount`) — a tree that auto-rolls-back stays gate-disabled (mutations still paused) until a process restart, it is simply no longer left sitting in the regressed state while paused.
- Pinned by `TestSnapshotTreeMultiRevision`, `TestListRevisionsEmpty` (`internal/evolution/quality_gate_test.go`), `TestEvolveTreeV2_DisabledGateTriggersAutomaticRollback` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`), `TestMetricsTracker_SaveAggregatesRollbacks` (`internal/gardener/gardener_test.go`), and `TestLoadGardenerMetricsParsesRollbacks` (`internal/dashboard/metrics_test.go`).

## ADR-116: `RunFullPipeline` Executes Every Present Sprint, Halts on a Thinktank-Phase Error, and Gets Its First Production Caller (Q1 Correctness Program, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q1 Correctness — Fix and wire the dashboard's dead-code `RunFullPipeline` autonomous workflow entry point." ADR-072/080/081/082/089 each flagged the same residual in turn: `internal/dashboard/workflow_engine.go`'s `Workflow.RunFullPipeline` was fully unit-tested but had zero callers outside `workflow_engine_test.go`, even as `handleAnalyze` and the `/api/workflow/*` approval endpoints steadily gained production callers for the rest of `Workflow`'s methods. Two independent correctness bugs sat underneath that caller gap. First, `RunFullPipeline`'s sprint-execution phase was a hardcoded `w.ExecuteSprint(1, compOrch); w.ExecuteSprint(2, compOrch)` pair — a task whose `SprintTarget` was 3 or higher (e.g. one derived from `DissentingNotes`) was silently never executed regardless of approval. Second, all five thinktank orchestrator calls (`RunResearchRound`/`RunDebate`/`RunSynthesis`/`RunPeerReview`/`RunReportGeneration`) had their errors discarded via `_ =` — ADR-080's Consequences named this explicitly as unresolved by its own `handleAnalyze`-side error-surfacing fix — so a failed phase left `RunFullPipeline` to proceed into `RecommendationsToTasks` with stale or empty `Synthesis` instead of halting.

**Decision (milestone 1 of 3):** `Workflow` gains an unexported `distinctSprintTargets()` method that, under `w.mu`, collects every distinct `SprintTarget` present in `w.Tasks` into a sorted slice. `RunFullPipeline`'s sprint-execution phase becomes `for _, sprintNum := range w.distinctSprintTargets() { w.ExecuteSprint(sprintNum, compOrch) }`, so it executes exactly the sprints that have tasks rather than an assumed two.

**Decision (milestone 2 of 3):** `RunFullPipeline` checks the error each of the five `ttOrch` phase calls returns; on the first non-nil error it sets `w.Status = "failed"` and returns immediately, before `RecommendationsToTasks`, task creation, or `ExecuteSprint` run at all — mirroring the check-and-halt pattern ADR-080 already gave `handleAnalyze`'s own three-phase call sequence.

**Decision (milestone 3 of 3):** `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go` gains `handleWorkflowRunFullPipeline`, registered at `POST /api/workflow/run-full-pipeline?topic=`. It builds a `thinktank.Orchestrator` and a `startup.CompanyOrchestrator` the same way `handleAnalyze`/`handleSprintExecute` already do, constructs `wf := dashboard.NewWorkflow(tt.Name, tt, companyState)`, calls `wf.RunFullPipeline(ttOrch, compOrch)`, stores the resulting `*dashboard.Workflow` into the package-level `currentWorkflow` under `currentWorkflowMu` (mirroring `handleAnalyze`'s locking convention), and persists every derived `WorkflowTask` into `taskStore` — keyed on the same `wf.ID+"-"+wt.ID` convention `handleAnalyze`/`handleWorkflowApprove`/`handleWorkflowReject` share, with `PickTreeForTask` routing — before responding with `{"topic": ..., "status": wf.Status}`. `internal/api/openapi.go` gains a matching `Route` for the new path, keeping `DashboardRoutes()` complete per ADR-099's route-coverage invariant.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — all 3 milestones landed together, closing the "`RunFullPipeline` has no production caller" residual first flagged in ADR-072's Consequences and repeated through ADR-080, ADR-081, ADR-082, and ADR-089.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A task targeting sprint 3 or beyond now actually executes instead of being silently skipped by the previously-hardcoded two-sprint loop. Pinned by `TestRunFullPipeline_ExecutesEverySprintPresent` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`), which pre-seeds three approved tasks across sprints 1–3 and asserts all three reach `StatusCompleted`.
- ✅ A failing thinktank phase now halts `RunFullPipeline` at `w.Status = "failed"` before any task is created, instead of silently proceeding into `RecommendationsToTasks` with stale or empty synthesis. Pinned by `TestRunFullPipeline_HaltsOnPhaseFailure`, which fails the synthesis phase and asserts the pipeline stops immediately (no peer-review/report phases run, zero tasks created, `ExecuteSprint`'s `RunSprint` never called).
- ✅ `RunFullPipeline` — the last of `Workflow`'s core methods still without a production caller — now has one. Pinned by `TestHandleWorkflowRunFullPipeline_PersistsWorkflowAndTasks` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), which asserts `currentWorkflow.Status == "completed"`, and that `taskStore` holds one persisted task per derived `WorkflowTask` under the `wf.ID+"-"+wt.ID` convention.
- ⚠️ Because `RunFullPipeline` still never auto-approves (ADR-081) and each call to `/api/workflow/run-full-pipeline` builds a brand-new `Workflow`, every task it derives starts `StatusPending`; `ExecuteSprint`'s per-sprint loop still runs `compOrch.RunSprint()` (the company-state simulation) but advances no task status on that first pass. Real per-task bt-agent dispatch for these tasks still depends on a subsequent explicit `/api/workflow/approve` or `/api/tasks/approve` decision and `handleSprintExecute` picking them up from `taskStore` — this endpoint drives the full research-through-sprint-simulation pipeline in one call, but does not itself bypass the human approval gate.
- Pinned by `TestRunFullPipeline_ExecutesEverySprintPresent`, `TestRunFullPipeline_HaltsOnPhaseFailure` (`internal/dashboard/workflow_engine_test.go`), and `TestHandleWorkflowRunFullPipeline_PersistsWorkflowAndTasks` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`).

## ADR-117: The A2A Card Registry Becomes Refreshable at Runtime Instead of a One-Shot `NewServer` Snapshot (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-15):** `Server.CardCache` and its mirrored `BTAgentExecutor.CardCache` (ADR-108) were both built exactly once, inside `NewServer`, from a single `BuildCardRegistry(reg, baseURL)` snapshot of the agent registry at process-startup time. Neither field was ever reassigned afterward. An agent created after that point — via the `bt_agent_create` MCP tool or autopilot's `activateAutomation` (both call `Create`/`InstallFromTemplate` against the very same live `*agent.Registry` the server was built from) — had no card in either cache: it was unreachable at its own per-agent A2A endpoint, absent from the global agent card, and invisible to `AuctionCardSource`'s candidate pool, so it could neither be delegated to directly nor win an auction, until the whole process restarted and `NewServer` ran again. ADR-108 flagged this gap explicitly as an open caveat.

**Decision:** `Server` gains a `RefreshCards() error` method (`internal/a2a/server.go`) that calls `BuildCardRegistry(s.Reg, s.BaseURL)` again and reassigns `s.CardCache` to the result, additionally reassigning `s.Executor.CardCache` when `s.Executor` is non-nil — a wholesale replacement of both references to the same conceptual registry, not an in-place mutation of the old map. `mcpDeps` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) gains a `refreshA2ACards func() error` field; `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` sets it to `a2aSrv.RefreshCards` only inside the `a2aErr == nil` branch that already wires the tree resolver and auction candidate source, so it stays `nil` (and every caller nil-checks it) whenever the A2A server failed to start. Both agent-creation call sites invoke it immediately after their own `Create` call succeeds — `bt_agent_create`'s handler (covering both the direct-`Create` and `InstallFromTemplate` paths) and autopilot's `activateAutomation` (`cmd/bt-agent/autopilot.go`, which also now returns any non-"already exists" `Create` error instead of silently swallowing it) — logging a warning via `engine.Warn` rather than failing the request if the refresh itself errors.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ An agent created or activated at runtime becomes reachable over A2A and eligible for auctions the same tick it is registered, closing the restart-required gap ADR-108 left open.
- ✅ `Server.CardCache` and `Executor.CardCache` are kept as two references to one freshly-built map rather than drifting into two independently-stale ones, and `AuctionCardSource`'s closure reads `s.CardCache` directly, so it too reflects the refreshed registry with no separate wiring.
- ⚠️ The refresh is caller-triggered, not automatic — an agent created through some other path than `bt_agent_create`/`activateAutomation` (e.g. a future MCP tool or direct `agentReg.Create` call that forgets to invoke `refreshA2ACards`) would silently regress to the old one-shot-snapshot behavior for that path.
- ⚠️ `RefreshCards` rebuilds the entire registry from scratch on every call rather than incrementally adding the one new/changed agent; fine at today's agent-fleet scale, but a large registry would make every creation pay a full rebuild.
- Pinned by `TestServer_RefreshCards_PicksUpAgentCreatedAfterStartup` (`internal/a2a/server_test.go`), which creates an agent after `NewServer`, calls `RefreshCards`, and asserts the new agent appears in `Server.CardCache`, `Executor.CardCache`, and the `AuctionCardSource()` candidate pool.

## ADR-118: `bt_evolve_memetic` and the CMA-ES Branch of `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` Stop Discarding Their Evolved Winner (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-15):** ADR-042 gave the genetic-family tools (`bt_evolve_genetic`, `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`'s genetic-fallback branch, `bt_evolve_selection_pressure`) a `persistEvolvedWinner` seam so their bred winner survives as a resolvable, discoverable tree instead of vanishing with the tool call. Two production evolve tools were never migrated onto that seam and still computed a winner only to throw it away: `bt_evolve_memetic`'s handler called `pop.MemeticEvolve(...)`, consulted the returned `best` only for `evolution.CountNodes(best)` and `pop.BestFitness` in the JSON report, then discarded `best` itself; and `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`'s CMA-ES branch destructured `evolution.TuneTreeParameters(...)`'s tuned `*evolution.SerializableNode` return value into `_`, keeping only `bestFitness`.

**Decision:** Both call sites (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) now pass their winner to the existing `persistEvolvedWinner(deps, baseTreeID, winner, fitness, result)` helper before marshaling their response — `bt_evolve_memetic` calls it with `params.Tree`/`best`/`pop.BestFitness` against the top-level `result` map, and the CMA-ES branch calls it with `b.TreeID`/the now-named `tunedTree`/`bestFitness` against that bottleneck's report `entry` map — exactly the same seam and derived `"<baseTreeID>-evolved"` id the genetic-family tools already use, requiring no new persistence code.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Both tools now report an `evolved_tree_id`, and their winner is resolvable via `treeStore.LoadNamed`, registered in the knowledge graph with a positive `StructuralFitness`, and (for `bt_evolve_memetic`) connected back to its base tree through `DiscoverRelated`, exactly like the ADR-042 tools.
- ✅ A `cmaes`-routed and a `genetic`-routed entry in the same `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` report are no longer asymmetric in whether their winner survives the tool call — only in `algorithm` and the parameter-vs-topology fields ADR-020 already documents.
- ⚠️ `bt_evolve_qd` and `bt_evolve_island` still remain fitness-only write-backs (ADR-042's original caveat, unchanged by this).
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveMemeticPersistsEvolvedWinnerTree` and `TestBTEvolveBottlenecksCMAESPersistsEvolvedWinnerTree` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

## ADR-119: Adaptive, Track-Record-Driven Generation Budgets for the Five Benchmark-Gated Evolution Tools (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Give the five benchmark-gated evolution algorithms adaptive, track-record-driven generation budgets instead of a hardcoded default." `bt_evolve_qd`, `bt_evolve_multiobjective`, `bt_evolve_pareto`, `bt_evolve_qlearning`, and `bt_evolve_island` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) each gate their winner through `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner` (ADR-096/ADR-111/ADR-113) and each fell back to the identical hardcoded `params.Generations = 10` whenever a caller left the parameter unset — regardless of whether that tree's recent evolved winners had actually been passing or failing the gate. A tree with a string of regressions got no more search budget on its next call than a tree that had never regressed once.

**Decision (milestone 1 of 3):** Add `evolution.TrackRecord` (`internal/evolution/track_record.go`): a `Runs []TrackRecordRun` archive of bare accept/reject outcomes, `Record`/`Save`/`Load` mirroring `ExperienceBank`/`QTable`'s atomic tmp+rename-under-`acquireExperienceLock` idiom and 500-entry cap, and `RecommendedGenerations(defaultGenerations)` — over the most recent `trackRecordWindow` (5) runs, an all-accepted or cold-start history returns the default unchanged, otherwise the default scales up by `1 + rejection_rate` (`math.Round`). Wire it into `bt_evolve_qd` and `bt_evolve_multiobjective`: each loads a shared `trackRecordArchivePath(treeID)` before computing its `Generations` fallback, calls `track.Record(gateRejected)` with that call's own `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner` verdict, and saves back — regardless of accept/reject, since a rejection is exactly the signal the next call needs to see.

**Decision (milestone 2 of 3):** Apply the identical load/record/save wiring to `bt_evolve_pareto` and `bt_evolve_qlearning`, reusing the same `trackRecordArchivePath(treeID)` — deliberately one archive per base tree, not per tool, so a regression any one of the four algorithms discovers on a tree raises every other algorithm's next generation budget on that same tree.

**Decision (milestone 3 of 3):** Apply the same wiring to the fifth and last benchmark-gated tool, `bt_evolve_island`, closing the program. Additionally surface the previously-internal adaptive-budget state on all five tools' result JSON: `track_record_win_rate` (`TrackRecord.WinRate()`, the acceptance fraction over the same 5-run window, 1.0 when cold) and `track_record_recommended_generations` (the value `RecommendedGenerations` would return on the *next* call, post-record) sit alongside the existing `benchmark_gate_rejected`/`benchmark_base_success_rate`/`benchmark_winner_success_rate` fields, with non-fatal `track_record_load_error`/`track_record_save_error` on I/O failure matching every other archive's error-surfacing convention in this package.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1–3 of 3 landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ All five durable-archive `bt_evolve_*` tools now request more search budget on a tree with a recent history of benchmark-gate rejections instead of always retrying with the same fixed `generations=10`, and the effect compounds across algorithms sharing one tree rather than resetting per tool.
- ✅ The adaptive driver is observable rather than purely internal state: a caller can read `track_record_win_rate`/`track_record_recommended_generations` off any of the five tools' results without needing to inspect the archive file directly.
- ✅ An explicit `generations` request parameter still takes precedence on every tool — `RecommendedGenerations` only fills the `params.Generations <= 0` fallback path, matching the pre-existing hardcoded-10 behavior's own scope exactly.
- ⚠️ `internal/evolution/track_record.go` ships with no dedicated unit test file of its own; `TrackRecord.Record`/`RecommendedGenerations`/`WinRate`/`Save`/`Load` are exercised only indirectly through the three `cmd/bt-agent` integration tests below, unlike `ExperienceBank`/`QTable`/`MAPElitesGrid`, each of which has a package-level test file alongside its production code.
- ⚠️ The scaling formula (`1 + rejection_rate`, capped implicitly at 2× the default since `rejection_rate <= 1`) is a fixed, unconfigurable heuristic — no request parameter or `Config` field lets a caller tune the window size, the cap, or the scaling curve, mirroring the same fixed-constant caveat `ADR-102`'s crisis-mutation-budget calibration flagged and resolved for the gardener's own hardcoded doubling, but left unresolved here.
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveQDAndMultiObjectiveShareTrackRecordDrivenGenerationBudget`, `TestBTEvolveParetoAndQLearningShareTrackRecordDrivenGenerationBudget`, and `TestBTEvolveIslandSharesTrackRecordDrivenGenerationBudget` (all `cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

---

## ADR-120: `RunTask`'s Terminal Switch Preserves `goap_fusion_rate_limited` Instead of Collapsing It to a Raw Success/Failure Code (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-15):** ADR-077 (2026-07-13) taught `RunTask`'s terminal `switch` (`internal/engine/tree.go`) to special-case `bb.Outcome == "pending_approval"`, but the tree's other deliberate outcome sentinel — `goap_fusion_rate_limited`, the Claude rate-limit carryover ADR-016 introduced and ADR-025/ADR-075 built a deferred-vs-failed dispatch chain around — got no equivalent protection. The leaf that sets it (`runSuperpowersRuntimeFromExistingPlanAction` and the Claude review fallback, `internal/engine/actions_superpowers_prod.go`) deliberately returns `-1`, not `0`, so a parent `Selector` still falls through to `ScheduledAnalysisPath`; consumers that read `bb.Outcome` mid-tree (`goapFusionImplDegradedSection`, `isGoapInfraCycleFailure`'s milestone-refund check) saw the sentinel correctly, since they run as leaf/deferred logic inside `tree.Run()`, before RunTask's post-loop switch ever executes. But every consumer *outside* the tree — `internal/agent/runner.go`'s `result.Outcome = bb.Outcome` immediately after `RunTask` returns, which is exactly what `recordSchedulerAttempt` (ADR-025) and `AgentResult.Outcome` (ADR-075) read — instead saw whatever the terminal switch had already stamped `bb.Outcome` to: `case code == 1: bb.Outcome = Success` if the in-tree fallback completed, or `case code == -1: bb.Outcome = Failure` otherwise. The sentinel never survived to reach the scheduler, so the deferred-vs-failed routing ADR-025 built was silently inert for rate-limited cycles.

**Decision:** Add `case bb.Outcome == "goap_fusion_rate_limited":` as the first arm of `RunTask`'s terminal `switch`, ahead of the `code`-keyed arms — an empty case, mirroring ADR-077's `pending_approval` arm, that leaves `bb.Outcome` untouched instead of falling through to a `code`-derived Success/Failure or the `Partial` default.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `goap_fusion_rate_limited` now reliably reaches every consumer ADR-016/ADR-025/ADR-075 documented as reading it post-`RunTask` — `recordSchedulerAttempt`'s `RecordDeferred` routing, the DLQ's non-dead-letter disposition, and `AgentResult.Outcome` across the `AgentExecutor` boundary — regardless of whether the tree's raw terminal `code` ends up `1` (fallback path completed) or `-1` (no fallback, or the fallback also failed). Pinned by `TestRunTask_PreservesRateLimitCarryoverOutcome` (`internal/engine/tree_test.go`), which drives a bare single-leaf tree that sets the sentinel and returns `-1` and asserts it survives `RunTask` unmodified.
- ✅ In-tree consumers (`goapFusionImplDegradedSection`, `isGoapInfraCycleFailure`) already read the sentinel correctly mid-tick and are unaffected — the gap was specifically in what survived *past* `RunTask`'s return.
- ⚠️ How many past cycles this actually mis-routed to a false success/failure, rather than the intended deferral, is not recoverable from this fix alone — it removes the clobber going forward without auditing historical scheduler/DLQ records.

---

## ADR-121: The Self-Healing Envelope Reaches the Last Three GA Variants — Q-Learning, Memetic, and MAP-Elites (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-15):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Close the self-healing envelope gap across the remaining GA variants (Q-Learning, Memetic, MAP-Elites)." ADR-038 and ADR-051 brought `Evolve`, `EvolveWithExperience`, `EvolvePareto`, `EvolveAll`, and `NSGAIIPopulation.Evolve` onto the shared `Population.selfHealGeneration` envelope, but both ADRs' own Consequences explicitly left three variants outside it: `Population.EvolveQLearning` ran its own bare per-generation loop with no `PopulationState`/`Crisis`/`Specialists` consultation at all; `Population.MemeticEvolve` (`internal/evolution/local_search.go`) gated its crossover/mutation replacement on a hardcoded `rand.Float64() < 0.3` with the same absence of crisis/specialist logic; and `MAPElitesPopulation.EvolveMAPElites` (`internal/evolution/map_elites.go`) carried its own hand-inlined duplicate of the detect→act→resurrect→reset sequence (`mp.Crisis`, `recordCrisisReasons`, `resurrectExtinctSpecialists`, `GetEmergencyMutationRate`) driven by a MAP-Elites-grid-aware `PopulationState` (`BuildPopulationStateWithGrid`) instead of the shared method — two implementations of one envelope, with the seeded `Specialists` registries on `bt_evolve_qlearning`/`bt_evolve_memetic` sitting unconsulted.

**Decision (milestone 1 of 3):** `MemeticEvolve` now wraps its crossover/mutation replacement step in `p.selfHealGeneration(eliteCount, supervisor, reproduce)`, with `reproduce` receiving the envelope's effective `mutationRate` in place of the hardcoded `0.3` gate. The per-generation local-search refinement (`searcher.Search` on the top-`refineTopN` individuals) still runs before the envelope, unchanged — it is a per-individual quality pass, not part of the population-level breeding step the envelope wraps. Pinned by `TestMemeticEvolve_ResurrectsExtinctSpecialist` (`internal/evolution/local_search_test.go`), mirroring the collapsed-diversity/extinct-goap-archetype setup every other `selfHealGeneration` migration in this family used.

**Decision (milestone 2 of 3):** `EvolveMAPElites` deletes its hand-inlined crisis/resurrection block entirely and calls the embedded `*Population`'s `selfHealGeneration` instead, passing a `reproduce` closure that captures the MAP-Elites-specific niche-elite-then-fitness-elite preservation and diverse-parent crossover/mutation step; the grid's current niche winners (`mp.Grid.Elites()`) are captured *before* the envelope runs, since `selfHealGeneration` only touches `Population` fields, never the grid. This is a genuine behavior change in the illuminator's crisis signal, not just a refactor: the shared envelope's `DetectPopulation` call now reads plain genome-level `Population.Diversity()` instead of `MAPElitesGrid.DiversityScore()` (occupied cells ÷ `estimateTotalCells`).
