Because `estimateTotalCells` derives its denominator only from the domains/buckets actually *observed* among occupied cells, a population collapsed onto a single behavioral niche always reported the trivially maximal `DiversityScore() == 1.0` (one occupied cell over an estimated total of one) — the exact total-collapse case the ADR-031 milestone-5 illuminator crisis path was built to catch, undetected by its own signal. `TestMAPElitesPopulation_EvolveMAPElites_ResurrectsExtinctSpecialist` (`internal/evolution/map_elites_test.go`, rewritten from the ADR-031 milestone-5 diagonal-specimen fixture) demonstrates this directly: ten identical-genome, identical-descriptor individuals collapse the grid to one cell reading `DiversityScore() == 1.0` under the old grid-aware code (no crisis, no resurrection), while the same population's `Population.Diversity() == 0.1` correctly trips `diversity_collapse` under the shared envelope. `bt_evolve_qd`'s separately-reported `diversity_score`/`cell_count` fields (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) are unaffected — only the internal crisis-detection input changed.

**Decision (milestone 3 of 3):** `bt_evolve_qlearning` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go:1490`) and `bt_evolve_memetic` (`tools.go:1424`) — the two remaining evolve tools whose responses never surfaced `Population.HealthSnapshot()` — now include a `"health"` key from the shared `evolveHealthProjection(pop)` helper (ADR-037), matching `bt_evolve_genetic`/`bt_evolve_multiobjective`/`bt_evolve_pareto`/`bt_evolve_qd`. Pinned by `TestBTEvolveQLearningAndMemeticSurfacePopulationHealthSnapshot` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), which drives both tools and asserts a `health` object carrying an array `crisis_reasons`, a non-negative `resurrections`, and a positive `last_mutation_rate`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-15) — milestones 1–3 of 3 landed, closing the residual gap ADR-038 and ADR-051 both left open.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Every production `Population`-backed GA/multi-objective/island/QD variant (`Evolve`, `EvolveWithExperience`, `EvolvePareto`, `EvolveAll`, `NSGAIIPopulation.Evolve`, `EvolveQLearning`, `MemeticEvolve`, `EvolveMAPElites`) now runs through one `selfHealGeneration` implementation instead of drifting or duplicated copies, closing the gap ADR-038 and ADR-051 both flagged as open.
- ✅ `bt_evolve_qlearning` and `bt_evolve_memetic` now actually consult the `Specialists` registry `newProductionPopulation` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) already seeds for every MCP-tool population via `SeedSpecialistRegistry` — a registry that sat unconsulted by these two tools since it was first seeded (ADR-031).
- ✅ Fixes a structural blind spot in the MAP-Elites illuminator's own crisis path: the pre-ADR-121 grid-aware detector could not flag a population collapsed onto a single behavioral niche, because `MAPElitesGrid.DiversityScore()`'s denominator is estimated only from niches already observed.
- ⚠️ The illuminator's crisis signal is no longer behavior-space aware: a population that stays genome-diverse while its *illuminated* niches genuinely narrow no longer trips a MAP-Elites-specific alarm on that basis alone — though `DiversityScore()`'s self-referential estimate means the pre-ADR-121 signal was not a reliable detector of that case either.
- ⚠️ Unlike milestones 1–2, `EvolveQLearning`'s migration onto `selfHealGeneration` has no dedicated characterization test asserting crisis-reason/resurrection behavior in `internal/evolution/learning_test.go` — it is exercised only indirectly through milestone 3's `cmd/bt-agent` health-surfacing integration test, which asserts the `health` object's shape but not that a collapsing Q-learning population actually resurrects a specialist.
- Pinned by `TestMemeticEvolve_ResurrectsExtinctSpecialist` (`internal/evolution/local_search_test.go`), `TestMAPElitesPopulation_EvolveMAPElites_ResurrectsExtinctSpecialist` (`internal/evolution/map_elites_test.go`), and `TestBTEvolveQLearningAndMemeticSurfacePopulationHealthSnapshot` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

---

**Consolidation note (2026-07-16, from §5.3's annotations):** when `EvolveQLearning` joined the shared `selfHealGeneration` envelope, the per-offspring mutation stays Q-table-selected regardless of the envelope's emitted `mutationRate` — the envelope's rate still drives crisis response and health reporting; it does not override the Q-table's own action selection.

## ADR-122: `BTFusionTree`'s HITL Gate Reclassified `local_reversible`/`auto_approve`, Ending Every Unattended Cycle's Deadlock (Q3 Reliability, Milestone 1/2)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q3 Reliability — Fix the bt-fusion HITL gate misclassification that deadlocks every unattended hourly cycle." `BTFusionTree()`'s (`internal/domains/bt_fusion.go`) `ApproveFusionReportWrite` gate — guarding only the write of a durable Obsidian research report plus a build/test verification pass, no destructive or irreversible action — carried `side_effect_class: "external"` with no `auto_approve` key. Per `internal/engine/hitl_gate.go`, `sideEffectRequiresHITL` treats `"external"` (alongside `"destroy"`) as always requiring a live human regardless of `hitl.Policy.Enabled`, and with no `auto_approve` metadata the created request defaulted to `hitl.StatusPending`: `humanApprovalGateCmd.Run` set `bb.Outcome = "pending_approval"` and returned `0` (Running) every tick, so a scheduled, unattended `bt_fusion` cycle could never self-resolve the gate and stalled on it indefinitely. `GoapFusionTree()`'s own write gate, `ApproveGoapFusionApply` (`internal/domains/goap_fusion.go`), already classified the equivalent local, reversible write as `side_effect_class: "local_reversible"` with `auto_approve: true` — the same shape this gate should have had from the start.

**Decision (milestone 1 of 2):** `ApproveFusionReportWrite`'s `Metadata` now sets `"side_effect_class": "local_reversible"` and `"auto_approve": true`, matching `ApproveGoapFusionApply` exactly. `sideEffectRequiresHITL` no longer forces the gate through the mandatory-human path, and `autoApproveFromNode` stamps a freshly created request `hitl.StatusSkipped` instead of leaving it `StatusPending`, so `humanApprovalGateCmd.Run` proceeds straight to the `ApplyFusion`/`VerifyFusionBuild`/`ReportFusionStatus` children within the same tick rather than parking the cycle. Pinned by `TestBTFusionApproveGateAutoApproves` (`internal/domains/bt_fusion_test.go`), which asserts both metadata fields directly on the constructed tree.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — milestone 1 of 2 landed; a second milestone remains open under the same program (scope not yet determined by this cycle).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A scheduled/unattended `bt_fusion` cycle no longer deadlocks on `ApproveFusionReportWrite`: the gate auto-resolves in-tick, matching the non-wedging intent `goap_fusion`'s equivalent gate already had.
- ✅ The fix is metadata-only — no change to `HumanApprovalGate`'s engine semantics, `ApplyFusion`'s write behavior, or the HITL store — so the blast radius is exactly the one node's classification.
- ⚠️ `hitl.Policy.Enabled` behavior is otherwise unchanged for genuinely external/destructive gates elsewhere in the tree registry; this reclassification applies only to `ApproveFusionReportWrite`, not as a blanket policy change.

---

## ADR-123: `mcpDeps` Gains a `bbMu` Mutex, and `engine.Server` Gains a `RegisterBlackboardTool` Primitive, for the Shared-Blackboard Data Race Across `bt_run_task` and the Cross-File Tool Handlers That Touch `deps.bb` (Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability, Milestones 1, 4 & 5/5)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability — Close the mcpDeps shared-blackboard data race left unfixed by the prior guard attempt." `mcpDeps` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) holds a single `*engine.Blackboard` (`deps.bb`) that every MCP tool handler mutates directly — `engine.Blackboard` (`internal/engine/tree.go:107`) is documented as "shared state passed through the behavior tree" but carries no synchronization of its own. `bt_run_task`'s handler assigned `deps.bb.Task`/`Complexity`/`Plan`/`Result`/`Outcome`/`KgResults`/`CachedResult`, called `engine.RunTask(deps.bb, *deps.bt)`, then read `deps.bb.Outcome`/`Complexity`/`DurationMs`/`Plan` back into the response with no lock around any of it. Since the MCP server dispatches concurrent tool calls against the same `mcpDeps`, two overlapping `bt_run_task` invocations could interleave: one call's `Task` write, or the delayed read of `Result`/`Outcome` after `RunTask` returns, could be stomped by a second concurrent caller before the first caller's response was built — corrupting which task a response actually reports on (Q1) as well as constituting a live data race under `-race` (Q3).

**Decision (milestone 1 of 5):** `mcpDeps` gained a `bbMu sync.Mutex` field plus `lockBB()`/`unlockBB()` helper methods, and `bt_run_task`'s handler now calls `deps.lockBB()`/`defer deps.unlockBB()` immediately after the health check, holding the lock across the entire Task-assign → `RunTask` → response-read critical section. Pinned by `TestBTRunTaskConcurrentCallsDoNotRaceOnSharedBlackboard` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), which fires 8 concurrent `bt_run_task` calls with distinct task strings through one `mcpDeps`/`engine.Blackboard` pair and asserts each response's echoed result matches its own request; run with `-race`, the test also asserts zero data races on `deps.bb`.

**Decision (milestone 4 of 5):** `bbMu` now also guards the cross-file `deps.bb`/`*deps.bt` call sites the prior milestone's residual-gap note did not yet name: `bt_blocks_compose`'s `save:true` branch (`cmd/bt-agent/blocks_tools.go`, assigns `deps.bb.TreeStore` and rebuilds `*deps.bt`) and `bt_hitl_compose_task`'s `save:true` branch (`cmd/bt-agent/hitl_tools.go`, rebuilds `*deps.bt`) each now call `deps.lockBB()`/`deps.unlockBB()` around their mutation. `injectPersonaContext` (`cmd/bt-agent/persona_tools.go`) — which resets and repopulates `deps.bb.ChainState` and was previously safe only by the accident of being called exclusively from within `bt_run_task`'s already-locked section — now acquires `deps.bbMu` itself via `deps.lockBB()`/`defer deps.unlockBB()`, delegating its actual mutation to a new unexported `injectPersonaContextLocked`. Because `sync.Mutex` is not reentrant, `bt_run_task` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`), which already holds `bbMu` across its own critical section, was rewired to call `injectPersonaContextLocked` directly instead of the now self-locking `injectPersonaContext`, avoiding a self-deadlock. Pinned by three new regression tests in `cmd/bt-agent/blocks_tools_test.go` — `TestBTBlocksComposeSaveHoldsBBMu`, `TestBTHITLComposeTaskSaveHoldsBBMu`, `TestInjectPersonaContextHoldsBBMu` — sharing a `blockOnHeldBBMu` helper that holds `bbMu` itself (standing in for a concurrent handler such as `bt_run_task`) and asserts the target call blocks until the lock is released instead of racing straight through.

**Decision (milestone 5 of 5):** Rather than closing milestone 4's named residual gap by wiring `deps.bbMu` into `bt_delegate_to_tree` and the four `bt_use_*_tree` switchers directly, milestone 5 lands a structural primitive one layer down, in `internal/engine/mcp_server.go`: `Server` gains its own `bbMu sync.Mutex` field and a `RegisterBlackboardTool(name, description, props, required, handler)` method that wraps `RegisterTool`, running the whole handler body under `s.bbMu.Lock()`/`defer s.bbMu.Unlock()`. Unlike `mcpDeps.bbMu` — an ad hoc lock a handler author must remember to call inside their own function body, the exact failure mode that left milestone 4's five handlers unguarded — `RegisterBlackboardTool` enforces the lock at registration time, so a tool sharing a Blackboard cannot be added without it. `TestServer_Run_MixedToolConcurrentCallsDoNotRaceOnSharedBlackboard` (`internal/engine/mcp_server_test.go`) is the regression proof: it registers two distinct tools (`task_alpha`, `task_beta`) via `RegisterBlackboardTool` against one shared `*Blackboard` and tree, drives a mixed concurrent burst of both through the real `Server.Run()` stdin/stdout dispatch loop (the 5-slot semaphore + goroutine path at `mcp_server.go:148-209`, not the `Invoke()` in-process shortcut), and asserts each response's echoed result matches its own request's task — proof under `go test -race` that no concurrent call to a *different* tool sharing the Blackboard can stomp another's in-flight state, when both tools register through this primitive.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — milestones 1, 4, and 5 of 5 landed. Milestone 5 proves the structural primitive is race-free when used; it does not migrate `cmd/bt-agent`'s tool registrations onto it, so the production gap milestone 4 flagged is not closed by this program (see below).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Concurrent `bt_run_task` calls through the same `mcpDeps` no longer corrupt each other's `Task`/`Result`/`Outcome`, and `-race` reports the critical section clean.
- ✅ `bt_blocks_compose(save:true)`, `bt_hitl_compose_task(save:true)`, and `injectPersonaContext` no longer bypass `bbMu` — all three previously mutated `deps.bb`/`*deps.bt` with zero synchronization even while a concurrent `bt_run_task` held the lock across its own critical section, so no tool handler in `blocks_tools.go`, `hitl_tools.go`, or `persona_tools.go` is left unprotected.
- ✅ `engine.Server.RegisterBlackboardTool` gives any MCP server built on this package a registration-time guarantee that a tool sharing a Blackboard is synchronized, closing the "forgot to opt in" failure mode milestone 4 diagnosed — proven by `TestServer_Run_MixedToolConcurrentCallsDoNotRaceOnSharedBlackboard` under `-race`.
- ⚠️ ~~**Still open in production:** `cmd/bt-agent`'s tool registrations (`registerMCPTools`, `cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) were not changed by milestone 5. `bt_delegate_to_tree` and the tree-switching tools `bt_use_go_tree`/`bt_use_finance_tree`/`bt_use_research_tree`/`bt_use_domain_tree` still register via plain `server.RegisterTool` with no lock at all, exactly as milestone 4 left them, and still race against a concurrent `bt_run_task` or each other. Only the primitive a follow-up migration would use to close that gap now exists and is proven correct — the gap itself persists.~~ — resolved 2026-07-16 (ADR-124): all six shared-blackboard tools in `cmd/bt-agent` now register via `RegisterBlackboardTool`, and the ad hoc `mcpDeps.bbMu`/`lockBB`/`unlockBB` scaffolding this ADR introduced is removed in favor of it.
- Callers reaching for `injectPersonaContext` outside `bt_run_task` must use the plain (self-locking) name; only `bt_run_task` itself, which already holds `bbMu`, should call `injectPersonaContextLocked` directly.

---

## ADR-124: `cmd/bt-agent` Migrates All Six Shared-Blackboard Tools onto `Server.RegisterBlackboardTool`, Closing ADR-123's Residual Production Gap

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q1 Correctness / Q3 Reliability — Close the remaining unguarded `deps.bb`/`deps.bt` races in `cmd/bt-agent`'s tree-switching and delegation tools," milestones 2–4/4. ADR-123 milestone 5 added `engine.Server.RegisterBlackboardTool` (`internal/engine/mcp_server.go`) — a registration-time locking primitive — but explicitly left `cmd/bt-agent`'s own tool registrations untouched: `bt_delegate_to_tree` and the four `bt_use_*_tree` switchers still called plain `server.RegisterTool` with zero synchronization around the `*deps.bb`/`*deps.bt` they read and mutate, and the handlers that already had protection (`bt_run_task`, `bt_blocks_compose`, `bt_hitl_compose_task`, `injectPersonaContext`) relied on the ad hoc `mcpDeps.bbMu`/`lockBB()`/`unlockBB()` scaffolding — the exact "a handler author must remember to opt in" failure mode `RegisterBlackboardTool` exists to close.

**Decision:** Every `mcpDeps` handler that shares `*deps.bb`/`*deps.bt` now registers through `server.RegisterBlackboardTool` instead of `server.RegisterTool`: `bt_run_task`, `bt_use_go_tree`, `bt_use_finance_tree`, `bt_use_research_tree`, `bt_use_domain_tree`, and `bt_delegate_to_tree` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`), plus `bt_blocks_compose` (`blocks_tools.go`) and `bt_hitl_compose_task` (`hitl_tools.go`). With the whole handler body now running under the `Server`-wide `bbMu` (`internal/engine/mcp_server.go`), the per-handler `deps.lockBB()`/`deps.unlockBB()` calls the four already-guarded handlers made are redundant and removed, and `mcpDeps` itself drops its `bbMu sync.Mutex` field and `lockBB`/`unlockBB` methods (`tools.go`) entirely — one shared-blackboard lock instead of two independently-maintained ones. `injectPersonaContext` (`persona_tools.go`), the self-locking wrapper `bt_run_task` previously had to route around via `injectPersonaContextLocked` to avoid deadlocking on the non-reentrant `mcpDeps.bbMu`, is deleted outright; only `injectPersonaContextLocked` remains, now documented as safe to call only from a handler already registered via `RegisterBlackboardTool`.

**Testing:** `TestSixSharedBlackboardToolsUseRegisterBlackboardTool` and `TestMcpDepsHasNoAdHocBlackboardMutexScaffolding` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`) pin the two structural invariants directly by grepping `tools.go`'s source: all six tool names must appear behind `server.RegisterBlackboardTool(`, and `bbMu`/`lockBB`/`unlockBB` must not appear at all. `TestBTUseResearchTreeHoldsServerLock`, `TestBTUseDomainTreeHoldsServerLock`, and `TestBTDelegateToTreeHoldsServerLock` (`tools_test.go`), plus the renamed `TestBTBlocksComposeSaveHoldsServerLock`/`TestBTHITLComposeTaskSaveHoldsServerLock` (`blocks_tools_test.go`), exercise the runtime behavior via a shared `blockOnHeldServerLock` helper (renamed from `blockOnHeldBBMu`): it now registers a throwaway blocking tool through `RegisterBlackboardTool` to occupy the `Server`-wide mutex, instead of calling `deps.lockBB()` directly, and asserts each target tool's invocation blocks until that mutex is released. `TestRegisterMCPToolsCommentMatchesActualToolCount` now sums `server.RegisterBlackboardTool(` call sites alongside `server.RegisterTool(` ones so the tool-count invariant it pins still holds. The standalone `TestInjectPersonaContextHoldsBBMu` is removed along with the function it tested.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — milestones 2–4 of 4 landed, closing the program and the "Still open in production" gap ADR-123 flagged.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `bt_delegate_to_tree` and all four `bt_use_*_tree` switchers — unguarded even after ADR-123 milestone 4 — now serialize on the same `Server`-wide lock as `bt_run_task`, closing the last unguarded `*deps.bb`/`*deps.bt` mutation sites in `cmd/bt-agent`.
- ✅ `mcpDeps` no longer carries its own `bbMu`; there is exactly one shared-blackboard lock (`engine.Server.bbMu`, enforced at registration time) instead of the two independently-maintained mechanisms ADR-123 left in place.
- ✅ `injectPersonaContext`'s deadlock-avoidance split (self-locking wrapper vs. a `*Locked` body callers had to remember to use inside an already-locked section) is gone — only the `*Locked` body remains, safe by construction because every caller is now a `RegisterBlackboardTool` handler.
- No behavior change to any tool's response shape or business logic — this program is synchronization-only.

---

## ADR-125: The ExperienceBank Write Side Now Threads Failing-Task Context, Closing ADR-109's Warm-Start Loop (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Thread failing-task context through the ExperienceBank write side so gardener mutations close the ADR-109 warm-start loop." ADR-109 conditioned `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' genetic-path *retrieval* on the bottleneck's own `LastFailureTask`, and gave `ExperienceBank.AddFromMutation` an optional `failureContext` parameter for the *write* side — but flagged that no production recorder actually supplied it: `Population.mutateAndRecord` (driven by `EvolveWithExperienceContext`) and both `AddFromMutation` call sites in `internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go` still recorded with no failure context, so every entry the live GA and 24/7 gardener loops wrote carried no `failing_task=` text, and `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' query-conditioned retrieval could only re-rank on `TreeType`/`MutationOp`/`TargetNode`/`Strategy`/`Summary`/`QualityScore` — the same fields `RetrieveByTreeType` already scored on — not on genuine failing-task semantic overlap.

**Decision:** **Milestone 1:** `Population.EvolveWithExperienceContext`'s `query` parameter (`internal/evolution/learning.go`) is threaded through `mutateAndRecord` into its `bank.AddFromMutation` call, replacing the bare trailing `nil` with `query` as the `failureContext` argument — so a fitness-improving mutation recorded during a non-empty-query run now carries `failing_task=<query>` in its `ExperienceEntry.Context`. **Milestone 2:** A new `lastFailureTask(records []evolution.Record) string` helper (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`) returns the `Task` text of the most recent record (by `Timestamp`) whose `Outcome == evolution.Failure`, or `""` when none failed; its result is passed as the `failureContext` argument to both `AddFromMutation` calls inside `evolveTreeV2` (the greedy per-candidate ACCEPT path and the deep-search apply path), replacing their bare trailing `nil`s. **Milestone 3:** `biasCandidatesWithExperience` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`) gains a `query string` parameter — the same milestone-2 `lastFailureTask` value, threaded in from `evolveTreeV2`'s call site — and conditions retrieval on it: a non-empty query calls `bank.Retrieve(query, experienceBiasTopK)`, the tree-type-agnostic Jaccard-similarity search ADR-109 already gave `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`, so an entry recorded against a *different* tree's failing task can still warm-start this tree's candidate ordering; an empty query falls back to today's `evolution.RetrieveExperienceHints` tree-type-only path unchanged.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — all 3 milestones landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Closes the gap ADR-109 flagged in its own Consequences: the GA (`mutateAndRecord`) and gardener (`evolveTreeV2`, both call sites) recorders now supply genuine failing-task context, so `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' query-conditioned retrieval (ADR-109) and the gardener's own `biasCandidatesWithExperience` retrieval can both match on real failing-task semantic overlap instead of only pre-existing tree-type/operator fields.
- ✅ The gardener's candidate biasing is no longer scoped solely to its own tree's type: a mutation strategy proven against one tree's failing task can warm-start a different tree's candidate ordering when their failing tasks are semantically similar, mirroring the cross-tree-type retrieval ADR-109 gave the daemon's bottleneck tool.
- ✅ Both threading points are behavior-preserving on the empty-query/no-failure path: `EvolveWithExperienceContext`'s existing empty-query callers and a gardener cycle with no prior failing reflection record see the identical historical `RetrieveExperienceHints`/tree-type-scoped behavior.
- Pinned by a new `TestEvolveWithExperienceContext_RecordsFailingTaskInContext` (`internal/evolution/learning_test.go`, milestone 1), `TestEvolveTreeV2_RecordsFailingTaskContext` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`, milestone 2), and `TestBiasCandidatesWithExperience_QueryPathSurfacesOffTreeTypeEntry` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2_test.go`, milestone 3), which seeds an off-tree-type entry that only the non-empty-query path can surface and asserts the empty-query fallback leaves it untouched.

---

## ADR-126: `/api/trees` Surfaces Fitness and Evolution Lineage, Closing Milestones 1–2/4 of the Dashboard's Dead Blind Spot (Q1 Correctness / Q2 Evolvability)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q1 Correctness / Q2 Evolvability — Surface knowledge-graph fitness and evolution lineage from the dashboard's dead `/api/trees` blind spot." `RegisterEvolved` (ADR-042) has written `evolved_from` edges and maintained `StructuralFitness`/`EvolvedCount` since 2026-07-13, and `DiscoverRelated` has walked those edges since even earlier — but `handleTrees` (`GET /api/trees`, `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`) returned only `id`/`name`/`category`/`node_count`, so none of that knowledge-graph state — fitness, structural fitness, run count, evolved count, last outcome, or lineage — ever reached the dashboard. The Evolution tab's "Algorithms Active" panel and `best_fitness` stat were sourced only from the disconnected `gardener-metrics.json` aggregate (§8.11 ADR-032), never from live `KnowledgeGraph` state.

**Decision:** **Milestone 1:** A new typed `KnowledgeGraph.EvolutionLineage(treeID string) (baseID string, evolvedIDs []string, ok bool)` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`, §8.5) resolves the `evolved_from`-edge lineage for a tree in either direction — base→descendants or descendant→base-plus-siblings — returning `ok=false` when none exists. **Milestone 2:** `handleTrees` adds `fitness`, `structural_fitness`, `run_count`, `evolved_count`, `last_outcome`, and (when `EvolutionLineage` reports one) a `lineage: {base_id, evolved_ids}` object to each `/api/trees` entry; `handleMetricsLive` separately builds a `[]dashboard.TreeSnapshot` per tree and passes it to a `Collect` now widened to a third parameter, which stamps a new `Metrics.TopWinners` field via `rankTopWinners`'s descending-`StructuralFitness` sort (§5.4, §8.11).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — milestones 1–2/4 landed; **milestone 3/4 (Evolution-tab UI) landed only its `metrics.go`/`TopWinners` backend half** — `cmd/bt-dashboard/static/js/tabs/evolution.js` was not modified, so the "Algorithms Active" panel and its `gardener.best_fitness` stat still render exactly as before ADR-032; nothing in the UI reads `TopWinners` or the new `/api/trees` fields yet. ~~Milestone 4/4 is not yet started.~~ — resolved 2026-07-16 (ADR-127): `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` now consults `EvolutionLineage` before re-evolving a tree with a fitter, non-regressing evolved descendant. Milestone 3/4's UI-wiring gap above is untouched by that fix and remains separately open.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `evolved_from` edges and `StructuralFitness`/`EvolvedCount` bookkeeping that `RegisterEvolved` has maintained since ADR-042 finally have a query API (`EvolutionLineage`) and an HTTP consumer (`/api/trees`) — the data was always correct, just unreachable outside `DiscoverRelated`'s fuzzy discovery match and direct KG inspection.
- ✅ `/api/trees` responses are additive and backward-compatible: existing `id`/`name`/`category`/`node_count` fields are unchanged, `lineage` is omitted (not null) for a tree with no evolution history.
- ⚠️ The Evolution tab is not yet live: `Metrics.TopWinners` and the enriched `/api/trees` payload have no browser-side consumer, so an operator looking at the dashboard today still sees the same static panel and `gardener-metrics.json`-sourced stat this program set out to replace. The backend/frontend split means this ADR should not be read as "the Evolution tab now reflects KnowledgeGraph state" — only its data source does.
- Pinned by `TestEvolutionLineage_BaseToEvolved`, `TestEvolutionLineage_EvolvedToBase`, `TestEvolutionLineage_NotFound` (`internal/knowledge/graph_discover_test.go`, milestone 1), `TestHandleTrees_IncludesFitnessAndLineage` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`, milestone 2), and `TestCollect_SurfacesTopEvolvedWinnersFromTrees` (`internal/dashboard/metrics_test.go`, milestone 2).

---

## ADR-127: `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` Consults `EvolutionLineage` Before Re-Evolving a Tree With a Fitter, Non-Regressing Evolved Descendant, Closing Milestone 4/4 of ADR-126's Program (Q1 Correctness / Q2 Evolvability)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Milestone 4/4 of the "Q1 Correctness / Q2 Evolvability — Surface knowledge-graph fitness and evolution lineage from the dashboard's dead `/api/trees` blind spot" program ADR-126 began. `RegisterEvolved`'s own doc comment (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) has described a benefit of the `evolved_from` edge it writes since ADR-042 — so fitness-aware discovery and the gardener can find the bred winner on the next run — that no code path actually acted on: `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' per-bottleneck loop (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) unconditionally spent a fresh CMA-ES or genetic-evolution budget on every detected bottleneck, even one whose earlier run had already produced and registered a fitter evolved descendant via that exact bookkeeping.

**Decision:** `KnowledgeGraph.EvolutionLineageBestFitness(treeID string) (fitness float64, ok bool)` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) resolves `treeID`'s lineage through the existing `EvolutionLineage` (ADR-126) and returns the highest `StructuralFitness` among `treeID`'s own evolved descendants; `ok=false` when `treeID` has none of its own (no lineage at all, or `treeID` is itself the evolved side of some other base — querying a descendant's siblings is deliberately out of scope). A new `lineageSkipsReEvolution(kg, treeID, baseFitness)` helper (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) combines that with the tree's shared `TrackRecord` archive at `trackRecordArchivePath(treeID)` — the same per-tree archive `bt_evolve_qd`/`bt_evolve_multiobjective`/`bt_evolve_pareto`/`bt_evolve_qlearning`/`bt_evolve_island` already record benchmark-gate accept/reject outcomes to (ADR-119): it reports true only when a descendant's fitness beats `baseFitness` (the bottleneck's runtime `SuccessRate`) *and* `track.WinRate() >= 0.5` — a recent history of more benchmark-gate rejections than acceptances means the stored evolved winner may no longer be trustworthy, so re-evolution proceeds instead of deferring to a possibly-stale lineage. `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' per-bottleneck loop calls this immediately after the existing baseTree-resolution check and before either the CMA-ES or genetic branch runs; a skipped tree is appended to a new `lineage_skipped` result list (mirroring the pre-existing `skipped` list for unresolvable trees) instead of silently dropping out of `report`, and contributes no entry to the `algorithms` tally.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — milestone 4/4, closing the program ADR-126 began.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Closes the loop `RegisterEvolved`'s doc comment has described since ADR-042: a bottleneck tree with an already-fitter, non-regressing evolved descendant no longer burns another evolution budget on itself every time `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` runs.
- ✅ The gate is `TrackRecord`-aware, not fitness-only: a tree whose stored evolved winner has recently been failing `benchmarkGateEvolvedWinner` re-evolves anyway rather than deferring forever to a fitness number live benchmark runs no longer back.
- ✅ Skipped bottlenecks stay observable via the new `lineage_skipped` list, distinct from the pre-existing `skipped` (unresolvable-tree) list.
- ⚠️ `EvolutionLineageBestFitness` only resolves `treeID` as a lineage *base*; a bottleneck that is itself an evolved descendant of some other tree is not covered by this check, matching `EvolutionLineage`'s own base→descendants query shape.
- ⚠️ The `TrackRecord` archive this reads is written by the five benchmark-gated evolve tools (ADR-119), not by `bt_evolve_bottlenecks` itself — its `WinRate()` gate here reflects those other tools' history on the same tree, not any history of its own.
- ⚠️ This closes milestone 4/4 as scoped (a re-evolution-gating consumer of `EvolutionLineage`); it does not touch milestone 3/4's still-open gap (ADR-126): the Evolution-tab UI still has no browser-side consumer for `TopWinners` or `/api/trees`'s new fields.
- Pinned by `TestBTEvolveBottlenecksSkipsTreeWithFitterNonRegressingEvolvedDescendant` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`).

---

## ADR-128: `ACPClient.GenerateCtx` Guards Its `cmd.Stderr` Buffer With a Mutex, Closing a Data Race Between the Subprocess's Copy Goroutine and the Timeout/Scan-Error Read Path (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-16):** NotebookLM research goal 1: fix the `internal/llm/acp.go` stderr data race in `ACPClient.GenerateCtx`. `GenerateCtx` set `cmd.Stderr` to a plain `*bytes.Buffer`. Because that target is not an `*os.File`, Go's `os/exec` package starts its own goroutine that continuously copies the subprocess's stderr pipe into the buffer for as long as the subprocess is alive — a detail internal to `exec.Cmd`, not visible from `GenerateCtx`'s own code. Meanwhile `GenerateCtx`'s `request` closure reads `stderr.String()` on its `ctx.Done()`/`scanErr` branches to enrich a timeout or failure error, with no synchronization against that background copy goroutine — a live `bytes.Buffer.Write`/`bytes.Buffer.String` data race whenever the context deadline or a scan error fires while the subprocess is still writing to stderr, exactly the case of a hung or crash-looping ACP subprocess the surrounding call path already hardens against (ADR-053/ADR-054).

**Decision:** A new `syncBuffer` type (`internal/llm/acp.go`) wraps a `bytes.Buffer` with a `sync.Mutex`, exposing `Write` and `String` methods that both take the lock; `cmd.Stderr = &stderr` now holds a `*syncBuffer` in place of the bare `*bytes.Buffer`. No other logic in `GenerateCtx` changes — the same `ctx.Done()`/`scanErr` branches call `stderr.String()` exactly as before, now synchronized against `os/exec`'s copy goroutine instead of racing it.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `GenerateCtx`'s stderr-enriched timeout/scan-error path is race-free under `-race` even when the subprocess is still alive and actively writing to stderr at the moment the deadline fires.
- ✅ No behavior change for callers: `stderr.String()` still returns whatever content has been captured so far, only now safely.
- Pinned by `TestACPClientGenerateCtx_StderrBufferRace` (`internal/llm/acp_test.go`), which drives a helper subprocess that floods stderr continuously and never responds on stdout, forcing the context deadline to fire while the copy goroutine is still writing.

## ADR-129: `setGoapStateDurable` Makes GOAP Fusion Charge Stamps Durable, and `clearSuperpowersPlanState` Retires Them on Cycle Completion (Q3 Reliability, Milestones 1–2/2)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q3 Reliability — Persist GOAP fusion research-goal/milestone charge stamps durably so resumed cron ticks charge and refund correctly." `PrioritizeGoapGoals` (`internal/engine/actions_goap_fusion.go`) stamps four keys every cycle it queues a program milestone or a research goal — `program_milestone_charged`, `program_milestone`, `research_goal_charged`, `research_goal_charged_text` — via the plain `setGoapState`, which writes ChainState only. Per §6.4/ADR-016's already-established durable pattern, ChainState dies with the run whenever a cron tick builds a fresh `Blackboard` (`RunOnce`), so these stamps had no memory across such a boundary, unlike the saved-plan path (`loadSuperpowersPlanState`/`saveSuperpowersPlanState`) and the Claude backoff deadline, both of which already fall back to the agent-scope blackboard. Separately, `clearSuperpowersPlanState` — called from every site that retires a completed or abandoned Superpowers cycle (`actions_superpowers_prod.go:804,917,1066`) — wiped only the two plan-resume keys, never the four charge stamps, so a stamp from a just-finished cycle could survive into a later, unrelated cycle and have `chargeGoapResearchGoalFailure`/`refundGoapMilestoneAttemptForInfraFailure` charge or refund the wrong goal or milestone.

**Decision:** **Milestone 1:** a new `setGoapStateDurable(bb, key, value)` calls `setGoapState` and then, when `bb.BB`/`bb.BB.AgentName` are set, also writes `"goap_fusion_"+key` into the agent-scope blackboard (`blackboard.Scope{Kind: ScopeAgent, ID: AgentName}`) — the same shape `saveSuperpowersPlanState` already uses. `PrioritizeGoapGoals`'s four stamp sites (`actions_goap_fusion.go:341,361,381,385`) now call it instead of `setGoapState`. **Milestone 2:** `clearSuperpowersPlanState` (`actions_goap_fusion.go:781`) now iterates a six-key list — `superpowers_plan_path`, `superpowers_active_plan`, `program_milestone_charged`, `program_milestone`, `research_goal_charged`, `research_goal_charged_text` — deleting each from both ChainState and the agent-scope store, in place of the two hardcoded deletes it had.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ The four charge stamps now survive a ChainState-only death: any reader that consults the agent-scope blackboard directly (not yet any production reader — see below) can recover which milestone or research goal a cycle charged even after the originating `Blackboard` is gone.
- ✅ `clearSuperpowersPlanState` can no longer leak a completed/abandoned cycle's charge stamp into a later cycle's failure handler; all three of its call sites get this for free with no change to their own code.
- ~~⚠️ The failure/refund readers themselves — `chargeGoapResearchGoalFailure` (`actions_goap_fusion_goal_budget.go:60`) and the refund path's `goapChargedMilestoneRef` (`actions_goap_fusion_refund.go:122`) — still read the stamp from `bb.ChainState` only, with no agent-scope fallback. A tick that resumes a saved plan straight into `Implement` (§6.4's Preflight "resume saved plan" branch) never re-runs `PrioritizeGoapGoals`, so its fresh, empty ChainState still has nothing for these readers to find on a genuine failure — the stamp is durable on disk, but not yet read back. The program's own title ("so resumed cron ticks charge and refund correctly") is therefore not yet fully realized; a follow-up must add the agent-scope read-back to close it.~~ — resolved 2026-07-18, see ADR-160.
- Pinned by `TestPrioritizeGoapGoals_StampsResearchGoalDurably` (`actions_goap_fusion_goal_budget_test.go`), which asserts the agent-scope store holds the research-goal stamp after `PrioritizeGoapGoals` runs, and `TestClearSuperpowersPlanState_WipesDurableChargeStamps` (`actions_goap_fusion_test.go`), which asserts all four durable keys are gone (from both ChainState and the agent-scope store) after `clearSuperpowersPlanState` runs.

## ADR-130: `IsKnownChainKind` Closes the ChainAction chain_type / Leaf-Children Validation Gap at Authoring Time (Q1 Correctness, Complete)

**Context (2026-07-16):** Program "Q1 Correctness — Close the ChainAction pre-execution validation gap so a mistyped chain_type or nested children fails at authoring time, not at LLM-call runtime." Two related authoring-time holes existed for `ChainAction` nodes. First, `parseChainConfig` reads `ChainConfig.ChainType` from the segment before `:` in a node's `Name` (§8.2), but neither validation entry point (`validateNode`/`ValidateTree`, `walkValidate`/`ValidateTreeFull`) checked it against the declared `ChainKind` constants — a mistyped or stale `chain_type` produced no signal until the node ticked and `buildChainActionFn`'s dispatch `switch` (`internal/engine/chains.go`) fell through to its `default` case, setting `bb.Outcome = "chain_failed"`. Second, `ChainAction` was missing from the shared `leafNodeTypes` set (§8.13) even though `buildNode`'s `case "ChainAction":` (`tree.go:322-325`) builds a childless `BuildChainAction` leaf and never looks at `node.Children` — a `ChainAction` with declared children silently dropped its subtree at build time, the same gap class §8.13 had already closed for `Action`/`Condition`/`AlwaysSucceed`.

**Decision:** **Milestone 1:** `IsKnownChainKind(kind string) bool` (`internal/engine/chains.go`), backed by a package-level `knownChainKinds` map covering all 11 declared `ChainKind` constants (`llm_call` through `tool_action`), reports whether a string matches one exactly. **Milestone 2:** `validateNode`'s new `case "ChainAction":` (`internal/engine/validate.go`) calls `parseChainConfig` and appends `<name>: unknown chain_type %q` to `ValidateTree`'s messages when `IsKnownChainKind` reports false. **Milestone 3:** `"ChainAction": true` was added to `leafNodeTypes` (`internal/engine/verifier.go`), so the pre-existing shared-map mechanism (§8.13) now also rejects a `ChainAction` node that declares `Children`, via both `walkValidate` (`ValidateTreeFull`) and `validateNode` (`ValidateTree`). **Milestone 4:** `walkValidate`'s type switch (`internal/engine/verifier.go`) gained its own `case "ChainAction":`, parsing the node's `ChainConfig` via `parseChainConfig` and appending the same `<name>: unknown chain_type %q` message to `ValidateTreeFull`'s `info.Errors` when `IsKnownChainKind` reports false — closing the gap milestone 2 left in the deep validator.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-16) — all 4 milestones landed.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A mistyped or unsupported `chain_type` now fails `ValidateTree` at authoring time with the offending value named in the message, instead of only surfacing as a `chain_failed` outcome the first time the node ticks.
- ✅ `ChainAction` now shares §8.13's leaf-children invariant: a `ChainAction` declaring `Children` fails both `ValidateTreeFull` and `ValidateTree` instead of silently building into a tree that drops the subtree.
- ✅ The `chain_type` check is now independently enforced by both validation entry points: `validateNode`/`ValidateTree` (milestone 2) and `walkValidate`/`ValidateTreeFull` (milestone 4) each append their own `unknown chain_type %q` error, so a caller that validates exclusively through either path still catches a mistyped `chain_type` at authoring time.
- Pinned by `TestIsKnownChainKind` (`internal/engine/chains_test.go`), `TestValidateTree_ChainAction_UnknownChainType`, `TestValidateTree_ChainAction_KnownChainType`, `TestValidateTree_ChainAction_MixedTree` (`internal/engine/validate_test.go`), and `TestVerifierFull_ChainActionRejectsChildren`, `TestVerifierFull_ChainActionUnknownChainType`, `TestVerifierFull_ChainActionKnownChainType` (`internal/engine/verifier_test.go`).

## ADR-131: Composable Behavior-Tree Building Blocks (formerly docs/adr ADR-008)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-04)

**Context:** The BT Agent Platform grew from monolithic domain trees toward reusable, evolvable units. Agents need consistent pre-gates, tool execution, quality checks, and human oversight without duplicating large JSON trees.

**Decision:**

1. **Building blocks** live in `internal/blocks` with stable IDs (`core:pre_gate`, `core:quality_gate`, …).
2. **Composition** uses `SubTreeRef` nodes expanded at build time via `blocks.Expand` registered on `engine.RegisterTreeExpander`.
3. **Domain trees** remain for specialized routing; composed presets (`agentic`, `full`, `hitl`) provide default task pipelines.
4. **HITL** gates (`HumanApprovalGate`, tiered/post-review blocks) fail closed when approval is pending.
5. **Evolution** may mutate only `Mutable` custom blocks; `core:*` builtins are frozen unless promoted to `custom:*_vN`.
6. **Fitness** is recorded per `block_id` metadata (`bt_block_fitness_score`) for gardener ordering.
7. **Ops blocks** (`trace_checkpoint`, `audit_log`, `dlq_escalate`) integrate tracing, JSONL audit, and DLQ without MCP-only paths.

**Consequences:**

- Positive: Smaller diffs, shared reliability wrappers, MCP `bt_blocks_*` tooling, dashboard HITL API.
- Negative: Expand step required before `BuildTree`; unknown `SubTreeRef` fails at runtime if not expanded.
- Migration: Domain trees can adopt composed presets incrementally; expert archetypes reference block IDs.

**References:** `.hermes/plans/2026-06-04-agentic-blocks-master-roadmap.md`; Phases 0–5 implementation plans under `.hermes/plans/`.

## ADR-132: Scoped Blackboard for Context Offloading (formerly docs/adr ADR-009)

**Status:** Accepted (2026-06-14)

**Context:** Agent runs accumulated context in three places with no shared model: (1) prompt stuffing — `injectMemoryContext()` appended full memory blocks and prior run outputs to every task string; (2) the ephemeral engine blackboard — `engine.Blackboard` held `Task`, `Plan`, `Result`, and unstructured `ChainState` for a single tree tick loop only; (3) pipeline string passing — workflow steps exchanged outputs via `wfState.prev` template expansion, not a queryable store. Agents could not read/write structured context during ReAct loops, operators had no MCP access to run/session state, and large histories inflated prompts.

**Decision:** Introduce `internal/blackboard/` with a **Manager** partitioning key-value **Entries** by scope:

| Scope | Lifetime | Persistence |
|-------|----------|-------------|
| `run` | Single agent execution | In-memory only |
| `session` | Workflow/pipeline run ID | JSON under `${AGENT_HOME}/blackboard/session/` |
| `agent` | Agent name | JSON under `${AGENT_HOME}/blackboard/agent/` |

Each `RunOnce` attaches a `blackboard.Handle` to `engine.Blackboard` and registers ReAct tools: `bb_read`, `bb_write`, `bb_list`, plus `bb_session_*` when a pipeline session ID is present. Memory/history injection **seeds run-scoped keys** (`memory/*`, `history/runs`) and leaves a short `bb_read` hint in the task instead of full payloads. The workflow orchestrator promotes step outputs to session keys (`steps/{id}/output`, `prev/output`, `input`). Chain templates support `{{.BB.run_id}}`, `{{.BB.session_id}}`, `{{.BB.agent}}`, and `{{.BB.<key>}}` (run scope, summary when large). MCP exposes `bt_bb_read`, `bt_bb_write`, `bt_bb_list`, `bt_bb_delete` for operator and external agent access.

**Consequences:**

- **Positive**: Large context moves off-prompt; agents fetch on demand via tools or templates.
- **Positive**: Pipeline steps share session-scoped state without copying megabyte strings between YAML templates.
- **Positive**: Aligns with ADR-003 file persistence — no new database.
- **Negative**: Run-scoped data is lost after execution unless copied to session/agent scope explicitly.
- **Negative**: Template `{{.BB.key}}` returns summaries for large values; full payload requires `bb_read`.
- **Negative**: Agent-scoped persistence is available but not yet auto-populated from runs (manual/MCP only).

**Related:** ADR-003 (file persistence), ADR-004 (agent platform); operator guide: [docs/agents.md](../agents.md) — Blackboard section.

## ADR-133: Personalized Self-Evolving Agents (formerly docs/adr ADR-010)

**Status:** Accepted — implemented (Phases 0–6 core landed 2026-07-08; see the plan for per-phase status)

**Context:** The platform evolves pre-authored trees well (gardener v2 with quality gates), but it cannot *create* trees from user intent: generated trees are not resolvable by `domains.ResolveTreeID`, crossover ignores parent structure, GOAP plans are transient per-run state, and no per-user identity exists (memory, reflections, and the experience bank are agent- or globally-scoped). The goal is an agent that observes its user, derives goals, compiles GOAP plans into persistent behavior trees, and evolves them from that user's feedback — closing the loop `observe → goal → plan → tree → run → reflect → evolve`. Full roadmap: [docs/plans/2026-07-08-personalized-self-evolving-agents.md](../plans/2026-07-08-personalized-self-evolving-agents.md).

**Decision:**

1. **Executable-by-construction** — every generated tree must be resolvable (dynamic `ResolveTreeID` fallback hook loading `tree-<id>.json`), validated (`ValidateTreeFull`), benchmark-smoke-tested (`benchmark.QuickValidate`), and KG-registered before it exists.
2. **Persona layer** (`internal/persona`) — per-user profile, interaction log, and habit mining; all personalization state under `~/.go-bt-evolve/users/<user>/` with ADR-003 atomic writes.
3. **Goal Factory** (in `internal/goap`) — LLM structured extraction of `goap.Goal` grounded in a world-state vocabulary registry; recurring patterns become automation goals; the existing `GoalQueue` is activated per user.
4. **Plan→BT compiler / Tree Factory v2** — `goap.CompilePlanToTree` turns plans into persistent trees (precondition guards → registered actions or ChainActions → effect writes) inside the standard PreGate/Reflect scaffold, keeping a dynamic replan fallback branch; crossover splices real parent `SerializableNode` JSON.
5. **HITL automation policy** — auto-compiled automations enter the existing HITL queue; only on approval is an agent YAML with schedule written. Pattern threshold (≥3 occurrences) and a per-user cap on active auto-created agents prevent spam.
6. **Feedback-as-fitness** — a `user_satisfaction` dimension in `MultiFitness` fed by explicit (`bt_feedback`) and implicit signals; per-user gardener registries and experience banks evolve personal trees under the existing quality-gate, snapshot, and rollback rails.
7. **Reuse over rebuild** — activate the idle `goap.Agent`, `GoalQueue`, `BlackboardBridge`, `Goal.Deadline`, `HumanApprovalGate`, and `ExperienceBank`; unify `engine.PlannerNode` onto the `internal/goap` A* planner instead of maintaining two implementations.

Phase 6 consolidation: `engine.PlannerNode` now delegates to the `internal/goap` A* planner (single search implementation); the gardener v1 `RunCycle`/`evolveTree` pipeline, unused `Config.TT`, and `factory.SkillSpec` were removed, with the v1-only rails (evidence gate, bloat cap, crisis detection) ported into `evolveTreeV2`.

**Consequences:**

- ✅ The agent grows with its user: recurring work becomes approved, scheduled, evolvable automations
- ✅ Closes the creation↔execution disconnect (risk R9 at acceptance; renumbered R14 in [§11](11-risks-debt.md)) — generated trees actually run
- ✅ No new persistence technology: ADR-003 file layout extends to per-user workspaces
- ✅ Q3 reliability rails (quality gate, rollback, circuit breakers, budgets) apply to personal trees unchanged
- ⚠️ LLM goal extraction can produce unplannable goals — mitigated by world-state grounding + `ValidatePlan` with a capped repair loop
- ⚠️ Per-user gardener scans add cycle load — mitigated by round-robin budgets and the existing evidence gate
- ⚠️ New trees start without reflections — seeded with compile-time validation and first supervised runs so the evidence gate does not freeze them

## ADR-134: `RebuildTarget.Unit` Closes the Deploy-Drift Restart-Handoff Gap: Swapped Sibling Units Restart Too, and `bt-dashboard`/the MCP Binary Join Fleet-Wide Adoption (Q3 Reliability, Milestones 1–3/3)

**Context (2026-07-17):** Program "Q3 Reliability — Close the deploy-drift restart-handoff gap: adopted binaries must restart their services." The restart-handoff mechanism itself (`driftRestartFn`, smoke-test-then-restart, `.previous` rollback on a failed smoke test, `AutoRestartEnabled`/`BT_AUTO_RESTART_ON_DRIFT`) had already landed (commit 4b5e413) but only ever restarted the daemon's *own* unit — `DriftWatchOnce` (`internal/agent/deploy_drift.go`) called `driftRestartFn(cfg.Binary)` once, after a successful rebuild+swap, with no notion that `RebuildBinaries` can swap several sibling binaries in one pass. Live case 2026-07-16 23:46: `bt-agent`'s fleet-wide rebuild swapped `bin/bt-gardener` to `fd0746d`, but the running gardener process — a separate systemd unit — kept executing `ce20198` until a human ran `systemctl restart` by hand. Separately, `DefaultRebuildTargets` deliberately excluded `bt-dashboard` ("callers pass the set they own"), so the daemon's fleet-wide sweep rebuilt nothing for it, and `bt-dashboard`'s own `DashboardRebuildTargets` wrote its rebuilt binary to the repo root while the production unit's 2026-07-15 drop-in `ExecStart` override actually runs it from `bin/` — a "successful" self-rebuild that never landed where the running unit executed from. The MCP server binary `bin/bt-agent`, which `.mcp.json` spawns fresh per cycle-session, was nobody's rebuild target at all and needed two manual rebuilds on 2026-07-16 (30+ commits stale at b2a318a).

**Decision:** **Milestone 1 (sibling-unit restart):** `RebuildTarget` (`internal/agent/rebuild.go`) gains a `Unit string` field — the owning systemd unit name without `.service`, empty for unit-less targets (e.g. `bt-agent-cli`, a CLI tool with no long-running service). `DriftWatchOnce`, immediately after a successful rebuild+smoke-test and its in-flight re-check but before restarting itself, loops `cfg.Targets` and calls `driftRestartFn(t.Unit)` for every swapped target with a non-empty `Unit` other than `cfg.Binary` itself — restarting siblings first, the daemon's own unit last (preserving prior ordering/behavior). The whole loop is gated on the same `AutoRestart`/`AutoRestartEnabled()` flag as the pre-existing self-restart; a sibling restart failure is logged (`slog.Error`) but does not block adopting the fix on the daemon's own unit. **Milestone 2 (`bt-dashboard` fleet adoption):** `DefaultRebuildTargets` now includes `bt-dashboard` (`OutPath` under `bin/`, `Unit: "bt-dashboard"`), matching the corrected production `ExecStart` path; `DashboardRebuildTargets` becomes a direct alias of `DefaultRebuildTargets` rather than appending a second, differently-pathed `bt-dashboard` entry — kept as its own name only so `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`'s watcher wiring stays self-documenting. `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go` additionally wires `AutoRestart: agent.AutoRestartEnabled()` into its `DriftWatchConfig`, mirroring `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`; without it a correctly-pathed self-rebuild would still only log "restart to adopt" and never actually restart the unit. **Milestone 3 (MCP binary):** a `bt-agent-mcp` target (`Pkg: "./cmd/bt-agent"`, `OutPath: bin/bt-agent`) joins `DefaultRebuildTargets`, left unit-less (`Unit: ""`) since MCP client sessions spawn a fresh process per session rather than running under a long-lived systemd unit — milestone 1's sibling-restart loop skips it by the same `t.Unit == ""` guard that already skips `bt-agent-cli`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-17)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A fleet-wide rebuild that swaps multiple sibling binaries in one pass (e.g. `bt-agent`'s default targets rebuilding `bt-gardener`/`bt-dashboard`/the MCP binary alongside itself) now restarts every swapped unit-owning sibling, not just the daemon's own — closing the exact gap the 2026-07-16 23:46 live incident exposed. Pinned by `TestDriftWatchOnceRestartsSwappedSiblingUnits` (`internal/agent/deploy_drift_restart_test.go`), which asserts restart order (siblings first, self last) and that a unit-less target (`bt-agent-cli`) triggers no restart call.
- ✅ `bt-dashboard` is now covered by the same reliable rebuild+restart mechanism `bt-agent`/`bt-agent-cli`/`bt-gardener` already had, with its binary written to the path its production systemd unit actually executes from. Pinned by `TestDefaultRebuildTargets_IncludesBtDashboard` and `TestDashboardRebuildTargets_OutPathUnderBin` (`internal/agent/rebuild_test.go`) and `TestDashboardDriftWatcherWiresAutoRestart` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`, a source-level audit since `main()` can't run inside a unit test).
- ✅ `DashboardRebuildTargets` collapsing into an alias of `DefaultRebuildTargets` (instead of appending a second `bt-dashboard` entry on top of it) avoids a double-build/double-swap of the same binary within one `RebuildBinaries` call — pinned by `TestDashboardRebuildTargets_NoDuplicateTargets`.
- ✅ The MCP server binary `bin/bt-agent` — previously nobody's rebuild target — now rebuilds automatically via the daemon's fleet-wide sweep, correctly unit-less so the sibling-restart loop never attempts to restart a per-session-spawned process. Pinned by `TestDefaultRebuildTargets_PinsFullListIncludingMCPBinary` (`internal/agent/deploy_drift_restart_test.go`).
- ⚠️ A sibling unit restart failure is logged but non-fatal by design (`slog.Error`, loop continues) — a systemd-level failure to restart one sibling (e.g. `bt-gardener`) does not block the daemon from adopting its own fix, but also does not retry or escalate; an operator must still notice the error log for that specific sibling.
- Pinned by the tests named above; §7.2.2 (`07-deployment.md`) records the corresponding binary-layout fix (`bt-gardener`/`bt-dashboard` under `bin/`, matching each unit's `ExecStart`).

---

## ADR-135: `GenerateWithModel`/`generateCtx` Adopt Retry-With-Full-Jitter, and `FallbackLLM.generate` Gains a Per-Model Circuit Breaker (Q3 Reliability, Milestones 2–4/5)

**Context (2026-07-17):** Program "Q3 Reliability — Harden the LLM text-generation call path with retry-with-full-jitter and circuit breakers." `internal/knowledge/embeddings.go`'s `GetEmbedding` already carried this treatment (ADR-074), and `ACPClient` already carried a circuit breaker (ADR-054), but the two primary text-*generation* call sites had neither: `OpenAICompatClient.GenerateWithModel`'s `c.client.Do(httpReq)` failed the caller on the first transient HTTP response (429/5xx) with no retry, and re-dialed an already-unresponsive backend on every subsequent request with no failure memory at all. `ollama.Client.generateCtx`'s `c.llm.Call(callCtx, prompt)` — the call path `DefaultConfig()` (the platform's default LLM client) resolves to — had the same single-shot behavior. Separately, `FallbackLLM.generate` had a synchronous `reliability.Recover` panic guard (ADR-053) but no memory of *ordinary* (non-panic) failures across calls: a model in the chain that fails on every request was relaunched and re-failed on every single `Generate` call rather than being skipped in favor of the next model down the chain.

**Decision:** Three milestones. **(2)** `GenerateWithModel` wraps its request-build → `Do` → parse sequence in `reliability.DefaultRetryPolicy().ExecuteContext`, rebuilding the `*http.Request` (and its `bytes.Reader` body) fresh on every attempt — the reader is exhausted after the first `Do`, so retrying the original request would send an empty body. `checkRateLimit`'s existing retryable/non-retryable classification (429/5xx vs. 400/401) is unchanged; it now runs inside the retry loop instead of as a single-shot check. `OpenAICompatClient` also gains a `breaker *reliability.CircuitBreaker` field (`reliability.NewCircuitBreaker("openai-compat:"+baseURL, 3, 60*time.Second)`, one per client instance), checked via `Allow()` before the retry loop runs and recorded (`RecordSuccess`/`RecordFailure`) on the loop's overall outcome. **(3)** `Client.generateCtx` wraps `c.llm.Call` in the same `reliability.DefaultRetryPolicy()`, so a retryable Ollama backend failure gets the same jittered retry `GetEmbedding` (ADR-074) and `GenerateWithModel` now have; every caller of `DefaultConfig()`'s client inherits this transparently, with no signature change. **(4)** `FallbackLLM` gains a mutex-guarded `breakers map[string]*reliability.CircuitBreaker` and a `breakerFor(name)` accessor that lazily creates one breaker per `model.Name` (`reliability.NewCircuitBreaker("llm.fallback."+name, 3, 60*time.Second)`). `generate` checks `breaker.Allow()` immediately before invoking a model and records success/failure/panic on the same breaker exactly as `GenerateWithModel`/`ACPClient` (ADR-054) do — so a model that trips its breaker is *skipped*, not retried, on every call within the 60s cooldown — while the existing `reliability.Recover` panic guard (ADR-053) continues to wrap the call itself, unchanged.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-17) — milestones 2–4/5 of the stated program. Milestones 1/5 and 5/5 are not part of this change.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A transient 429/503-class response from an OpenAI-compatible backend now gets a jittered retry instead of failing `GenerateWithModel` on the first response, while a 400/401-class response still fails immediately with no wasted second call — pinned by `TestOpenAICompat_GenerateWithModel_RetryPolicyByStatusCode` (`internal/llm/openai_compat_test.go`, a 4-case table covering both classes).
- ✅ The default Ollama client degrades gracefully on a transient 5xx-style failure instead of failing the caller's request outright — pinned by `TestClient_GenerateCtx_RetryPolicyByFailureKind` (`internal/llm/ollama_test.go`), mirroring the same retryable/non-retryable table shape.
- ✅ A model that fails on every call in a `FallbackLLM` chain trips its own breaker after 3 consecutive failures and is skipped — not re-invoked — on subsequent calls within the 60s cooldown, while a healthy model later in the chain keeps serving every request — pinned by `TestFallbackLLM_CircuitBreakerSkipsPersistentlyFailingModel` (`internal/llm/fallback_test.go`), which drives 10 `Generate` calls against a chain of \[always-failing, always-healthy\] models and asserts the failing model's invocation count stays below the call count.
- ⚠️ `TestOpenAICompat_429ReturnsRateLimitError`/`TestOpenAICompat_429NonJSONBody` (`internal/llm/ratelimit_test.go`) had their `Retry-After` fixtures lowered from 120s/5s to 1s: `GenerateWithModel`'s retry loop now sleeps for the server's real `Retry-After` value between attempts, so the prior multi-minute fixture would have made the unit test sleep out several real windows before its attempts were exhausted.
- ⚠️ `OpenAICompatClient`'s breaker is keyed by `baseURL` and built once in `NewOpenAICompatClient` (one breaker per backend endpoint); `FallbackLLM`'s breakers are keyed by `model.Name` and created lazily per chain instance (one breaker per model in the chain). These are deliberately different granularities — matching the same choice ADR-054 (`ACPClient`, keyed by `cfg.Command`) and ADR-074 (a single package-level `ollama-embeddings` breaker) made for their respective call shapes — not an inconsistency to reconcile.
- ⚠️ This entry covers only the three milestones named above; milestone 1/5 and milestone 5/5 of the stated program are not addressed by this change and remain open.

---

## ADR-136: `internal/engine/chains.go`'s Chain Executors Adopt the Shared `reliability.RetryPolicy`, Closing Milestone 5/5 of the LLM Text-Generation Reliability Program

**Context (2026-07-17):** Program "Q3 Reliability — Harden the LLM text-generation call path with retry-with-full-jitter and circuit breakers" (ADR-135, milestones 2–4/5). `internal/engine/chains.go`'s chain executors call `bb.LLM.Generate` directly and predate ADR-135: `execMapReduce` (decompose, per-subtask, reduce) and `execRefine` (initial, critique, revise) each hand-rolled an un-jittered "call, if err retry exactly once" pattern with no Retry-After awareness, and the six single-shot executors (`execLLMCall`, `execRAGQuery`, `execToolCall`, `execConversation`, `execStructuredOutput`, `execRetrievalQA`) had no retry at all. A 429 hitting any of these paths was retried (or first-tried) immediately, ignoring the backend's `Retry-After` hint that `reliability.RetryPolicy.ExecuteContext` already knows how to honor via `RetryAfterFromError`/`ParseRetryAfter`.

**Decision:** A shared `chainRetryPolicy` (`internal/engine/chains.go`) wraps every chain executor's `bb.LLM.Generate` call in `reliability.RetryPolicy.ExecuteContext`, run under `bb.TraceContext` when present so caller-driven cancellation propagates into the retry sleep. `MaxRetries: 2` preserves the exact "one retry, then give up" attempt-count parity the hand-rolled versions and their call-count-sensitive tests already assumed. `RetryUnknown: true` is load-bearing: `ClassifyError` falls through to `ErrCatUnknown` for any error whose message doesn't match a known substring pattern, which is the shape of every `chains_test.go` mock LLM error — leaving `RetryUnknown` at `DefaultRetryPolicy`'s `false` would have silently stopped retrying them (Auth/Validation/ResourceExhausted still fail fast regardless, since `IsRetryable()` gates before `RetryUnknown` is consulted). `Jitter: FullJitterStrategy` replaces the immediate un-jittered retry. Two helpers cover both call shapes: `generateWithRetry` (no telemetry, for the six single-shot executors) and `generateWithRetryPolicy` (takes an `onRetry` callback, for `execMapReduce`/`execRefine`, which populate `map_reduce_decompose_retried`/`map_reduce_reduce_retried`/`refine_retried` `ChainState` counters from `RetryPolicy.OnRetry` instead of a manual boolean/counter). No circuit breaker is added at this layer — `bb.LLM` is typically the ADR-135 breaker-wrapped `OpenAICompatClient`/`ollama.Client`/`FallbackLLM`, so per-backend circuit breaking is already provided one layer down; this milestone's scope is retry-with-jitter parity at the chain-executor call sites only.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-17) — closes milestone 5/5 of the program ADR-135 left open; milestone 1/5 remains open.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A 429 hit by any chain executor (including the six previously single-shot ones) now backs off using the backend's `Retry-After` value instead of re-hammering immediately — pinned by `TestChainAction_LLMCall_HonorsRetryAfter`, `TestChainAction_MapReduce_DecomposeHonorsRetryAfter`, and `TestChainAction_Refine_InitialCallHonorsRetryAfter` (`internal/engine/chains_test.go`).
- ✅ The six previously single-shot executors (`execLLMCall`, `execRAGQuery`, `execToolCall`, `execConversation`, `execStructuredOutput`, `execRetrievalQA`) now retry once on a transient error instead of failing on the first attempt — pinned by `TestChainAction_SingleShotExecutors_RetryOnTransientError`.
- ✅ `execMapReduce`/`execRefine`'s existing call-count-sensitive tests (e.g. `TestChainAction_MapReduce_PartialFailureRecovery`, which enumerates calls 1–6 by comment) stay green unchanged — `MaxRetries: 2` and `RetryUnknown: true` were chosen specifically to preserve their exact attempt counts rather than adopting `DefaultRetryPolicy()` as-is.
- ⚠️ Milestone 1/5 of the program (ADR-135's other open milestone) is not addressed by this change and remains open; its scope was not identified in this pass.
- Pinned by the tests named above; see ADR-135 for the sibling milestones (2–4/5) at the `internal/llm` client layer this milestone's chain executors ultimately call through.

---

## ADR-137: Evolved-Tree Structural Metadata, Lineage Edges, and Resurrection-on-Load Survive a Daemon Restart (Q2 Evolvability, Milestones 1–3/4)

**Context (2026-07-17):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Make evolved-tree knowledge-graph metadata survive a daemon restart, not just the tree file on disk." `RegisterEvolved` (ADR-042) writes `StructuralFitness`/`NodeCount`/`Category`/`EvolvedCount` onto an evolved tree's in-memory `TreeMeta` and connects an `evolved_from` edge (ADR-078/ADR-083), and `EvolutionLineage` (ADR-126) queries that edge — but `feedback_persist.go`'s `SaveFeedback`/`LoadFeedback` (ADR-003, ADR-028, ADR-097/ADR-105) only ever serialized `Fitness`/`RunCount`/`EvolvedCount`/`LastOutcome`/`LastDuration`/`RecentRuns` and `uses_tool` edges. Unlike static domain trees, an evolved tree's `TreeMeta` is never rebuilt from its tree file at startup — it only re-enters `kg.Trees` when an evolution pass calls `RegisterEvolved` again. So a daemon restart left the evolved tree file and its `-evolved` ID intact on disk, but its structural-quality signal, node count, category, and lineage edge gone from the knowledge graph until (if ever) the same tree was re-evolved — and `LoadFeedback`'s prior `continue // unknown tree` branch actively discarded any snapshot entry for an ID not already in `kg.Trees`, which for an evolved tree post-restart was always true.

**Decision:** Extend the feedback snapshot and its restore path rather than introduce a separate persistence file, keeping ADR-003's single-file-per-concern shape intact. `treeFeedback` gains `StructuralFitness`/`NodeCount`/`Category` (`omitempty`, so pre-existing snapshots and non-evolved trees are unaffected), populated in `SaveFeedback` and restored in `LoadFeedback` alongside the existing fields (milestone 1/4). `SaveFeedback`'s edge filter widens from `e.Type == "uses_tool"` to also match `"evolved_from"`, reusing the existing `ToolEdges`/`tool_edges` field and JSON key rather than adding a new one — a smaller diff that keeps old feedback files (which have no `evolved_from` entries to lose) forward-compatible without a version bump (milestone 2/4). `LoadFeedback`'s unknown-tree branch changes from skipping the entry to resurrecting it — `tree = &TreeMeta{ID: id, Name: id}; kg.Trees[id] = tree` — before applying the snapshot fields, because an evolved tree's absence from `kg.Trees` post-restart is expected and legitimate, not a sign of a stale/bogus ID the way it would be for a mistyped static-tree reference (milestone 3/4). The resurrected `TreeMeta` only gets `ID`/`Name` plus the feedback-snapshot fields — `Description`/`Capabilities`/`Keywords`/`Synonyms` stay empty until a real `RegisterEvolved` call re-populates them, since those fields were never part of the feedback snapshot's "runtime feedback only" contract (`feedbackSnapshot`'s doc comment) and inventing them from the base tree at load time would require re-deriving inheritance logic `RegisterEvolved` already owns.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-17) — milestones 1–3/4. Milestone 4/4 closed the same day by ADR-138: `persistEvolvedWinner` now flushes the feedback snapshot to disk on every evolved-winner registration instead of relying solely on the scheduler's run-outcome path.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `StructuralFitness`/`NodeCount`/`Category` and the `evolved_from` lineage edge now survive `SaveFeedback`/`LoadFeedback` round trips even when the evolved tree isn't otherwise registered before `LoadFeedback` runs — pinned by `TestSaveLoadFeedback_EvolvedMetadataRoundTrip`, `TestSaveLoadFeedback_EvolvedFromEdgeRoundTrip`, and `TestLoadFeedback_ResurrectsUnregisteredEvolvedTree` (`internal/knowledge/feedback_persist_test.go`), the last of which also asserts `EvolutionLineage` resolves correctly against the resurrected node.
- ✅ `dashboard.EvolutionLineage`'s `/api/trees` consumer (ADR-126) and `bt_evolve_bottlenecks`' lineage-skip guard (ADR-127) both keep working across a restart instead of silently losing their signal until the next evolution pass — closing the same class of gap ADR-097 flagged for `RecentRuns` (resolved separately by ADR-105, §8).
- ⚠️ A resurrected `TreeMeta` is a bare stand-in (`ID`/`Name` only, feedback fields populated) until a subsequent `RegisterEvolved` call restores `Category`-driven `Capabilities`/`Keywords`/`Synonyms` inheritance — `Discover`/`DiscoverRelated` on a resurrected-but-not-yet-re-evolved tree will not fuzzy-match on those fields, only on `EvolutionLineage`'s edge walk.
- Rejected: keeping the old skip-unknown-tree behavior and instead having every evolution pass proactively re-`Register` its known evolved trees at startup — rejected because nothing currently enumerates "all evolved tree IDs" independent of the tree-store directory listing, which would have made this a tree-store-scanning change instead of a `feedback_persist.go`-local one, a larger surface for the same restart-survival goal.
- Rejected: a separate `evolved_edges` JSON key instead of widening `tool_edges` — rejected as unnecessary surface area; `tool_edges` was already a general "edges this snapshot cares about" bucket, and the field's own updated doc comment (`internal/knowledge/feedback_persist.go`) now records the type widening for the next reader.

---

## ADR-138: `persistEvolvedWinner` Flushes the Feedback Snapshot to Disk on Every Evolved-Winner Registration, Closing Milestone 4/4 of ADR-137's Program (Q2 Evolvability)

**Context (2026-07-17):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Make evolved-tree knowledge-graph metadata survive a daemon restart, not just the tree file on disk." ADR-137 (milestones 1–3/4) taught `SaveFeedback`/`LoadFeedback` to round-trip an evolved tree's `StructuralFitness`/`NodeCount`/`Category` and its `evolved_from` lineage edge, and to resurrect a `TreeMeta` for an evolved tree missing from `kg.Trees` after a restart — but left milestone 4/4's scope unidentified. The gap: nothing in the `bt_evolve_*` MCP tool path ever calls `FlushFeedback`. `persistEvolvedWinner` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) commits `RegisterEvolved`'s bookkeeping into the in-memory `KnowledgeGraph` only; the sole production caller of `MarkFeedbackDirty`+`FlushFeedback` was `internal/agent/scheduler.go`'s `persistRunFeedback`, invoked after a *scheduled run* completes (§8.4). An evolution winner registered purely through an interactive `bt_evolve_*` MCP call — with no scheduled run landing in between — had its ADR-137 metadata correct in memory but never reached the feedback snapshot file, so a daemon restart before the next scheduled run silently dropped it, even though the evolved tree file itself survived on disk — exactly the gap ADR-137's program targeted, just on the write side instead of the load side.

**Decision:** Call `deps.kg.MarkFeedbackDirty()` and `deps.kg.FlushFeedback(false)` from `persistEvolvedWinner` immediately after a successful `RegisterEvolved`, inside the same `deps.kg != nil` guard and after the same `persisted=true` gate the function already enforces (ADR-083's atomic bookkeeping-and-disk-write pairing) — so the flush shares `RegisterEvolved`'s all-or-nothing guarantee rather than firing on a skipped or failed persist. `FlushFeedback(false)`, not `force=true`, reuses the existing throttle window (§8.4, `ConfigureFeedbackPersistence`'s `minInterval`) instead of adding a second, unthrottled disk-write path for the same file; a flush failure is logged (`slog.Warn`) and swallowed rather than surfaced as a tool error, mirroring `persistRunFeedback`'s own no-fail-the-caller posture — an evolved tree is fully usable in-memory even when this particular flush is delayed to the next throttle-eligible write.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-17) — closes ADR-137's Q2 Evolvability program (milestones 1–4/4).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Every `bt_evolve_*` MCP tool that routes through `persistEvolvedWinner` now reaches the feedback snapshot on disk immediately instead of depending on a scheduled run landing before the next restart — pinned by `TestPersistEvolvedWinner_FlushesFeedbackToDisk` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), which arms a zero-`minInterval` graph, calls `persistEvolvedWinner` once, and asserts the flushed file round-trips the evolved tree's `StructuralFitness` via a fresh `KnowledgeGraph.LoadFeedback`.
- ✅ Shares the throttle window with the scheduler's own flushes rather than adding a second, independently-throttled write path against the same feedback file.
- ⚠️ A flush failure is only logged, not surfaced in the tool's JSON result — an operator watching only MCP tool output, not daemon logs, would not see it; consistent with the `persistRunFeedback` behavior this mirrors.
- Rejected: an unthrottled `FlushFeedback(true)` on every evolved-winner registration — rejected as unnecessary disk-write pressure under back-to-back evolve calls; the existing throttle window already bounds write frequency, and `MarkFeedbackDirty` guarantees a throttled-away write isn't lost, only delayed.

---

## ADR-139: `dlqReplayOutcomeError` Extends the Healthy-Outcome Classification to the DLQ Replay Executor, Ending Endless Re-Replay of Rate-Limit and No-Code Outcomes (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-17):** ADR-025 taught `recordSchedulerAttempt` to classify the Claude rate-limit carryover (`agent.RateLimitCarryoverOutcome`, §6.4/ADR-016) as a healthy, terminal, non-failing disposition rather than a failure or a success, and a later scheduler-path fix (2026-07-15) extended the same treatment to the other "healthy no-code" outcomes `agent.IsHealthyOutcome` recognizes (`no_change`: analysis-only; `degraded`: deterministic fallback) after retrying them was found to burn a full Claude cycle per attempt and dead-letter otherwise-honest runs. The scheduler's own circuit-breaker success classifier, `cycleBreakerSuccess` (`internal/agent/scheduler.go`), applies the identical definition. The drop-safe DLQ replay executor installed by `dlq.SetReplayExecutor` (`cmd/bt-agent/main.go`, ADR-025/ADR-036) never received the same fix: its inline classification was a blanket `res.Outcome != "success"`, so a replayed dead letter that gracefully paused on a rate limit — or legitimately landed on `no_change`/`degraded` — was treated as a failed replay. Because `reliability.DeadLetterQueue.Replay` only drops an entry on a nil error (reliability.go:249–251), that misclassification kept a healthy replay's entry in the DLQ, where it was requeued and re-replayed on every subsequent scan until ADR-025's `MaxReplayAttempts` (5) terminally `Abandoned` it — silently discarding a task that had never actually failed.

**Decision:** Extract the inline check into `dlqReplayOutcomeError(res *agent.RunResult) error` (`cmd/bt-agent/main.go`), mirroring `recordSchedulerAttempt`'s and `cycleBreakerSuccess`'s classification: a nil result or a `"success"` outcome resolves as healthy; `agent.RateLimitCarryoverOutcome` or any outcome `agent.IsHealthyOutcome` recognizes also resolves as healthy (nil error, so `Replay` drops the entry); anything else wraps `agent.OutcomeErrorDetail` into the returned error so the entry is kept and retried. The `SetReplayExecutor` closure now delegates to `dlqReplayOutcomeError(res)` instead of re-inlining the old check.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-17)

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A replayed dead letter that gracefully pauses on a rate limit, or lands on `no_change`/`degraded`, is now dropped as a healthy result instead of endlessly re-replayed toward `Abandoned` — pinned by `TestDLQReplayOutcomeError_HealthyNonSuccessNotFailing` (table-driven over success/rate-limit-carryover/no_change/degraded, plus a genuine-failure and a nil-result control) and `TestDLQReplayExecutorUsesOutcomeClassifier`, a source-audit test that fails if the closure ever reverts to re-inlining the old blanket check instead of delegating to `dlqReplayOutcomeError`.
- ✅ All three outcome-classification call sites — `recordSchedulerAttempt` (scheduler retries), `cycleBreakerSuccess` (per-agent circuit breaker), and now the DLQ replay executor — apply the identical healthy-outcome definition (`agent.IsHealthyOutcome` plus `agent.RateLimitCarryoverOutcome`), closing the classification drift between the scheduler path and the replay path that ADR-025's original fix never reached.
- ⚠️ A genuine failure still keeps its DLQ entry and is retried up to `MaxReplayAttempts` before `Abandoned`, unchanged from ADR-025/ADR-036 — this fix narrows what counts as a failure, it does not change the abandonment mechanics.

## ADR-140: `bt_evolve_selection_pressure` Adopts the ADR-127 Lineage-Skip Guard and `bt_evolve_genetic` Writes Fitness Back to the Base Tree, Closing the Q2 Evolvability Fitness-Feedback/Lineage-Skip Consistency Program (Milestones 1–2/2)

**Context (2026-07-17):** Program "Q2 Evolvability — Close the fitness-feedback and lineage-skip consistency gaps across the production evolution-tool family," milestones 1–2/2.
