**Decision (milestone 3/3):** `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go` gains a package-level `dashPersonaStore *persona.Store`, loaded once at startup from `agent.UsersDir()` (mirroring the existing `dashAgentRunner`/`dashCBStore` package-var injection pattern), and wires two new exported hooks in `internal/dashboard/hitl_handlers.go` — `dashboard.AgentRegistry` (set to `dashAgentRunner.Registry`) and `dashboard.PersonaStore` (set to the new store) — following the same pattern as the package's existing `DiscoverTreeFn`. `HandleHITL`'s approve and reject branches each call a new `finalizeHITLResolution(req, approved bool)` immediately after a successful `store.Approve`/`store.Reject`; it calls both `persona.FinalizeAutomationApproval(AgentRegistry, PersonaStore, req, approved)` and `persona.FinalizeFeedbackEscalation(PersonaStore, req, approved)` unconditionally. No request-kind branching is needed in the dashboard because both shared functions already no-op on a request that doesn't match their own context/`NodeName` shape (milestone 1's guard on `req.Context["automation"]`, milestone 2's on `req.NodeName == "FeedbackReviewEscalation"`).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-18) — all 3 milestones landed. Pinned by `internal/persona/automation_finalize_test.go`'s `TestFinalizeAutomationApproval_ApprovedActivatesAgentAndLedger`, `TestFinalizeAutomationApproval_RejectedQuarantinesTreeAndLedger`, and `TestFinalizeAutomationApproval_NilOrNonAutomationRequestIgnored`; `cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`'s `TestHandleHITL_ApproveActivatesAutomation`, `TestHandleHITL_RejectQuarantinesAutomationTree`, and `TestHandleHITL_ApproveResumesFeedbackEscalation` (driving the real `dashboard.HandleHITL` HTTP handler end-to-end against an isolated registry/store/HITL store); and the existing `cmd/bt-agent/autopilot_test.go`/`feedback_tools_test.go` regressions, which pass unchanged, confirming the MCP path's behavior is identical after delegating to the shared functions.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Dashboard-approved/rejected automations now activate, resume, and quarantine identically to the MCP `bt_hitl_approve`/`bt_hitl_reject` path, closing the parity gap between the two HITL surfaces for every outcome ADR-161/162/173 defined.
- ✅ Both binaries now share a single finalization implementation (`internal/persona/automation_finalize.go`) instead of duplicating the ledger-update/registry-activation/tree-quarantine logic; a future change to any finalization rule only needs to land in one place.
- ⚠️ The dashboard binary does not run its own A2A server, so `persona.ActivateAutomation`'s MCP-side card-refresh step (`deps.refreshA2ACards`) has no dashboard equivalent — a dashboard-approved automation is scheduled and runnable but does not trigger an A2A card refresh; the gap is inert only because the dashboard doesn't otherwise expose an A2A card registry to refresh.
- Rejected alternative: giving the dashboard its own copy of the finalization logic (as it effectively had none before) was rejected in favor of extracting the existing, already-tested MCP logic — duplicating it a second time would have reintroduced exactly the kind of behavioral drift this ADR closes.
- Pinned by the tests named above.

---

## ADR-176: `util.SaveJSONAtomic`/`LoadJSON` Become the One Canonical Implementation of ADR-003's Atomic-Write Pattern, Fixing Three Sites That Had Silently Drifted from It (Q5 Consistency & Reuse)

**Context (2026-07-18):** ADR-003 mandates atomic file persistence — write a temp file, then `os.Rename` it over the destination — precisely so a crash mid-write can never leave a truncated state file. In practice the pattern had been hand-copied at every call site instead of shared: `internal/evolution/pareto.go`'s `ParetoFront.Save`/`Load`, `internal/reliability/reliability.go`'s `DeadLetterQueue.save`, `internal/hitl/store.go`'s `Store.save`, `internal/doormate/store.go`'s `Store.atomicWrite`, `internal/research/store.go`'s `Store.Save`, and `internal/gardener/gardener.go`'s `Registry.SaveTree` each reimplemented marshal→`MkdirAll`→write-`.tmp`→rename with small variations in indentation, permissions, and error wrapping. Three sites in the same `reliability.go` file had drifted further than a cosmetic variation: `TaskQueue.save`, `SchedulerState.persist`, and `PriorityQueue.save` called plain `os.WriteFile` directly to the destination path — an in-place rewrite that keeps the same inode, so a crash mid-write truncates the live queue/scheduler-state file, silently violating the ADR-003 guarantee that the file sitting next to `DeadLetterQueue.save` in the same source file already honored.

**Decision:** A new `internal/util/persist.go` exports `SaveJSONAtomic(path string, v interface{}) error` (`MkdirAll` the parent, `json.MarshalIndent`, write `path+".tmp"` at `0644`, `os.Rename` into place) and `LoadJSON(path string, dest interface{}) error` (a missing file is a silent cold start; any other read/parse error is returned) as the one canonical implementation of the pattern, replicated from `pareto.go`'s already-proven `Save`/`Load`. It intentionally covers only the write/read mechanics — cross-process exclusion (e.g. `DeadLetterQueue`'s `acquireExperienceLock`) stays the caller's responsibility, since not every call site needs it and the helper has no way to know which lock a given caller must take. `TaskQueue.save`, `SchedulerState.persist`, and `PriorityQueue.save` are migrated first (fixing the real crash-truncation gap), then `DeadLetterQueue.save`, `hitl.Store.save`, `doormate.Store.atomicWrite`, `research.Store.Save`, and `gardener.Registry.SaveTree` are migrated for consistency — each call site that previously set a non-default file mode (`hitl` 0600, `doormate` 0640) restores it with an explicit `os.Chmod` after the call, since the helper hardcodes 0644.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-18) — all 3 milestones of the Q5 Consistency & Reuse program landed in one pass. Pinned by `internal/util/persist_test.go` (round-trip, parent-dir creation, no leftover `.tmp`, cold-start-on-missing-file, corrupt-file error, overwrite); `TestTaskQueue_SaveAtomicReplace`, `TestSchedulerState_PersistAtomicReplace`, and `TestPriorityQueue_SaveAtomicReplace` (`internal/reliability/reliability_test.go`, each using `os.SameFile` to prove the destination inode changes on every save rather than being rewritten in place); and `TestStoreSave_UsesCanonicalAtomicJSONFormatAndPermission` (`internal/hitl/store_test.go`), which pins both the restored 0600 mode and an accepted format change (below).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `TaskQueue`, `SchedulerState`, and `PriorityQueue` now genuinely honor ADR-003: a crash mid-write can no longer truncate the live queue or scheduler-state file, closing a gap that had existed silently alongside the correctly-atomic `DeadLetterQueue.save` in the same file.
- ✅ One tested implementation instead of six hand-copied ones — a future fix to the write/read mechanics (e.g. an `fsync` before rename) lands once in `util.SaveJSONAtomic` rather than needing six coordinated edits.
- ⚠️ `hitl.Store.save` previously wrote compact JSON (`json.Marshal`) specifically because, per its own removed comment, "indentation tripled its size" for a machine-only file; migrating to `util.SaveJSONAtomic`'s hardcoded `json.MarshalIndent` reintroduces that size cost for human readability the store doesn't need. Accepted as the cost of one canonical helper rather than special-casing compact output; pinned (not just permitted) by `TestStoreSave_UsesCanonicalAtomicJSONFormatAndPermission`.
- Rejected alternative: giving `SaveJSONAtomic` options for indentation/permissions/locking — rejected to keep the helper's surface minimal; six call sites' worth of variation behind an options struct would recreate the duplication it was meant to remove. Callers that need something other than the 0644/indented default restore it themselves with one extra line.
- Cross-process locking remains deliberately out of scope — see `DeadLetterQueue`'s `acquireExperienceLock` (→ §8) for the pattern call sites should follow when they need it.

---

## ADR-177: `reapOrphanedSuperpowersBranches` Force-Reaps Unmerged `superpowers/*` Branches Behind a 7-Day Age Gate, an Abandonment Check, and an Archive-Before-Delete Safety Net (Q3 Reliability)

**Context (2026-07-19):** Commit `8f4af50` (2026-07-13, never separately logged here) introduced `reapOrphanedSuperpowersBranches`, which reclaims orphaned `superpowers/*` branches — ones whose worktree directory is gone but whose branch ref lingers — via `git branch -d`, a delete that only succeeds for branches already merged into `master`. That was intentionally conservative: an unmerged orphan (un-landed work whose worktree dir was already cleaned up) simply survived forever for manual triage. In practice no manual triage was happening, so the conservatism just became a slower leak alongside the one the original sweep fixed.

**Decision:** When `git branch -d` fails (branch is unmerged), `reapOrphanedSuperpowersBranches` now applies three gates, all of which must clear before the branch is touched: (1) age — the branch's `%(committerdate:iso-strict)` must be more than `staleSuperpowersBranchMaxAge` (7 days) old, via `superpowersBranchOlderThanCutoff`, which treats an empty/unparseable date as *not* old rather than risk an unintended sweep; (2) it must be either an abandoned `pending_patch` run — `superpowersBranchRunAbandoned` reuses the existing `pendingPatchRecoveryAttempts`/`pendingPatchRecoveryMaxAttempts` bookkeeping (`actions_superpowers_prod.go`) that already gates a related recovery loop, rather than inventing a second abandonment signal — or missing its `verification/worktree.patch` landing artifact (`superpowersBranchPatchArtifactMissing`, same path convention `superpowers_apply.go` writes to). Only then does `archiveAndDeleteSuperpowersBranch` run: it captures `git diff --binary master...<branch>` to `docs/superpowers/runs/<id>/verification/reaped-branch.patch` *before* force-deleting with `git branch -D`, so a reaped branch's un-landed changes are archived, never silently discarded. `reapOrphanedSuperpowersBranches` gained a `runsDir` parameter (matching the existing `recoverGoapFusionPendingPatchesInDir(ctx, runner, runsDir)` convention) so tests can point the gates at a temp dir instead of the hardcoded `superpowersRunsDir` constant; the one production call site (`actions_superpowers_prod.go`) now passes it through.
- Rejected alternative: reap any unmerged orphan branch older than the age cutoff, without the abandonment/patch-missing check — rejected because "orphaned" only means the worktree dir is gone, not that the run failed; a young-but-orphaned run mid-recovery would lose work if age alone were sufficient.
- Rejected alternative: skip the archive step and rely on git reflog / `git branch -D`'s dangling-commit recovery — rejected as too fragile for unattended, best-effort cleanup; the reflog expires and dangling commits are eventually gc'd, whereas the `.patch` archive is a durable, run-scoped artifact.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-19). Pinned by `TestReapOrphanedSuperpowersBranchesAppliesAgeAndAbandonmentGates`, `TestSuperpowersBranchOlderThanCutoff`, `TestSuperpowersBranchRunAbandoned`, `TestSuperpowersBranchPatchArtifactMissing`, and `TestArchiveAndDeleteSuperpowersBranch` (`internal/engine/superpowers_worktree_sweep_test.go`).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Unmerged orphaned branches no longer accumulate unbounded — the population previously "never touched" now drains once a run is confirmed either abandoned or already fully landed elsewhere.
- ✅ Best-effort by design is preserved: a branch that is merely young, or that still has recovery attempts remaining with its patch artifact present, is never force-deleted — matching the function's existing never-fails-the-calling-run contract.
- ⚠️ The archive write and the branch delete are two separate git/filesystem operations with no shared transaction; a crash between them would leave the archive written but the branch not yet deleted (harmless — the next sweep re-evaluates and re-archives) rather than the reverse (branch gone, no archive), since delete only runs after the archive write succeeds.

---

## ADR-178: `gitStageArgs` Stops Re-Staging `graphify-out/`'s Regenerated Artifacts and `sectionAwareGraphContext` Becomes the One Canonical Graphify-Report Reader (Q5 Consistency & Reuse, Milestones 1–3/5)

**Context (2026-07-19):** `.gitignore` has excluded `graphify-out/` wholesale since the repo's earliest history, yet `graphify-out/graph.json` and `graphify-out/manifest.json` were force-tracked at some point and stayed tracked — a `graphify update .` regenerates both every cycle, and each cycle's landing commit re-staged and re-committed the resulting ~55k-line diffs, a direct contributor to `.git` reaching 781MB. The exclusion logic that should have prevented this was already inconsistent: `isGeneratedSuperpowersOrGraphifyPath` (`blockingMainRepoDirtySummary`'s dirty-repo gate) and `superpowersGeneratedCommitExclusions` (the per-task commit's `git add` pathspec, `actions_superpowers_prod.go`) both already treated `graphify-out/` as generated, but `gitStageArgs` — the apply-stage landing commit's own `git add -A` pathspec (`superpowers_apply.go:388`, also used by the auto-fix re-stage loop) — carried its own separate, hardcoded exclusion list covering only `docs/superpowers/runs/**` and `docs/superpowers/plans/**`. Separately, every LLM prompt that grounds itself in `GRAPH_REPORT.md` (`buildSeedProgramPrompt`, the NotebookLM query builder, the GrillMe query builder — all `actions_goap_fusion.go`/`goap_seed_program.go`) truncated the raw ~900KB report to its first 2500–3500 characters; on a report that size, only the header ever survived — none of the God Nodes, Community Hubs, Surprising Connections, or Low-Cohesion Files sections a research prompt actually needs. The arc42 `ReadGraphReport` action and `GraphIsFresh` condition (`arc42_nodes.go`) read/checked the same report through their own further-drifted logic, and `GraphIsFresh` treated the report file merely existing as proof it was current.

**Decision:** `gitStageArgs` is now derived from the same `superpowersGeneratedPathPrefixes`/`superpowersGeneratedCommitExclusions()` list `isGeneratedSuperpowersOrGraphifyPath` already uses, so the landing commit's pathspec cannot drift from the per-task commit's or the dirty-repo gate's again; because a `:(exclude)` pathspec suppresses `git add -A` regardless of whether a path is already tracked, this alone stops the landing commit from staging any further `graphify-out/` modifications, independent of when the historical `git rm --cached` on `graph.json`/`manifest.json`/`cache/` happens (`GRAPH_REPORT.md` is deliberately kept out of the exclusion set and stays tracked). `CleanupGraphifyOut` (`actions_goap_fusion_prod_additions.go`) gained a `git clean -fdx -- graphify-out/` step after its existing `git checkout -- graphify-out/`, since `checkout` only restores tracked paths and is a silent no-op against `graphify-out/`'s untracked, gitignored, regenerated outputs. A new `sectionAwareGraphContext(report string)` (`actions_goap_fusion.go`) extracts the report's Summary/God Nodes/Community Hubs/Surprising Connections/Low-Cohesion Files sections — the extraction `ReadGraphifyReport` already did ad hoc — and `readSectionAwareGraphContext()` wraps it with the file read to become the one canonical entry point, replacing the three separate raw-read-then-truncate call sites plus the arc42 `ReadGraphReport` action's own hardcoded path and untruncated dump. `goapFusionGraphReport` changed from a `const` to a `var` so tests can point it at a synthetic report. `GraphIsFresh` now parses a `` Built from commit: `<sha>` `` line under the report's `## Graph Freshness` section and compares it against `goapFusionRepo`'s current HEAD (`git rev-parse HEAD`) instead of merely checking the file exists.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-19) — milestones 1–3/5 of the Q5 Consistency & Reuse "graph fidelity and pipeline robustness" program; milestones 4/5 (a goal-note key fix) and 5/5 (budget-aware task batching) remain open. Pinned by `TestGitStageArgsExcludesGeneratedSuperpowersAndGraphifyPaths` and `TestCleanupGraphifyOutRemovesUntrackedRegeneratedArtifacts` (`superpowers_apply_test.go`); `TestArc42ReadGraphReport_UsesCanonicalGoapFusionReportPath` and `TestGraphIsFresh_ChecksBuiltCommitAgainstHEAD` (`actions_goap_fusion_test.go`); and `TestBuildSeedProgramPrompt_CarriesSectionAwareGraphContext`, `TestRunGoapFusionNotebookLMResearch_QueryCarriesSectionAwareGraphContext`, and `TestGrillMeNotebookLM_QueryCarriesSectionAwareGraphContext` (`goap_seed_program_test.go`).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ The landing/auto-fix commit path can no longer stage `graphify-out/**` changes at all, stopping the repeated ~55k-line `graph.json`/`manifest.json` diffs at the source; only a one-time `git rm --cached` on those paths (which `CleanupGraphifyOut` now assumes will follow) remains to shrink the already-tracked working set.
- ✅ Every LLM-facing consumer of `GRAPH_REPORT.md` shares one extraction function instead of four independently drifted implementations, so a report-scale document's analytical sections — not just its header — reach every research prompt that grounds itself in the graph.
- ✅ `GraphIsFresh` has a real freshness signal (embedded built-commit SHA vs. current HEAD) instead of file-existence alone, so a stale-but-present report no longer reads as fresh.
- ⚠️ Milestones 4/5 and 5/5 of the program are not part of this change; the goal-note key fix and budget-aware task batching remain open.

---

## ADR-179: `goapResearchGoalKey` Strips a Scoped Goal Line's `(files: …)` Suffix and `executeSuperpowersTaskBatch` Gates Each Task's RED Phase on Remaining Cycle Budget, Closing Milestones 4–5/5 of the ADR-178 Q5 Program

**Context (2026-07-22):** ADR-178 landed milestones 1–3/5 of the Q5 Consistency & Reuse "graph fidelity and pipeline robustness" program and flagged two remaining gaps. Milestone 4/5: `chargeGoapResearchGoalFailure` (`actions_goap_fusion_goal_budget.go`) stamps a research goal's implementation-failure budget keyed by `goapResearchGoalKey` on the goal line as `PrioritizeGoapGoals` queues it — before scoping. `scopeGoapGoalLine` (`goap_research_goals.go`) can append a deterministic `" (files: …)"` annotation to a pathless goal line before `WriteSuperpowersImplementationPlan` composes the task text `buildGoalDrivenImplementationPlan` (`superpowers_plan_builder.go`) parses. `goapGoalFailureNote`, which looks up the previous failure through that same `goapResearchGoalKey`, therefore keyed a scoped retry's lookup differently from how the goal had been charged — the `[PREVIOUS-ATTEMPT-FAILURE: …]` steering note could silently go missing from a scoped goal's retry plan even though its budget was correctly charged, letting an agent resubmit the same rejected approach with no visibility into why it failed. Milestone 5/5: `executeSuperpowersTaskBatch` (`superpowers_task_executor.go`) started every task's RED phase unconditionally; the only budget enforcement was `superpowersBudgetKillError`, which reclassifies a claude-phase failure only AFTER the cycle's outer context deadline has already SIGKILLed a mid-flight verification process. Run `20260718T164339` lost an already-green milestone to exactly that mid-flight kill.

**Decision:** `goapResearchGoalKey` now composes a new `stripGoapGoalScopeSuffix` (trims a trailing `" (files: …)"` suffix) with the existing `stripGoapGoalTransientNotes` before computing the goal's `research.Key`, so every caller that already goes through this one canonical key function — `chargeGoapResearchGoalFailure`'s charge, `goapGoalFailureNote`'s lookup, and `goapAbandonedResearchGoal`'s budget check — keys a goal identically whether or not `scopeGoapGoalLine` had to synthesize its scope suffix. The scope strip is deliberately its own function rather than folded into `stripGoapGoalTransientNotes`: that shared strip is also used by the plan builder's file-scope extraction (`extractGoFilePaths(stripGoapGoalTransientNotes(goal))`), which needs to read the scope suffix to recover the file list, so a shared strip would blind that call site. Separately, `executeSuperpowersTaskBatch` now calls a new `superpowersTaskBudgetInsufficient(ctx, task)` immediately before each task's RED phase. `superpowersTaskMinBudget` derives a per-task minimum from the task's own verification commands — the largest `-timeout` value found among them (regex-parsed), multiplied by `superpowersTaskBudgetMultiplier` (2x, a safety margin covering process startup and the gap between a test binary's own clean `-timeout` expiry and the outer deadline's SIGKILL). A task with no parseable `-timeout`, or a context with no deadline, is never gated. When the remaining `ctx.Deadline()` budget falls below that minimum, the batch stops cleanly before starting the RED phase: the triggering task and every later task are marked `"skipped"`, `run.PartialFailure` records a distinct `"batch-stopped-insufficient-budget"` reason, and — reusing the existing partial-failure snapshot convention — any already-committed per-task snapshots are mixed-reset back to the pre-batch `base` so completed, verified work still lands uncommitted in the worktree instead of being lost with the batch.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-22) — closes milestones 4/5 and 5/5 of the ADR-178 Q5 Consistency & Reuse program (all 5/5 now landed). Pinned by `TestBuildGoalDrivenPlan_InjectsPreviousFailureNoteThroughScopeSuffix` (`actions_goap_fusion_goal_budget_test.go`), which seeds a failure under the unscoped key, scopes the goal line the way `scopeGoapGoalLine` does, and asserts the retry plan still carries the `PREVIOUS-ATTEMPT-FAILURE` note; and `TestExecuteSuperpowersTaskBatchStopsCleanlyOnInsufficientBudget` (`superpowers_task_executor_test.go`).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A research goal's steering note now survives being scoped by `scopeGoapGoalLine` — a retried goal that needed synthesized file scope gets the same `[PREVIOUS-ATTEMPT-FAILURE: …]` guidance an unscoped retry always got, closing a silent gap where scoped goals could loop on the same rejected approach.
- ✅ A batch whose remaining cycle budget can no longer safely cover a task's own verification commands now stops cleanly before starting that task's RED phase, converting an after-the-fact SIGKILL classification (`superpowersBudgetKillError`) into a before-the-fact skip, and preserving already-completed tasks' verified work via the existing snapshot/mixed-reset carry-forward path instead of risking it to a kill.
- ⚠️ The 2x `-timeout` margin (`superpowersTaskBudgetMultiplier`) is a static heuristic, not derived from measured process-startup/teardown overhead; a verification command whose true startup cost exceeds the margin could still be gated too late, while an unusually fast machine gates somewhat earlier than strictly necessary. Not measured in this change.
- Rejected alternative: keep classifying budget kills only after the fact via `superpowersBudgetKillError` — rejected because reclassification cannot recover a mid-flight SIGKILL's lost verification work; only refusing to start a task protects an already-green milestone.

---

## ADR-180: `reliability.ScoreOutcome` Becomes the One Canonical Block-Fitness Scoring Formula, Replacing Two of Its Three Copy-Pasted Sites (Q5 Consistency & Reuse, Milestones 1–3/4)

**Context (2026-07-22):** The block-fitness scoring formula — scale `qualityScore` to a 0–100 percentage; if that's zero or below, fall back to 75 when `success` is true or `outcome` case-insensitively matches "success"/"completed", else 25; clamp to `[0,100]` — was hand-copied identically at three sites: `internal/blocks/fitness.go`'s `ScoreFromBlackboard`, `internal/engine/ops_actions.go`'s `fitnessScoreFromBB`, and an inline block in `internal/dashboard/executor.go`'s `recordBlockFitnessMetric`. A future change to the formula (e.g. a different fallback split, or an additional outcome keyword) would need three coordinated edits, with no test tying them together to catch drift.

**Decision:** `internal/reliability/reliability.go` gains `ScoreOutcome(outcome string, qualityScore float64, success bool) float64`, the one canonical implementation of the formula, alongside the package's existing circuit-breaker/retry/DLQ reliability primitives — a defensible home since block fitness is itself a reliability signal, and the package already has no import-cycle path back to `internal/blocks`, `internal/engine`, or `internal/dashboard`. `blocks.ScoreFromBlackboard` and `engine.fitnessScoreFromBB` are now one-line delegations to it, each pinned by a source-inspection test (`TestScoreFromBlackboard_DelegatesToReliabilityScoreOutcome`, `TestFitnessScoreFromBB_DelegatesToReliabilityScoreOutcome`) that fails if the duplicated inline formula ever reappears in either file, rather than only checking input/output equivalence. `internal/dashboard/executor.go`'s inline copy is deliberately left in place for this pass.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-22) — milestones 1–3/4 of the Q5 Consistency & Reuse program. Milestone 4/4 closed the same day by ADR-181: `internal/dashboard/executor.go`'s `recordBlockFitnessMetric` now delegates to `reliability.ScoreOutcome` too. Pinned by `TestScoreOutcome_*` (`internal/reliability/reliability_test.go`, covering the positive-quality-score, clamp-above-100, both zero-quality fallback branches, no-success-signal, and negative-quality-clamps-to-zero cases) and the two delegation-guard tests above (`internal/engine/ops_actions_test.go`, `internal/blocks/phase5_test.go`).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Two of the formula's three copies now share one tested implementation; a future formula change lands once in `reliability.ScoreOutcome` instead of needing coordinated edits across `blocks` and `engine`.
- ✅ `ScoreFromBlackboard`'s and `fitnessScoreFromBB`'s exported signatures are unchanged, so `RecordTaskBlockFitness` and other existing callers are unaffected by the consolidation.
- ⚠️ `internal/dashboard/executor.go`'s copy (milestone 4/4) is not part of this change — the formula still has two independent implementations in the codebase (`reliability.ScoreOutcome` and the dashboard's inline block) until that milestone lands.

---

## ADR-181: `AgentExecutor.recordBlockFitnessMetric` Adopts `reliability.ScoreOutcome`, Closing Milestone 4/4 of the ADR-180 Block-Fitness Consolidation Program (Q5 Consistency & Reuse)

**Context (2026-07-22):** ADR-180 (milestones 1–3/4) extracted `reliability.ScoreOutcome` as the one canonical block-fitness scoring formula and delegated `blocks.ScoreFromBlackboard`/`engine.fitnessScoreFromBB` to it, but deliberately left `internal/dashboard/executor.go`'s `recordBlockFitnessMetric` on its own byte-for-byte-identical inline copy for that pass. The formula still had two independent implementations in the codebase — a future change (a different fallback split, an additional outcome keyword) would still need a coordinated edit against the dashboard's copy in addition to `reliability.ScoreOutcome`, with no test tying the two together to catch drift.

**Decision:** `recordBlockFitnessMetric`'s inline `score := res.Quality * 100; if score <= 0 { ... }; if score > 100 { ... } else if score < 0 { ... }` block is replaced with a single call, `score := reliability.ScoreOutcome(res.Outcome, res.Quality, success)`, mirroring the delegation pattern ADR-180 already established for `blocks.ScoreFromBlackboard`/`engine.fitnessScoreFromBB`. `internal/dashboard` importing `internal/reliability` introduces no new import-cycle risk — `internal/reliability` already had no path back to `internal/blocks`, `internal/engine`, or `internal/dashboard`, which is what let ADR-180 pick it as the formula's home in the first place.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-22) — closes the Q5 Consistency & Reuse block-fitness consolidation program ADR-180 opened (milestones 1–4/4 all landed). Pinned by `TestRecordBlockFitnessMetric_DelegatesToReliabilityScoreOutcome` (`internal/dashboard/executor_test.go`), a source-inspection test in the same style as ADR-180's two delegation-guard tests — it fails if the duplicated inline formula (`score := res.Quality * 100`) ever reappears in `executor.go` — plus the pre-existing `TestRecordBlockFitnessMetric_RateLimitCarryoverOutcome_UsesHealthyTier` and related outcome-classification tests (ADR-145), which stay green because `reliability.ScoreOutcome`'s `success bool` parameter is still computed the same way (`agent.IsBreakerSuccess(res.Outcome, runErr)`) before the call.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ All three of the formula's copies now share one tested implementation (`reliability.ScoreOutcome`); a future formula change lands once instead of needing three coordinated edits across `blocks`, `engine`, and `dashboard`.
- ✅ `recordBlockFitnessMetric`'s existing `RateLimitCarryoverOutcome`/`success` classification (ADR-145) is untouched — only the score-formula body changed, not the `success` input feeding it.
- Rejected: leaving the dashboard's copy in place indefinitely on the reasoning that `internal/dashboard` shouldn't depend on `internal/reliability` — rejected because block fitness is itself a reliability signal (ADR-180's own reasoning for the package choice), and the dependency direction is safe; `internal/dashboard` already imports `internal/agent` and `internal/engine`, both of which sit alongside `internal/reliability` in the same layer.

---

## ADR-182: `bt-dashboard` Refreshes Its Own KG Analytics Gauges on Every `/api/metrics` Scrape Instead of Depending on `bt-agent`'s Separate-Process `bt_kg_analytics` Tool (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-22):** ADR-030 milestone 4 turned `ComputeAnalytics()`'s three signals — `CoverageGaps`/`Bottlenecks`/`SelectionPressure` — into Prometheus gauges (`bt_kg_coverage_gaps`, `bt_kg_bottlenecks`, `bt_kg_selection_pressure_trees`) via `dashboard.RecordKGAnalytics`, but flagged a caveat: "the gauges update only when `bt_kg_analytics` is invoked... so a Grafana panel goes stale until the tool is next called." That caveat understated the actual gap. `bt_kg_analytics` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`) runs inside the `bt-agent` MCP server process and calls `dashboard.RecordKGAnalytics` against *that process's own* package-level gauge variables (`internal/dashboard/metrics_utils.go`). `bt-agent` and `bt-dashboard` (port 9800, §7) are separate binaries with separate memory — `bt-dashboard`'s own `/api/metrics` scrape, the endpoint Prometheus/Grafana (§8.11) actually polls, never reflected `bt_kg_analytics`' updates at all, staleness aside; the gauges it served were whatever `bt-dashboard`'s own process last set, which before this change was never.

**Decision:** Mirror the `DiscoverTreeFn` injection-seam pattern ADR-100 established for the same import-cycle constraint (`internal/dashboard` cannot import `internal/knowledge` without risking a cycle back through `internal/knowledge`'s own dependents). `internal/dashboard/metrics_utils.go` gains `var KGAnalyticsRefreshFn func()`, and `PrometheusHandler` calls it, if set, at the top of every scrape — before `writePrometheusMetrics`. `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go` wires it immediately after constructing the dashboard's own `kg *knowledge.KnowledgeGraph` (the same `buildDashboardKnowledgeGraph` graph ADR-159 loads persisted feedback into): `dashboard.KGAnalyticsRefreshFn = func() { a := kg.ComputeAnalytics(); dashboard.RecordKGAnalytics(len(a.CoverageGaps), len(a.Bottlenecks), len(a.SelectionPressure)) }`. This makes every `/api/metrics` scrape recompute analytics from `bt-dashboard`'s own in-process graph rather than relying on a cross-process side effect; `bt-agent`'s `bt_kg_analytics` tool path is unchanged and continues to serve its own process's callers.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-22). Pinned by `TestPrometheusHandler_InvokesKGAnalyticsRefreshFn` (`internal/dashboard/metrics_utils_test.go`), which arms a stub `KGAnalyticsRefreshFn` and asserts a scrape invokes it, and `TestMainWiresKGAnalyticsRefreshFn` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), a source-inspection test asserting `main.go` wires the hook to call `kg.ComputeAnalytics()` and `dashboard.RecordKGAnalytics(...)`.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `bt-dashboard`'s own `/api/metrics` gauges now reflect live KG analytics computed in-process on every scrape, closing the ADR-030 milestone-4 caveat's understated cross-process gap, not just its staleness framing.
- ✅ Reuses the existing `DiscoverTreeFn` seam pattern (ADR-100) rather than inventing a new injection mechanism — `internal/dashboard` still has zero direct dependency on `internal/knowledge`.
- ⚠️ `ComputeAnalytics()` now runs on every Prometheus scrape interval rather than only on an explicit `bt_kg_analytics` tool call; for a large knowledge graph this trades a cheap gauge read for a per-scrape analytics recomputation. No scrape-interval throttling was added, since `ComputeAnalytics` is a pure in-memory pass over `kg.Trees` with no I/O.
- `bt-agent`'s `bt_kg_analytics` MCP tool call is left in place rather than removed as redundant, even though `bt-agent` registers no `PrometheusHandler`/`/metrics` route of its own (only `cmd/bt-dashboard` does) — so that call's `dashboard.RecordKGAnalytics` update was, and remains, write-only: it sets gauge variables nothing scrapes. This change does not make that call any more or less useful; it only stops `bt-dashboard`'s own `/api/metrics` from depending on it.

---

## ADR-183: `reliability.AcquireFileLock` Is Exported and `research.UpdatePrograms` Wraps Every `ProgramStore` Read-Modify-Write Under It, Closing Milestones 1–3/5 of the ProgramStore Concurrent-Writer Lost-Update Program (Q3 Reliability)

**Context (2026-07-22):** `research.ProgramStore` (`internal/research/programs.go`) backs the single shared `programs.json` the goap-fusion loop's self-tracked research programs live in — milestone attempt charges/refunds, red-pass completion streaks, and program registration/completion all read-modify-write it. Every one of those call sites did a bare `OpenPrograms` → mutate → `Save` with no cross-process coordination: `ProgramStore.Save`, unlike `reliability.DeadLetterQueue.save` (ADR-024), never merges against what is currently on disk, so two concurrent read-modify-write callers race and the second `Save` silently clobbers the first's already-persisted change with a stale in-memory copy — the same lost-update shape ADR-021/022/024 closed for ExperienceBank two Q3 Reliability cycles earlier. `self_fix_seed.go`'s own doc comment already named this gap explicitly ("that broader programs.json multi-writer LOCK gap is pre-existing and engine-wide ... tracked for the combined review") without fixing it, since a real fix touches every call site rather than just self-fix's own producers. The highest-risk instances are the milestone-attempt charge/refund/block paths (a lost update there mis-abandons or wrongly un-abandons a milestone) and program registration via `persistGoapProgram`, reached from several distinct sources in the same running fleet (notebooklm/grill research, claude_review, design-followup, arc42-seeder) — any two firing concurrently could drop a whole proposed program.

**Decision:** Reuse `reliability`'s existing DLQ sidecar-flock idiom instead of giving `research` a third bespoke copy (`internal/evolution/file_lock.go` already has its own for ExperienceBank). `reliability.acquireFileLock` is renamed and exported to `AcquireFileLock` (`internal/reliability/reliability.go`), with its doc comment widened from DLQ-specific wording to any package guarding its own sidecar file. `research.UpdatePrograms(path string, fn func(*ProgramStore) error) error` (`internal/research/programs.go`) imports `internal/reliability` and holds one `AcquireFileLock` across the whole open → `fn` → save sequence, so no writer's `Save` can land between another writer's open and save. Three call sites in `internal/engine/actions_goap_fusion_refund.go` migrate onto it: `refundGoapMilestoneAttemptForInfraFailure` (`RefundAttempt`), `handleGoapRedPassCycleFailure` (`RecordRedPass`/`MarkDone`), and `resetGoapMilestoneRedPassStreak` (`ResetRedPassStreak`). Two call sites in `internal/engine/goap_research_goals.go` migrate: `persistGoapProgram` (`Add`) and `completeGoapProgramMilestone` (its `MarkDone` loop). Read-only `OpenPrograms` callers that never `Save` (e.g. `programContinueNote`, the `NeedsFreshProgram` condition) are correctly left untouched — the lock only needs to guard writers.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-22). Pinned by `TestUpdatePrograms_ConcurrentWritersAllSurvive` (`internal/research/programs_test.go`, 30 concurrent `UpdatePrograms` callers against one seeded store), `TestRefundGoapMilestoneAttempt_ConcurrentWritersAllSurvive` (`internal/engine/actions_goap_fusion_refund_test.go`, 30 goroutines each refunding a distinct milestone of one shared `programs.json`), and `TestPersistGoapProgram_ConcurrentCallersAllSurvive` (`internal/engine/goap_research_goals_test.go`, 30 goroutines each registering a distinct program) — all asserting zero losses where the prior bare `OpenPrograms`+`Save` shape would have dropped some fraction under real goroutine-level races.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Closes the lost-update gap for the highest-risk `ProgramStore` writers first: milestone-attempt charge/refund/block and red-pass completion (mis-abandoning or wrongly un-abandoning a milestone) and program registration/completion (silently dropping a whole proposed program or a completed milestone) can no longer race each other.
- ✅ `research/programs.go` now imports `internal/reliability` rather than reimplementing the flock idiom a third time — one exported `AcquireFileLock` now guards both `reliability.DeadLetterQueue`'s sidecar and `ProgramStore`'s, alongside `evolution/file_lock.go`'s still-separate copy for ExperienceBank.
- ⚠️ Two verified write call sites against the same `programs.json` remain unmigrated and still race against the migrated ones and each other: `internal/engine/actions_goap_fusion.go`'s `PrioritizeGoapGoals` (the goal-budget charge via `RecordAttemptAndMaybeBlock`, still a raw `OpenPrograms` + `ps.Save()`) and `self_fix_seed.go`'s program-seeding path (`ps.Add` + `ps.Save()`, guarded only by its own self-fix-scoped in-process mutex plus a separate lock file, not yet consolidated onto this lock) — migrating both is the program's remaining milestones 4–5/5.
- ⚠️ Like ADR-024, the lock is advisory Linux flock with no timeout; a wedged holder stalls every writer that has been migrated onto it, including the newly-added call sites.

---

## ADR-184: `PrioritizeGoapGoals`'s Milestone-Attempt Charge and `seedCodeFixProgram`'s Program-Store Write Move onto `research.UpdatePrograms`, Closing Milestones 4–5/5 of the ADR-183 ProgramStore Lost-Update Program (Q3 Reliability)

**Context (2026-07-22):** ADR-183 migrated the highest-risk `research.ProgramStore` writers onto `research.UpdatePrograms`'s shared flock but explicitly flagged two write call sites left racing every migrated writer and each other: `PrioritizeGoapGoals`'s milestone-attempt charge in `internal/engine/actions_goap_fusion.go` (a bare `OpenPrograms` → `RecordAttemptAndMaybeBlock` → `ps.Save()`), and `self_fix_seed.go`'s `seedCodeFixProgram`, whose own file doc comment named the gap explicitly — its cap-check/seed/ledger-stamp/`ps.Save()` sequence was guarded only by an in-process mutex and an on-disk lock file scoped to self-fix-vs-self-fix contention, not against the rest of the engine. Auditing the remaining `research.OpenPrograms` call sites named in ADR-183's own file doc comment (`arc42_seeder.go`, `actions_superpowers.go`, `goap_seed_program.go`, `graphify_components.go`, `nlm_quota.go`) found only reads: each either inspects `ps.Active()`/`ps.Programs` to decide whether to seed or to render a prompt, or (for `goap_seed_program.go`'s `SeedNextProgram`) delegates its actual write to `persistGoapProgram`, already migrated in ADR-183's milestones 1–3. None of the five ever calls `ps.Save()` themselves, so none was ever part of the lost-update gap.

**Decision:** `PrioritizeGoapGoals`'s charge path moves its `RecordAttemptAndMaybeBlock` call and the `Save` it required into a `research.UpdatePrograms` callback, capturing the charged program ID/milestone index via closure variables (`chargedProgramID`, `chargedIdx`) since the callback can't return them directly; the durable `program_milestone_charged` stamp is written only after `UpdatePrograms` returns success. The subsequent read used to decide which milestones to queue stays a plain `research.OpenPrograms` (read-only, so it needs no lock) but now re-opens the store *after* the update commits rather than mid-critical-section. `seedCodeFixProgram` restructures its cap-check → `Add` → ledger-stamp → persist sequence into a single `research.UpdatePrograms` callback, using a new `errSelfFixAbort` sentinel to abort the callback (and thus skip its `Save`) on a cap hit, a title collision, or a ledger-write failure — preserving the original fail-safe semantics of leaving the on-disk store untouched on any of those paths, since a callback error stops `UpdatePrograms` before it calls `Save`. `graphify_components.go` and `nlm_quota.go` gain a comment on their read-only `OpenPrograms` calls (`deriveGraphifyReuseTopic`, `deriveNotebookLMResearchQuery`) recording why they were audited and left as-is, so a future reader doesn't have to re-derive that they're safe. `self_fix_seed.go`'s file doc comment and `selfFixStoreMu`'s comment are rewritten to drop the now-stale claim that the self-fix lock's scope is limited to self-fix-vs-self-fix — the program-store write itself is now covered by the same shared flock as every other writer, so self-fix's own lock is retired to its true remaining job (serializing the ledger read-modify-write across goroutines).

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-22). Pinned by `TestPrioritizeGoapGoals_ConcurrentWithPersistGoapProgramAllSurvive` (`internal/engine/actions_goap_fusion_test.go`) and `TestSeedCodeFixProgram_ConcurrentWithPersistGoapProgramAllSurvive` (`internal/engine/graphify_components_test.go`), each running the migrated writer concurrently against `persistGoapProgram` on one shared `programs.json` and asserting zero lost updates across all goroutines.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ Closes the ProgramStore Concurrent-Writer Lost-Update Program (Q3 Reliability) end to end: every `programs.json` write call site in the engine — milestone charge/refund/block, red-pass completion, program registration/completion, and self-fix seeding — now holds `reliability.AcquireFileLock` across its full read-modify-write, and the five remaining `OpenPrograms` call sites are confirmed read-only.
- ✅ `self_fix_seed.go`'s on-disk `store.lock` and `selfFixStoreMu` keep exactly one job each (ledger serialization across self-fix producers), with the now-redundant claim that they also guard the program-store write against the rest of the engine removed from both comments.
- ⚠️ Like ADR-183 and ADR-024, `AcquireFileLock` remains advisory Linux flock with no timeout; a wedged holder still stalls every migrated writer, now including `PrioritizeGoapGoals` and `seedCodeFixProgram`.

---

## ADR-185: `ReportSuperpowersImplementation`/`VerifyGoapFusionEvidence` Recognize the `committed_pr_opened` ApplyStatus, Closing a Fleet-PR Evidence-Gate False Negative (Q3 Reliability)

**Context (2026-07-23):** `pushLandingMasterToOrigin` (`internal/engine/actions_pr_shepherd.go`) sets `run.ApplyStatus = "committed_pr_opened"` when a bare-repo landing's direct `git push origin master` is rejected for a protected branch and it ships the commit to a fleet PR branch instead — a genuine success the PR shepherd drives to merge on later cycles. But `ReportSuperpowersImplementation` (`internal/engine/actions_goap_fusion_prod_additions.go`, ~line 348) only emitted the `## Superpowers Implementation Complete` heading for a fixed status list (`committed`, `applied`, `applied_no_commit`, `main_repo`, `dry_run`) that omitted `committed_pr_opened`, so the report fell back to `## Superpowers Implementation Pending Patch`. `VerifyGoapFusionEvidence`'s Complete-branch check (~line 218) compounded this with its own false negative: it tested for the exact substring `` Apply status: `committed` `` — which never matches `` Apply status: `committed_pr_opened` `` (no backtick immediately follows `committed`) — so even a corrected heading would still fail evidence verification. Together the two gaps meant a real fleet-PR landing success was reported and verified as an unrecognized/pending run.

**Decision:** `ReportSuperpowersImplementation`'s heading condition adds `|| status == "committed_pr_opened"` alongside the existing five recognized statuses. `VerifyGoapFusionEvidence`'s Complete-branch check replaces its single `` Apply status: `committed` `` substring test with `applyStatusEvidence := strings.Contains(out, "Apply status: `committed`") || strings.Contains(out, "Apply status: `committed_pr_opened`")`, used in place of the old single check alongside the existing `Run:`/`Artifacts:`/`Commit:` evidence requirements.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-23).

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A fleet-PR landing success (`ApplyStatus: "committed_pr_opened"`) now emits the `Complete` heading and passes `VerifyGoapFusionEvidence` with `bb.QualityAuthoritative` set, instead of being misreported as pending. Pinned by `TestVerifyGoapFusionEvidenceAcceptsCommittedPROpened` (`internal/engine/actions_goap_fusion_prod_additions_test.go`), which drives a `SuperpowersRun` with `ApplyStatus: "committed_pr_opened"` through both actions and asserts a `1` return and `QualityAuthoritative` from `VerifyGoapFusionEvidence`.
- ⚠️ Both fixes are a status-list/substring-match extension, not a structural change to the evidence gate (§8) — any future `ApplyStatus` value added to `pushLandingMasterToOrigin` or its siblings must be added to both call sites by hand, as this one was; nothing enforces the two lists stay in sync.

---

## ADR-186: `BT_SELECTOR_ORDERING_STRATEGY` Wires `OrderByIG`/`OrderByGini`/`OrderByHybrid`/`OrderByKiller` into Both Production Selector-Ordering Call Sites Instead of Deleting Them, Closing Milestone 4/5 of the Selector-Reordering Consolidation Program (Q2 Evolvability)

**Context (2026-07-23):** Program "Consolidate the two competing Selector-reordering subsystems in `internal/evolution` (`SelectorOptimizer` vs. `DTAnalyzer`/`BTOptimizer`) into one canonical implementation." An earlier milestone of this same program added `evolution.OrderByIG`/`OrderByGini`/`OrderByHybrid`/`OrderByKiller` (`internal/evolution/selector_optimizer.go`) as ranking strategies alongside the original `OrderBySuccessRate`, each fully implemented and unit-tested — but both real production call sites that construct a `SelectorOptimizer` hardcoded `evolution.OrderBySuccessRate` directly: `internal/domains/tree_resolver.go`'s `applyLearnedSelectorOrdering` (the resolve-time reorder pass ADR-079 left as the tree-resolver's own separate seam) and `internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`'s `Gardener.applyLearnedSelectorOrdering` (the persisted-tree pass ADR-079 wired into the daemon). The four alternate strategies were consequently dead code in production despite compiling and passing their own tests — the same "implemented but operator-unreachable" gap ADR-029/ADR-079 previously closed for the `SelectorOrdering` on/off toggle itself, now recurring one level down at the ranking-algorithm choice. A separate milestone of the same program (deleting `DTAnalyzer`/`BTOptimizer` as "now-fully-redundant") was evaluated and rejected as factually false — those types remain live production code with no `SelectorOptimizer` equivalent for their pruning/merging surface — so wiring rather than deleting was already the precedent for this family of gap.

**Decision:** Wire the strategies in rather than delete them, since all four operate over the same `SelectorStats` telemetry store `OrderBySuccessRate` already reads (`LoadSelectorStats`) — no new data collection is needed to try one, only a different enum value passed to `NewSelectorOptimizer`. `internal/evolution/selector_optimizer.go` gains `ParseSelectorOrderingStrategy(s string) SelectorOrderingStrategy`, the one canonical validator shared by every wiring site: it maps `s` onto a recognized strategy constant or falls back to `OrderBySuccessRate` for an empty or unrecognized value, so a typo'd or unset environment variable can never silently change existing behavior. Both production call sites now read the same `BT_SELECTOR_ORDERING_STRATEGY` environment variable through it: `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go`'s `wireSelectorOrdering` sets the new `gardener.EvolveV2Config.SelectorOrderingStrategy` field (defaulted to `OrderBySuccessRate` in `DefaultEvolveV2Config` and defensively re-defaulted in `Gardener.applyLearnedSelectorOrdering` for any caller that builds a zero-value config directly), and `internal/agentexec/wiring.go`'s `wireSelectorReorder` sets the new `domains.SelectorOrderingStrategy` package variable that `tree_resolver.go`'s `applyLearnedSelectorOrdering` now reads instead of the previous inline `OrderBySuccessRate` literal.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-23) — milestone 4/5 of the Selector-reordering consolidation program. Pinned by `TestWireSelectorOrdering_SelectsStrategyFromEnv` (`cmd/bt-gardener/config_test.go`) and `TestWireSelectorReorder_SelectsStrategyFromEnv` (`internal/agentexec/wiring_selector_test.go`), each asserting an unset env var keeps `OrderBySuccessRate` and a recognized value (`hybrid`, `information_gain`) reaches the respective config/package variable.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `OrderByIG`/`OrderByGini`/`OrderByHybrid`/`OrderByKiller` are no longer pure dead code — an operator can opt a deployment into any of the four via `BT_SELECTOR_ORDERING_STRATEGY` at both the daemon's persisted-tree pass and the tree-resolver's resolve-time pass, without a code change or new telemetry.
- ✅ An unset or unrecognized `BT_SELECTOR_ORDERING_STRATEGY` is guaranteed, by the shared `ParseSelectorOrderingStrategy` validator and its dedicated tests, to preserve `OrderBySuccessRate` — the pass already ships production telemetry for existing opt-in deployments (ADR-079), so this activation could not be allowed to silently change their ranking as a side effect.
- Rejected alternative: delete the four unused strategies as inert options (the fallback this milestone's own goal text offered) — rejected because they read the same already-collected `SelectorStats` store as `OrderBySuccessRate`, making the opt-in wiring cheap, and the sibling "delete the redundant subsystem" milestone of this same program was independently found to rest on a false premise when tried against `DTAnalyzer`/`BTOptimizer`.
- ⚠️ A third `SelectorOptimizer` construction site, `cmd/bt-agent/tools.go`'s `bt_evolve_selectors` MCP tool (~line 2018), still hardcodes `evolution.NewSelectorOptimizer(evolution.OrderBySuccessRate)` directly and does not read `BT_SELECTOR_ORDERING_STRATEGY` — an operator-invoked one-shot reorder through that tool cannot yet select an alternate strategy, unlike the two continuously-running production passes this change wires.

## ADR-187: `NewRunDeps`'s Config-Load-Failure Fallback Adopts `config.DefaultConfig()` Instead of a Zero-Value `Config`, Closing Milestone 1/3 of the agentexec Characterization Program (Q1 Correctness)

**Context (2026-07-23):** Program "Deterministic coverage backlog: characterization tests for `internal/agentexec/deps.go` and 2 more (Q1 Correctness)," milestone 1 of 3. `deps.go` had no direct test file of its own; the milestone's mandate was to pin `NewRunDeps`'s currently-observed behavior with a new `deps_test.go`, table-driven where natural, and to touch production code only if a test exposed a real bug. Writing the config-load-failure case exposed one: on a `config.Load()` error (e.g. an unreadable `BT_CONFIG_FILE`), `NewRunDeps` fell back to `&config.Config{}` — an all-zero-value struct with an empty `LLMProvider`, no host/model, and a zero timeout — rather than the platform's actual documented defaults (`config.DefaultConfig()`: `ollama` provider, `http://localhost:11434`, `qwen3.6:35b-a3b`, a 300s LLM timeout, etc.). A broken or transiently-unreadable config file silently downgraded a fresh `RunDeps` build to this crippled provider config instead of the same sane defaults every other config-load path in the platform already falls back to.

**Decision:** Fix minimally, in place, with no other behavior change: `NewRunDeps`'s `config.Load()` error branch (`internal/agentexec/deps.go:16-19`) now sets `cfg = config.DefaultConfig()` instead of `cfg = &config.Config{}`.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-23) — milestone 1 of 3; milestones 2–3 (the program's other 2 files) covered separately.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A missing or unreadable config file no longer produces an all-zero-value `Config` — `llm.NewProvider(cfg)` now receives the platform's real default provider/host/model/timeout, the same defaults every other successful `config.Load()` path effectively starts from, instead of a provider config that was empty and effectively non-functional.
- ⚠️ `TestNewRunDeps_ConfigLoadFailure_FallsBackToZeroConfig` (`internal/agentexec/deps_test.go`) keeps its pre-fix name and an inline comment still describing the old `&config.Config{}` fallback, even though it now asserts and pins the `DefaultConfig()` behavior — a future reader grepping the test name alone would infer the wrong fallback value.
- Pinned by `TestNewRunDeps_ConfigLoadFailure_FallsBackToZeroConfig`, plus `TestNewRunDeps_PopulatesAllFields`, `TestNewRunDeps_SharedReflectionsRoot`, `TestNewRunDeps_ResolveTree_MatchesDomainsResolver`, and `TestNewRunDeps_ResolveTreeForUser_MatchesDomainsResolver` (all `internal/agentexec/deps_test.go`), which otherwise characterize `NewRunDeps`'s pre-existing behavior with no further production changes.

---

## ADR-188: `Judge` and `Synthesize` Derive Their Timeout from the Original Caller `ctx`, Making `cfg.Timeout` a Per-Stage Budget Instead of an End-to-End One, Amending ADR-151 (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-23):** ADR-151 made `Run` (`internal/fusion/run.go`) derive a single `context.WithTimeout(ctx, cfg.Timeout)` immediately on entry and thread that same, steadily-shrinking context through all three stages — `RunPanel`, `Judge`, and `Synthesize` — in sequence. ADR-152 then gave `Judge` and `Synthesize` their own retry-with-full-jitter policies (up to 3 retries, 200ms–2s backoff) so a lone transient upstream failure after a successful panel wouldn't discard the whole fusion result. The two decisions combined had an unflagged interaction: `RunPanel` routinely consumes a large, variable fraction of the shared `cfg.Timeout` budget (it fans out to every configured analysis model and waits on the slowest), so by the time `Judge` inherited the same context, only a small, unpredictable remainder was left for its own LLM call — and ADR-152's retry policy sat inside that remainder too. A slow-but-successful panel stage could leave `Judge`/`Synthesize` with too little of their own budget to complete even a single attempt, let alone absorb a retry, silently undermining the reliability ADR-152 had just added.

**Decision:** `Run` now derives three independent `context.WithTimeout(ctx, cfg.Timeout)` contexts — one per stage — each from the original, undiminished caller `ctx` rather than from the previous stage's already-shrunk one: `panelCtx` for `RunPanel`, `judgeCtx` for `Judge`, and `synthCtx` for `Synthesize`, each with its own `defer cancel()`. `cfg.Timeout` changes meaning from a single end-to-end deadline for the whole `Run` call to a per-stage budget: every stage gets a full, fresh `cfg.Timeout` window regardless of how long the preceding stage took. `Config.Timeout`'s doc comment (`internal/fusion/config.go`) is updated to state this explicitly, since the field's JSON name and default (300s) are unchanged and its new per-stage semantics are otherwise not visible from the type.

**Rejected alternative:** adding separate `JudgeTimeout`/`SynthesizeTimeout` config fields, so an operator could size each stage's budget independently, was rejected as unwarranted complexity for this pass — nothing in the current failure data distinguishes panel-stage timeout needs from judge/synthesize-stage ones, and reusing the single `cfg.Timeout` field for all three stages needed no `Config`/`Normalize`/`Validate` changes at all, only a change to how `Run` derives contexts from it. Keeping a single shared, sequentially-shrinking context (ADR-151's original shape) was also rejected: it bounds total `Run` latency more tightly (≤`cfg.Timeout` versus the new worst case of ~3×`cfg.Timeout`), but that tighter bound came at the cost of `Judge`/`Synthesize` being starved by an unrelated, already-completed panel stage — the failure mode this change exists to close, and the one ADR-152's retry policy assumed away.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-23) — pinned by `TestRun_JudgeAndSynthesizeGetOwnTimeoutBudget` (`internal/fusion/fusion_test.go`), which sets `cfg.Timeout` to 500ms, stalls the panel stage for 400ms (consuming most of that budget), and asserts both `Judge` and `Synthesize` observe a remaining context deadline close to the full 500ms rather than the ~100ms left over from the panel stage. `TestRun_RespectsTimeout` and the ADR-151/ADR-152 test suite continue to pass unmodified.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ A slow-but-successful `RunPanel` stage can no longer starve `Judge`/`Synthesize`'s own timeout or the retry policy ADR-152 wraps around them — each stage always gets a full `cfg.Timeout` window from the original caller `ctx`, independent of how much of that window the preceding stage used.
- ⚠️ A `Run` call's worst-case total latency is no longer bounded by `cfg.Timeout` alone — a caller relying on ADR-151's original "`Run` returns within `cfg.Timeout`" guarantee (e.g. an upstream HTTP handler timeout sized off `cfg.Timeout`) must now budget for up to ~3×`cfg.Timeout` (panel + judge + synthesize, each up to a full budget) plus ADR-152's retry backoff within the judge/synthesize stages.
- ⚠️ The original caller `ctx`'s own deadline or cancellation (if any) still applies to and can cut short every stage — only the *panel-consumed* portion of the budget is no longer inherited downstream; an already-expired or explicitly cancelled caller `ctx` still fails fast at whichever stage is in flight, same as before this change.

---

## ADR-189: `reliability.ScoreOutcome`'s Outcome-String Fallback Is Removed — `success` Is the Sole Signal, Correcting ADR-180/ADR-181's False "Byte-Identical" Claim (Self-Fix, Fleet Review 2026-07-22)

**Context (2026-07-23):** ADR-180 described the block-fitness formula as "hand-copied identically at three sites" and ADR-181 migrated `internal/dashboard/executor.go`'s `recordBlockFitnessMetric` onto `reliability.ScoreOutcome` on that premise, relying on `TestRecordBlockFitnessMetric_RateLimitCarryoverOutcome_UsesHealthyTier` and friends staying green as proof the change was behavior-preserving. The premise was false: the dashboard's *pre-ADR-181* inline copy (`git show 4c7006a^:internal/dashboard/executor.go`) only ever checked `success` for its 75/25 fallback — it had no `strings.EqualFold(outcome, "success"/"completed")` disjunct at all, unlike `blocks.ScoreFromBlackboard`/`engine.fitnessScoreFromBB`'s copies (and the `reliability.ScoreOutcome` ADR-180 extracted from them). ADR-181's delegation therefore didn't preserve the dashboard's existing behavior — it silently *added* the outcome-string fallback to a call site that previously never had one. Concretely (fleet review 2026-07-22): `recordBlockFitnessMetric` computes `success` via `agent.IsBreakerSuccess(res.Outcome, runErr)`, which is `false` whenever `runErr != nil` regardless of `res.Outcome`'s text. Once delegated to the buggy `reliability.ScoreOutcome`, a zero-`Quality` run with a non-nil `runErr` but a stale `res.Outcome` of `"success"`/`"completed"` scored 75 (healthy) instead of the pre-ADR-181 25 (failure) — a real regression the "byte-identical"/behavior-preserving framing in ADR-180/181 missed, because none of their pinning tests exercised `runErr != nil` together with a still-"success"-labeled outcome.

**Decision:** `ScoreOutcome`'s fallback now trusts `success` exclusively; the `strings.EqualFold(outcome, "success")`/`"completed"` disjuncts are deleted (`internal/reliability/reliability.go`). `outcome` remains a parameter (unused in the fallback, kept for call-site source compatibility across `internal/blocks`, `internal/engine`, and `internal/dashboard`, and because it's still the right extension point if a future outcome-only signal is genuinely needed). This makes `success` the one authoritative healthiness signal at every call site — each of the three already computes it correctly (`agent.IsBreakerSuccess`, or an equivalent explicit boolean), so the outcome string was always redundant with, and in the dashboard's case actively contradicted, the caller's own classification.

**Rejected alternative:** reverting `recordBlockFitnessMetric` to a private inline copy of the pre-ADR-181 formula — undoing ADR-181's consolidation instead of fixing the shared function — was rejected: it would restore the dashboard's correct behavior but reintroduce the exact "hand-copied formula across three sites" duplication ADR-180 spent three milestones eliminating, and it would leave the same fallback bug live in `reliability.ScoreOutcome` for any future caller. A second, stricter variant (e.g. a `ScoreOutcomeStrict`) callable only from the dashboard was also rejected as needless API surface: `internal/engine/ops_actions.go`'s `success` is itself derived from `bb.Outcome == "success" || bb.Outcome == "completed"`, so it can never disagree with the outcome string, and `internal/blocks.ScoreFromBlackboard` has no production caller yet — neither of the other two call sites has any behavior that depends on keeping the string fallback, so one stricter formula serves all three without a second entry point.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-23) — closes the milestone the fleet review opened. Pinned by `TestScoreOutcome_ZeroQualitySuccessFalseOutcomeSuccessCaseInsensitiveFallsBackTo25`, `TestScoreOutcome_ZeroQualitySuccessFalseOutcomeCompletedCaseInsensitiveFallsBackTo25`, and `TestScoreOutcome_ExplicitSuccessFalseNotOverriddenByOutcomeString` (`internal/reliability/reliability_test.go`), plus `TestRecordBlockFitnessMetric_RunErrorOverridesOutcomeSuccessString` (`internal/dashboard/executor_test.go`), which pins the concrete dashboard regression end-to-end. All pre-existing `ScoreOutcome`/delegation tests (ADR-180/181, ADR-145's `RateLimitCarryoverOutcome` case) continue to pass unmodified, since every existing test's `success` and outcome-string agree with each other.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `internal/dashboard/executor.go`'s `recordBlockFitnessMetric` needed no change — the fix lives entirely in `reliability.ScoreOutcome`, which now matches the dashboard's original (correct) pre-ADR-181 semantics while staying the one shared implementation for all three call sites.
- ✅ Corrects the historical record: ADR-180's "hand-copied identically at three sites" and ADR-181's implicit behavior-preservation claim are both false as written and should be read with this caveat rather than edited in place, per this file's practice of amending rather than rewriting prior entries (see ADR-188 amending ADR-151).
- ⚠️ Any future caller that genuinely needs outcome-string-only classification without a reliable `success` bool has no supported path through `ScoreOutcome` anymore — it must classify `success` correctly before calling in (e.g. via `agent.IsHealthyOutcome`), not lean on `ScoreOutcome` to do it from the string.

---

## ADR-190: `domains.ExpectedDomainIDs` Becomes the One Canonical `ExpectedDomains` Conversion, and `cmd/bt-dashboard` Wires It So `bt_kg_coverage_gaps` Can Actually Go Non-Zero, Closing a Self-Fix Gap in ADR-182 (Self-Fix, Fleet Review 2026-07-22)

**Context (2026-07-23):** ADR-182 wired `dashboard.KGAnalyticsRefreshFn` so `bt-dashboard` recomputes and republishes its own KG analytics gauges — `bt_kg_coverage_gaps`, `bt_kg_bottlenecks`, `bt_kg_selection_pressure_trees` — on every `/api/metrics` scrape, the endpoint Prometheus/Grafana (§8.11) actually polls, instead of depending on `bt-agent`'s separate-process `bt_kg_analytics` tool. That closed the cross-process staleness gap but left a narrower one unflagged: `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`'s `buildDashboardKnowledgeGraph` never set `kg.ExpectedDomains`, only `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` did, inline, by iterating its `domainRegistry`. Left unset, `knowledge.CoverageGaps` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) falls back to the package's `defaultExpectedDomains` — an 8-entry hardcoded list every one of whose entries is always present in the static catalog — so `bt_kg_coverage_gaps`, ADR-182's own headline metric, was structurally guaranteed to compute zero gaps in the one process that serves the scrape ADR-182 made live.

**Decision:** `internal/domains/trees.go` gains `ExpectedDomainIDs(registry map[string]*evolution.SerializableNode) []string`, converting a domain-tree registry into the `"domain:<name>"` ID form `CoverageGaps` expects — the one canonical implementation of a conversion `cmd/bt-agent/main.go` previously had as an inline loop. `cmd/bt-agent/main.go`'s `expectedDomains` construction is replaced with `domains.ExpectedDomainIDs(domainRegistry)`, and `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`'s `buildDashboardKnowledgeGraph` now sets `kg.ExpectedDomains = domains.ExpectedDomainIDs(domains.AllDomainTrees())` immediately after loading persisted feedback — the same graph ADR-182's `KGAnalyticsRefreshFn` closure already reads `ComputeAnalytics()` from. Both binaries now derive their expected-domain set from the same live registry through the same shared function instead of one silently defaulting to the static fallback.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-23) — self-fix from fleet review 2026-07-22. Pinned by `TestBuildDashboardKnowledgeGraph_SetsExpectedDomainsAndSurfacesGaps` (`cmd/bt-dashboard/main_test.go`), which asserts `kg.ExpectedDomains` matches `domains.AllDomainTrees()` and then, end-to-end through the real `dashboard.PrometheusHandler()`'s scraped `/metrics` body, that deleting one expected domain from the graph flips `bt_kg_coverage_gaps` from `0` to `1` in the actual exposition text — not just in an internal `CoverageGaps` slice.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `bt_kg_coverage_gaps`, ADR-182's headline metric, can now genuinely go non-zero in the `bt-dashboard` process Prometheus scrapes, closing the gap that made ADR-182's live-refresh wiring cosmetic for this particular gauge.
- ✅ `cmd/bt-agent` and `cmd/bt-dashboard` now share one canonical `ExpectedDomains` conversion (`domains.ExpectedDomainIDs`) instead of `cmd/bt-dashboard` having none at all — a future change to the `"domain:<name>"` ID convention lands once.
- ⚠️ `defaultExpectedDomains` (`internal/knowledge/graph.go`) remains a static 8-entry fallback for any caller that never injects a live registry (e.g. a graph built directly in a test, or a future binary) — this fix closes the gap only for the two binaries that now call `ExpectedDomainIDs` explicitly.
- Rejected: duplicating the `"domain:"+name` conversion loop a second time in `cmd/bt-dashboard/main.go`, mirroring `cmd/bt-agent`'s original inline copy — rejected because a second hand-copy would recreate the exact drift this fix exists to close (`cmd/bt-agent` had the loop; `cmd/bt-dashboard` simply never got one), not just move it.

---

## ADR-191: `wireDTOrdering` Sets `Config.DTStatsPath`/`EvolveV2Config.DTOrdering` on the Live Gardener Daemon, Closing ADR-171's Flagged DT-Reordering Production-Wiring Gap (Q2 Evolvability)

**Context (2026-07-23):** ADR-171 (milestones 1–3/4) gave the entropy/Gini-based `DTAnalyzer`/`BTOptimizer` engine a telemetry bridge and non-destructive `OptimizeSelectors` reordering passes at tree-resolution time and at the gardener's mutation-apply time, but explicitly flagged at acceptance that "no `cmd/` entry point or `internal/agentexec` wiring yet sets `domains.DTStatsPath`, `gardener.Config.DTStatsPath`, or `EvolveV2Config.DTOrdering` on the live daemon" — the same "operator/automation-triggered rather than unattended" gap ADR-029 had for `SelectorOptimizer` before ADR-079's `wireSelectorOrdering` closed it. ADR-172 (milestone 4/4) added the read-only `AnalyzeTreeDiagnostics` reporting half of the program but explicitly did not close this residual: `applyDTOptimizerOrdering`'s mutating pass remained inert in production — off by default (`DTOrdering: false`, `DTStatsPath: ""`) — with only its own unit tests as callers.

**Decision:** `cmd/bt-gardener/config.go` gains `wireDTOrdering(cfg gardener.Config, v2Cfg gardener.EvolveV2Config, metricsDir string) (gardener.Config, gardener.EvolveV2Config)`, mirroring `wireSelectorOrdering` immediately above it: it sets `cfg.DTStatsPath = filepath.Join(metricsDir, "dt-stats.json")` and `v2Cfg.DTOrdering = true`, taking and returning the same `v2Cfg` `wireSelectorOrdering` already threaded through so the two independently-opt-in passes compose instead of one clobbering the other. `cmd/bt-gardener/main.go` calls it immediately after `wireSelectorOrdering`, before constructing `gardener.NewGardener(cfg)` — so both the daemon's periodic `RunCycleV2` loop and the `gardener_run_cycle` MCP tool (which forwards `main`'s wired `v2Cfg` via `newGardenerRunCycleTool`) now apply DT reordering on every evolve pass.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-23) — closes ADR-171's flagged production-wiring residual, which ADR-172 explicitly left open. Pinned by `TestWireDTOrdering_EnablesDTStatsPath` (`cmd/bt-gardener/config_test.go`), which asserts the `dt-stats.json` path and `DTOrdering == true`, and — chaining through `wireSelectorOrdering` first — that `SelectorOrdering` stays `true` rather than being clobbered by a freshly-built `EvolveV2Config`.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `applyDTOptimizerOrdering` (`internal/gardener/evolve_v2.go`) is no longer inert in production — the live gardener daemon and its MCP-triggered run-cycle both apply entropy/Gini-based Selector reordering from durable `DTAnalyzer` telemetry before persisting an evolved tree, alongside the existing `SelectorOptimizer` pass `wireSelectorOrdering` already enables.
- ✅ Reuses `wireSelectorOrdering`'s exact opt-in shape (a `metricsDir`-relative stats path plus a boolean flag) rather than inventing a new wiring convention for the second optimizer.
- ⚠️ `AnalyzeTreeDiagnostics` (ADR-172) still has no production caller of its own after this change — this ADR closes only ADR-171's mutating-pass wiring gap, not ADR-172's separately-flagged "no dashboard route, MCP tool, or `cmd/` subcommand" gap for the read-only diagnostic entry point (→ ADR-192).
- ⚠️ Like `SelectorOrdering` before `BT_SELECTOR_ORDERING_STRATEGY` (ADR-079/186), `DTOrdering` has no environment-variable off switch once wired — an operator who wants to disable just the DT pass without reverting the binary must edit `wireDTOrdering` directly.

---

## ADR-192: `bt_gardener_dt_diagnostics` MCP Tool Surfaces `Gardener.AnalyzeTreeDiagnostics` for HITL Review, Closing ADR-172's Flagged "No Production Caller" Gap (Q2 Evolvability)

**Context (2026-07-23):** ADR-172 added `Gardener.AnalyzeTreeDiagnostics` as a read-only entry point that clones a tree before running `BTOptimizer`'s destructive `PruneDeadPaths`/`MergeOverlappingPaths` analysis, specifically so an HITL reviewer could see a `DTImprovementReport`'s dead-path/overlap counts before deciding whether to enable `DTOrdering` (production-wired by ADR-191) or deeper pruning. It explicitly flagged at acceptance: "`AnalyzeTreeDiagnostics` has no dashboard route, MCP tool, or `cmd/` subcommand invoking it... 'surfaced for HITL review' describes the entry point's contract, not yet an operational reviewer-facing surface."

**Decision:** `cmd/bt-agent/tools.go` registers `bt_gardener_dt_diagnostics`, mirroring `bt_evolve_selectors`'s registration shape immediately above it in the file. Given a `tree` ID (and an optional `stats_path` override), the handler resolves the base tree via the existing `resolveTree` helper, constructs `gardener.NewGardener(gardener.Config{DTStatsPath: params.StatsPath})`, and calls `AnalyzeTreeDiagnostics(gardener.TreeEntry{Name: params.Tree, Tree: baseTree})`, returning the marshaled `DTImprovementReport` as the tool's JSON result. Unlike `bt_evolve_selectors` (which mutates and persists), the handler is read-only from the caller's perspective — an inline comment records that this rests on `AnalyzeTreeDiagnostics`'s own clone-before-analyze guarantee (ADR-172), not on anything this new handler itself adds.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-23) — closes ADR-172's flagged "no production caller" gap for the diagnostic-reporting half of the DTAnalyzer program. Pinned by `TestBTGardenerDTDiagnosticsRegisteredAndReturnsReport` (`cmd/bt-agent/tools_test.go`), which asserts the tool is registered, that a resolvable tree's response carries `tree_name`, `node_count`, and every other `DTImprovementReport` numeric field (present even unseeded, as zero values rather than missing keys), and that an unknown tree returns the shared `{"error":"unknown tree"}` shape every other `resolveTree`-backed tool uses.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ An HITL reviewer can now request `DeadPathsRemoved`/`OverlappingPaths`/`ReorderChanges` counts for a named tree through the same MCP surface every other operator-invoked tool uses, instead of `AnalyzeTreeDiagnostics` being reachable only from `internal/gardener`'s own tests.
- ✅ The tool takes no destructive action against the live registry tree — `resolveTree` returns the live `*evolution.SerializableNode`, but `AnalyzeTreeDiagnostics`'s own `cloneTreeForGardener` step (ADR-172) isolates it before analysis, so this new call site inherits that safety property rather than needing to reimplement it.
- ⚠️ The tool constructs a fresh `gardener.NewGardener(gardener.Config{DTStatsPath: params.StatsPath})` per call rather than reusing the daemon's own wired `Config` (ADR-191) — an operator must pass the same `dt-stats.json` path the daemon uses (or accept an unseeded analysis) to get diagnostics consistent with what the live `DTOrdering` pass would apply; the two are not automatically kept in sync.

---

## ADR-193: `reflection.Store.LoadAll` Filters to the `reflection-*.json` Prefix `Save` Actually Writes, Closing a Phantom-Record Gap That Inflated `ev_evaluate`/`la_fitness` Success Counts (Self-Fix, Fleet Review 2026-07-22)

**Context (2026-07-23):** `internal/evolution/reflection_store.go`'s `Store.LoadAll` globbed every `*.json` file in the store directory and unmarshalled each into a `Record`. In production, `Store`'s directory (`~/.go-bt-reflections`) is shared with sibling stores — the tree registry's `tree.json`/`tree-<id>.json` and the evaluator's `transposition.json` — none of which are `Record` JSON. `json.Unmarshal` doesn't error on a structurally-different-but-valid JSON object; it silently produces a zero-value `Record{}`, whose `Outcome` is the empty string. `cmd/bt-evaluator`'s `ev_evaluate` and `cmd/bt-langagent`'s `la_fitness` handlers both compute `total_tasks`/`success_rate` by iterating `LoadAll`'s result, so each phantom sibling-file record inflated `total_tasks` by one and — since an empty-string `Outcome` isn't `evolution.Failure` — was counted as a success, skewing `success_rate` upward. Commit 7d853dc's characterization tests deliberately pinned this as an observed quirk rather than treating it as correct; fleet review 2026-07-22 flagged it as a genuine bug to fix rather than leave pinned.

**Decision:** `Save` already named every record file with a `reflection-` prefix (`fmt.Sprintf("reflection-%s.json", r.TaskID)`); the fix makes that prefix an exported invariant `LoadAll` also enforces. `internal/evolution/reflection_store.go` gains a package-level `const reflectionFilePrefix = "reflection-"`, which both `Save` (building the filename) and `LoadAll` (filtering `os.ReadDir` entries via `strings.HasPrefix`) now reference, so the writer and reader can't drift independently. `LoadAll`'s directory-entry filter becomes `e.IsDir() || filepath.Ext(e.Name()) != ".json" || !strings.HasPrefix(e.Name(), reflectionFilePrefix)` — `tree.json`, `tree-<id>.json`, and `transposition.json` all fail the added prefix check and are skipped, since none of them start with `reflection-`.

**Rejected alternative:** giving each store's sibling data its own subdirectory (e.g. `reflections/`, `trees/`) was rejected as a larger, riskier change — it would require migrating on-disk layout for every existing deployment and touch every caller that constructs a `Store`/tree `Registry` with a shared directory path, for a fix that a filename-prefix filter (matching what `Save` already wrote) solves with no migration and no behavior change to any other store.

**Status:** Accepted (2026-07-23) — closes the milestone the fleet review opened; single milestone, no further parts. Pinned by new characterization cases in `internal/evolution/reflection_store_test.go` asserting a non-`reflection-`-prefixed `.json` file is excluded from `LoadAll`'s results, and by the corrected `cmd/bt-evaluator/main_test.go`/`cmd/bt-langagent/main_test.go` pins (`TestHandleEvaluate_WithTreeAndRecordsReportsFitnessFields`, `TestHandleFitness`), which now assert `tree.json` is excluded from `total_tasks`/`success_rate` instead of pinning it as a counted phantom record.

**Consequences:**
- ✅ `ev_evaluate` and `la_fitness` now report `total_tasks`/`success_rate` computed only from genuine reflection records — a store directory shared with `tree.json`/`transposition.json` no longer silently inflates the task count or the success rate.
- ✅ The fix is confined to `internal/evolution/reflection_store.go`; no caller of `LoadAll` needed to change, since the phantom records simply stop appearing rather than requiring downstream filtering.
- ⚠️ Any other future file written into a `Store`'s directory that happens to end in `.json` but isn't `reflection-`-prefixed is now silently skipped by `LoadAll` rather than erroring — consistent with the store's existing silent-skip behavior for non-`.json` files, but still a fail-open design a future caller should be aware of if it ever needs to detect an unexpected file in the directory.

---

## ADR-194: `execFusion` Threads `chainContext(bb)` Into `fusion.Run` Instead of a Bare `context.Background()`, and `fusion.RunPanel` Checks `ctx.Err()` Before Dispatch (NotebookLM Research)

**Context (2026-07-23):** `internal/engine/chains.go`'s `execFusion` (`ChainFusion`'s executor) called `fusion.Run(context.Background(), caller, fcfg, prompt, fusionToolsFromBB(bb))` — a fresh, never-cancelable context — while every other chain executor in the same file (`execLLMCall`, `execRAGQuery`, `execStructuredOutput`, `execRetrievalQA`, `execMapReduce`, `execRefine`, per ADR-136) already runs its LLM call under `chainContext(bb)` (`chains.go:261`), which returns `bb.TraceContext` when the caller set one and only falls back to `context.Background()` otherwise. `execFusion` was the one remaining chain executor substituting a hardcoded background context for the tree's actual cancellation signal — a caller tearing down a run (timeout, HITL abort, shutdown) had no way to stop an in-flight fusion panel/judge/synthesize call, unlike every sibling executor.
